25	 A markdown list of tasks to do in the near future, ordered
26	 highest-priority first. Keep entries brief — 1-3 lines.
27	 Detailed motivation, safety requirements, and ordering belong
28	 in `notes/chores/chores-NN.md` design subsections; link via `[N]` ref.
29	
30	 Entries are manually numbered (`1.` `2.` …) so the index is
31	 readable in the raw markdown source. Reorder/insert renumbers the
32	 entries below — accept the diff churn in exchange for being
33	 able to count without rendering. Reference by entry number
34	 ("let's work on #3"). Continuation lines under a two-digit
35	 entry indent 4 spaces (under `NN. `) rather than 3.
36	
37	1. **`vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo` subcommands.** Parallel
38	   shape to `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` (check vs rewrite).
39	   Renumber `## Todo` and `## Bugs` entries in `notes/todo.md`,
40	   normalize continuation-line indent to match prefix width.
41	   Rust replacement for the interim `notes/fix-todo.py`.
42	   Belongs in the broader "notes/repo hygiene" wave alongside
43	   `validate-repo` ([[19]]).
44	2. **single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.**
45	   `0.47.0` introduced the convention (single-field leaf names
46	   its field `value`, declares the flag via `#[arg(long = "…")]`,
47	   so consumers read `args.<leaf>.value` not `args.<leaf>.<leaf>`)
48	   on the new `squash` leaf. Sweep the pre-existing single-field
49	   leaves to match: `repo`, `dry_run`, `private`, `account`,
50	   `config`, `use_template` + their consumers
51	   (`init.rs`, tests).
52	
53	   Note: can a single field be defined as an type or enum instead
54	   of a struct and maybe eliminate the `args.<leaf>.<leaf>` name
55	   issue.
56	3. **Investigate `linkme` for subcommand registration.**
57	   Distributed-slice registry — each subcommand registers itself
58	   at link time; `main.rs` discovers them via the slice rather
59	   than matching a `Commands` enum. Reduces per-subcommand
60	   touchpoints from 3 (mod decl + enum variant + match arm) to 1
61	   (registration). Costs: loses compile-time exhaustiveness
62	   (missing registration = runtime gap); help-output ordering
63	   depends on link order unless sorted; macro-magic dependency.
64	   Revisit once the `0.50.0` trait sweep's per-arm cost has been
65	   felt under real "add a subcommand" load.
66	   <https://github.com/dtolnay/linkme>
67	4. **Investigate `inventory` as `linkme` alternative.** Same
68	   shape as `linkme` — runtime-iterable registry populated by
69	   `inventory::submit!` per subcommand. Trade-offs mirror
70	   linkme's. Pick one if/when the trait sweep's match becomes
71	   the felt bottleneck.
72	   <https://github.com/dtolnay/inventory>
73	5. **`por → dual` conversion.** Attach a `.claude`
74	   companion repo + `.vc-config.toml` to an existing por
75	   workspace; emit cross-links going forward. Manual

264	## Bugs
265	
266	   Known defects we're aware of but haven't scheduled a fix
267	   for. Each entry should describe what goes wrong, when,
268	   and the cost of the failure. Numbering convention same as
269	   `## Todo` (manual `1.` `2.` …).
270	
271	1. **`finalize::surface_previous_failures` is racy and
272	   bounded by "next vc-x1 run".** The current model has
273	   four gaps:
274	   - **Stale forever.** Markers sit on disk until the
275	     next `vc-x1 <anything>` runs. If the user abandons
276	     the workspace (e.g., CI / scheduled use), failures
277	     are never surfaced.
278	   - **Concurrent surface_previous_failures.** Two
279	     `vc-x1` runs racing: both `read_dir`, both print
280	     the marker, one deletes (the other's `remove_file`
281	     silently fails). The user sees the same failure
282	     printed twice.
283	   - **Mid-write torn read.** A `finalize --exec` child
284	     writing a marker while a sibling `vc-x1` is
285	     surfacing could read partial content. Atomic-rename
286	     on write would close this.
287	   - **No notify-at-failure path.** A detached
288	     `finalize --exec` failure only becomes visible when
289	     the user next runs *any* `vc-x1` command — fine for
290	     interactive use, invisible for CI / scheduled use
291	     where there may be no next run.
292	
293	   The exec-child gate in `main.rs` (since 0.52.0-3)
294	   patches one related case — the detached child eating
295	   its own prior markers before the user can see them —
296	   but doesn't address any of the above. Holistic fix
297	   needs locking + atomic writes + maybe a
298	   notify-at-failure path for hands-off use.
299

1	# Architecture
2	
3	How the `vc-x1` binary is structured: the clap-aware CLI layer
4	and the (emerging) clap-free subcommand layer. This is the
5	"what + some why" — generic and slow-moving. The *how* and the
6	per-cycle / per-subcommand status live in `notes/chores/chores-*.md`
7	and `notes/todo.md`.
8	
9	This is a living document; the app is under continuous
10	development and the repo will keep changing.
11	
12	## Overview
13	
14	`vc-x1` is a single binary that supports subcommands,
15	`vc-x1 <subcommand>`, for managing a jj-git **dual-repo workspace**
16	with an app repo plus a `.claude` bot-session repo. The commits
17	cross-reference each other via `ochid:` trailers at the bottom
18	of each commit message. See [`README.md`](README.md) for the
19	user-facing picture and [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) for the bot
20	workflow.
21	
22	## Two layers: CLI args vs subcommand Context + Params
23	
24	Goal: separate clap-aware argument parsing from subcommand
25	operation logic, so a future front-end (TUI, library embedding)
26	can call the same core without dragging clap along. The trigger
27	was `args.account.account`-style nested-leaf access leaking into
28	subcommand bodies; accessor shortcuts were rejected as hiding
29	the mismatch rather than fixing it.
30	
31	- **CLI layer (clap-aware)**: builds `XxxParams` from the
32	  user-facing `XxxArgs` `#[derive(Args)]` clap struct.
33	  Options/flags used in more than one subcommand live as
34	  flattened leaves/bundles in `src/options_flags/`.
35	- **Subcommand layer (clap-free)**: entrypoint shape
36	  `fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams) -> Result<…>` —
37	  isolates the subcommand from clap and from the user.
38	  - `Context` — platform state shared by every subcommand.
39	  - `XxxParams` — that subcommand's inputs, derived from
40	    config / command-line args.
41	- **Boundary conversion**: `XxxArgs → XxxParams` via
42	  `impl From<&XxxArgs>` or `impl TryFrom<&XxxArgs>`, depending
43	  on whether the conversion can fail.
44	
45	Two parameters per subcommand; the signature documents what the
46	subcommand depends on. `Context` is the same across all
47	subcommands; `XxxParams` carry the per-subcommand inputs.
48	
49	Rules for the subcommand layer (some still aspirational — see
50	[the Context+Params port](#args--context--params)):
51	
52	1. Plain options, flat fields, `Default` — no clap types in
53	   `XxxParams`. Domain types (`RepoSelector`, `Scope`) are
54	   fine; leaf wrappers (`RepoOption`) are not.
55	2. Typed errors (`enum XError`, not `Box<dyn Error>`) — a TUI
56	   matches variants to pick dialogs; CLI formats them.
57	3. Returned outcomes, not `println!` — each subcommand returns
58	   a structured result; CLI formats it, a library writes
59	   nothing to stdout itself.
60	4. Progress via an optional `&mut dyn ProgressSink` on
61	   long-running subcommands — CLI installs a stderr sink, tests
62	   a recording sink.
63	5. No globals, no implicit cwd/env reads in subcommands — CLI
64	   resolves cwd/env once at startup and builds `Context`.
65	
66	None of this is sacred — the whole structure is an experiment.
67	The current scope is deliberately narrow: a small `Context`
68	(two params, not one god blob); no trait-based DI yet; one
69	crate, not a workspace split; `src/options_flags/` leaves left
70	in place — only their consumers change shape. Widen any of it
71	when the evidence (a second front-end, a second consumer crate,
72	a real platform-surface divergence) calls for it — not before.
73	
74	### Naming
75	
76	Earlier drafts of this design used `Workspace` / `XOptions`;
77	the implementation (0.44.0) chose `Context` / `XxxParams`:
78	
79	- `Workspace` → `Context` — Cargo owns "workspace" formally,
80	  and this codebase already uses "workspace" for the dual-repo
81	  project root (`find_workspace_root`).
82	- `XOptions` → `XxxParams` — avoids visual collision with
83	  `Option<T>` (a params struct mixes required and
84	  `Option<T>`-typed fields) and with `src/options_flags/`.
85	
86	## Module map
87	
88	The *kinds* of module and what they're for — not an exhaustive
89	catalog (read the `//!` docstrings; `notes/chores/chores-*.md` for
90	per-subcommand refactor status):
91	
92	**CLI layer:**
93	
94	- `src/main.rs` — the clap edge: `Cli` (`Parser`), `Commands`
95	  (`Subcommand`) + the dispatch arms, `cli_with_banner()` (the
96	  per-subcommand `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner), `CompleteEnv` wiring,
97	  and `bm_track` (the permanent `main`-bookmark tracking
98	  diagnostic on every command's entry/exit).
99	- `src/options_flags/` — reusable `#[derive(Args)]` *leaves*
100	  (one flag, or a small fixed pair) and *bundles*
101	  (`#[command(flatten)]` of N leaves) shared across
102	  subcommands, so a flag is defined (parser, help, completer)
103	  exactly once. See
104	  [`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md).
105	- `src/common.rs` — helpers the read-only commit-query
106	  subcommands (`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`) share:
107	  `for_each_repo`, `collect_ids`, `resolve_revset`, the
108	  `DotSpec` / `..`-notation parsing, `Header` /
109	  `resolve_header`, the `format_*` printers, and the
110	  `-R`/`-s` resolution stack — `find_workspace_root` /
111	  `default_scope` / `scope_to_repos` / `resolve_repos`
112	  (the top-level entry combining the `-R PATH` override and
113	  the `-s code|bot|code,bot` role selection).
114	
115	**Subcommand-layer scaffolding:**
116	
117	- `src/context.rs` — `Context`, the shared platform handle
118	  (today: the loaded `UserConfig` + the `--log` path; built
119	  once at startup via `Context::load(log)`).
120	- `src/config.rs` — `UserConfig` (`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`).
121	- `src/options_flags/scope.rs` — `Scope` (a newtype over
122	  `Vec<Side>` — the `code`/`bot` role set) and `parse_scope`.
123	
124	**Subcommand modules** — `src/<x>.rs` holds the subcommand's
125	`XxxArgs` (`#[derive(Args)]`, composing `options_flags/` leaves
126	where one exists) and the entrypoint `pub fn x(...)`. Modules:
127	`chid`, `desc` (+ `desc_helpers`), `list`, `show`,
128	`validate_desc`, `fix_desc`, `clone`, `init` (+ `init/params`),
129	`symlink`, `sync`, `finalize`, `push` (a resumable state
130	machine). Which ones have ported to `(ctx, params)` and which
131	compose `options_flags/` leaves: `notes/chores/chores-*.md` +
132	`notes/todo.md`.
133	
134	**Support:**
135	
136	- `src/repo_utils.rs`, `src/url.rs`, `src/toml_simple.rs`,
137	  `src/logging.rs` — jj/repo helpers, URL parsing, a minimal
138	  TOML reader, the CLI logger.
139	- `src/test_helpers.rs`, `src/test_tmp_root.rs` —
140	  `#[cfg(test)]` fixtures and tempdir resolution. Subcommand
141	  modules also carry their own `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (and
142	  `integration_tests` for `sync` / `push`).
143	
144	## Subcommand model
145	
146	Adding a subcommand `x`:
147	
148	1. Create `src/x.rs` with `pub struct XxxArgs`
149	   (`#[derive(Args)]`), composing leaves from
150	   `src/options_flags/` where one exists.
151	2. Add a variant `X(x::XxxArgs)` to `Commands` in `src/main.rs`
152	   with a `///` summary (and `#[command(long_about = …)]` for
153	   the long form).
154	3. Add a dispatch arm in `main()` calling `x::x(&args)` (or,
155	   once ported, `x::x(&ctx, &params)`).
156	4. Doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive `--help`; add
157	   `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, …)]` on any field whose doc
158	   comment uses bullets (clap otherwise reflows them into
159	   prose).
160	5. Dynamic value completion (`--account=<TAB>`) is an
161	   `ArgValueCompleter` attached to the leaf in
162	   `src/options_flags/` — completion is a clap-aware concern
163	   and stays at this layer.
164	
165	## args → Context + Params
166	
167	"The Context+Params port" — each subcommand's
168	`pub fn x(args: &XxxArgs)` is structured as
169	`pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams)`, with an
170	`XxxParams` flat struct + a `From` (or `TryFrom`, if the
171	conversion is fallible) at the binary edge. `init` (0.44.0)
172	is the worked example; complete across all 12 current
173	subcommands as of 0.49.0, and the shape new subcommands
174	follow. Out of scope until a real consumer surfaces: typed
175	errors, returned-outcomes-vs-`println!`, `ProgressSink`,
176	`Context` fields beyond `UserConfig` + `--log`. Per-subcommand
177	status: `notes/chores/chores-*.md` (the "Refactor tracking" section).
178	
179	## per-subcommand flags → `src/options_flags/`
180	
181	Independently of the Context+Params port — "the options_flags
182	extraction" — per-subcommand inline `#[arg]` fields are being
183	lifted into reusable leaves/bundles under `src/options_flags/`
184	so a flag is defined (parser, help, completer) exactly once. See
185	[`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md)
186	for the leaf / bundle / Pattern-A mechanics and the
187	Flag-vs-Option classification; `notes/chores/chores-*.md` for
188	per-subcommand status.
189	
190	## See also
191	
192	- [`README.md`](README.md) — user-facing overview and
193	  per-subcommand usage.
194	- [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) — bot workflow, versioning,
195	  commit/push conventions, code conventions.
196	- [`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md)
197	  — leaf/bundle patterns for the options_flags extraction.
198	- [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md) — live task list, including
199	  both refactors.
200	- `notes/chores/chores-*.md` — per-cycle design captures and the
201	  refactor-tracking tables (currently
202	  [`chores-10.md`](notes/chores/chores-10.md); `chores-09.md` and
203	  earlier are closed).
204	
```

1	# vc-x1
2	
3	- [Overview](#vc-x1)
4	- [Architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md)
5	- [Usage](#usage)
6	  - [Revision shortcuts](#revision-shortcuts)
7	  - [Shell completion](#shell-completion)
8	  - [validate-desc](#validate-desc)
9	  - [fix-desc](#fix-desc)
10	  - [clone](#clone)
11	  - [init](#init)
12	  - [symlink](#symlink)
13	  - [finalize](#finalize)
14	  - [Testing push + finalize](#testing-push--finalize)
15	- [Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers](#cross-repo-linking-with-git-trailers)
16	- [jj Tips for Git Users](#jj-tips-for-git-users)
17	- [Thoughts for the future](#thoughts-for-the-future)
18	- [Contributing](#contributing)
19	- [License](#license)
20	
21	This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
22	We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
23	in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
24	initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
25	made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
26	and one with the conversation with the bot.
27	
28	I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
29	each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
30	of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
31	changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
32	between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
33	evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
34	most importantly **why** the code evolved.
35	
36	The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
37	(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
38	existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
39	own IDE for vc.
40	
41	See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
42	for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
43	doing so and I then created this README.md file.
44	
45	## Usage
46	
47	```
48	vc-x1 list [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # List commits in a jj repo
49	vc-x1 desc [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show full description of a commit
50	vc-x1 chid [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Print changeID(s) for a revision
51	vc-x1 show [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show commit details and diff summary
52	vc-x1 validate-desc [OPTS]                 # Validate commit descriptions
53	vc-x1 fix-desc [OPTS]                     # Fix commit descriptions (dry-run default)
54	vc-x1 clone <REPO> [NAME] [OPTS]          # Clone a dual-repo project
55	vc-x1 init <NAME> [OPTS]                  # Create a new dual-repo project
56	vc-x1 symlink [TARGET] [OPTS]             # Create Claude Code project symlink
57	vc-x1 sync [OPTS]                          # Fetch + sync both repos to their remotes
58	vc-x1 finalize --bookmark <B> [OPTS]       # Squash working copy into target
59	vc-x1 --version                            # Print version
60	vc-x1 --help                           # Print help
61	```
62	
63	**The `..` notation:** dots on the revision show which direction to
64	list commits:
65	
66	- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
67	- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
68	- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
69	
70	COMMITS is the total number of commits to show (x is always included).
71	Without dots, `x` with a count defaults to `x..` (ancestors).
72	For `..x..`, the budget is split: ancestors get the extra on odd counts.
73	
74	```
75	vc-x1 list -r @              # just @ (1 commit)
76	vc-x1 list -r @.. -n 5       # @ + 4 ancestors (5 commits, @ at top)
77	vc-x1 list -r ..@ -n 3       # 2 descendants + @ (3 commits, @ at bottom)
78	vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 3     # 1 descendant, @, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
79	vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 4     # 1 descendant, @, 2 ancestors (4 commits)
80	```
81	
82	### Shell completion
83	
84	vc-x1 provides tab completion for all subcommands and flags using claps
85	[unstable-dynamic](https://docs.rs/clap_complete/latest/clap_complete/env/struct.CompleteEnv.html)
86	only feature. It is a "simple" implementation and does not handle completing
87	revisions but still useful. To enable it, add one of the following to your shell's startup file:
88	
89	```bash
90	# bash (~/.bashrc)
91	source <(COMPLETE=bash vc-x1)
92	
93	# zsh (~/.zshrc)
94	source <(COMPLETE=zsh vc-x1)
95	
96	# fish (~/.config/fish/config.fish)
97	source (COMPLETE=fish vc-x1 | psub)
98	```
99	
100	Completions are generated dynamically by the binary, so they stay in
101	sync with the installed version automatically.
102	
103	### Positional shorthand
104	
105	REVISION and COMMITS can be given as positional arguments, omitting
106	`-r` and `-n`:
107	
108	```
109	vc-x1 list x                # just x (1 commit)
110	vc-x1 list x 5              # x.. 5 → x + 4 ancestors (5 commits)
111	vc-x1 list x.. 5            # same as above
112	vc-x1 list ..x 3            # 2 descendants + x (3 commits)
113	vc-x1 list ..x.. 3          # 1 descendant, x, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
114	vc-x1 list ..x.. 1          # just x (1 commit)
115	```
116	
117	**Defaults:** with no arguments, REVISION is `@` and COMMITS is 1
118	(just the working copy).
119	
120	Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-n`/`--commits`, `-R`/`--repo`, and
121	`-s`/`--scope` take precedence over positional arguments.
122	
123	### Multi-repo queries
124	
125	`-s`/`--scope` selects workspace sides by role keyword; `code` is the
126	app repo, `bot` is the `.claude` repo, `code,bot` is both. The
127	workspace root is found by walking up from cwd (the existing
128	`find_workspace_root` rule).
129	
130	```
131	vc-x1 chid -s code,bot          # both sides of the workspace
132	vc-x1 chid -s code              # just the app repo
133	vc-x1 chid -s bot               # just the bot repo
134	vc-x1 list @.. 3 -s code,bot    # works with chid / desc / list / show
135	```
136	
137	`-R`/`--repo` takes a single path. Used alone it points at one jj
138	repo (workspace lookup is skipped); combined with `-s` it overrides
139	the workspace root for the role lookup:
140	
141	```
142	vc-x1 chid -R .                  # single repo at .
143	vc-x1 chid -R .claude            # single repo at .claude
144	vc-x1 chid -R ../other -s code,bot   # both sides of ../other workspace
145	```
146	
147	Defaults preserve prior behavior — no flag → `[.]`, `-R foo` alone
148	→ `[foo]`. When multiple repos are resolved (any `-s` that names
149	more than one role), output is labeled with bold `=== path ===`
150	headers by default. Control the label with `-l`/`--label` and
151	`-L`/`--no-label`:
152	
153	```
154	vc-x1 chid -s code,bot            # default label: === path ===
155	vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -l "---"   # custom label:  --- path ---
156	vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L         # no label (raw output)
157	```
158	
159	Examples:
160	
161	```
162	$ vc-x1 chid -s code,bot
163	=== . ===
164	kwoyvposvsmv
165	
166	=== .claude ===
167	zzpksklxyrnw
168	
169	$ vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L
170	kwoyvposvsmv
171	zzpksklxyrnw
172	
173	$ vc-x1 list @.. 3 -s code,bot
174	=== . ===
175	kwoyvposvsmv eae175c3c1b5 (no description set)
176	pqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)
177	ssokyszzwsxw eeb15bc6d839 Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)
178	
179	=== .claude ===
180	zzpksklxyrnw 2388185e42ee (no description set)
181	tzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)
182	slmkmroqtqtp 1c33ccaa567d Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)
183	
184	$ vc-x1 desc -r @- -s code,bot
185	=== . ===
186	pqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)
187	
188	    Move completed items [1]-[13] and their references from todo.md to done.md.
189	    Replace completed jj-organization todo with new items.
190	
191	    ochid: tzupykyyvnrp
192	
193	=== .claude ===
194	tzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)
195	
196	    Session: reviewed and committed notes reorganization for 0.19.1.
197	
198	    ochid: pqxtxxpnsmot
199	
200	$ vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -l "---"
201	--- . ---
202	kwoyvposvsmv
203	
204	--- .claude ---
205	zzpksklxyrnw
206	```
207	
208	Most decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,
209	`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters
210	like `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-l '***'`). Double quotes
211	won't protect against `!` (bash history expansion).
212	
213	With a single repo resolved, no label is printed — backward
214	compatible with the no-flag default. Multi-repo (`-s code,bot`) is
215	supported for `chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains
216	single-repo.
217	
218	`-s` is keyword-only — `code`, `bot`, `code,bot`, `bot,code`.
219	Path-based single-repo operation uses `-R` (above).
220	
221	### validate-desc
222	
223	Read-only scan of commit descriptions against the other repo. The other
224	repo is read from `.vc-config.toml` (`workspace.other-repo`) by default,
225	or overridden with `--other-repo`. Reports status per commit: `ok`
226	(valid ochid), `lost` (ochid: lost), `none` (ochid: none), `err`
227	(issues found), or `miss` (no ochid trailer).
228	
229	```
230	# Validate all ancestors of @ (reads other repo from .vc-config.toml)
231	vc-x1 validate-desc @..
232	
233	# Validate with explicit other repo
234	vc-x1 validate-desc --other-repo .claude @..
235	
236	# Validate specific range
237	vc-x1 validate-desc -r @.. -n 20
238	```
239	
240	Use `--help` for the full status label legend.
241	
242	### fix-desc
243	
244	Fix commit descriptions against the other repo. Default is dry-run —
245	use `--no-dry-run` to write changes. Reads other repo from
246	`.vc-config.toml` by default.
247	
248	```
249	# Dry-run: show what would be fixed
250	vc-x1 fix-desc @..
251	
252	# Actually fix
253	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --no-dry-run
254	
255	# Add missing ochid trailers by matching title
256	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --add-missing
257	
258	# Limit fixes
259	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --no-dry-run -m 3
260	
261	# Use a fallback for IDs not found in other repo
262	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --fallback /.claude/lost
263	```
264	
265	| Flag | Description |
266	|------|-------------|
267	| `--other-repo <PATH>` | Override other repo from .vc-config.toml |
268	| `--no-dry-run` | Write fixes [default: dry-run] |
269	| `--add-missing` | Infer and add ochid for commits without one |
270	| `-m, --max-fixes <N>` | Stop fixing after N commits changed [default: all] |
271	| `--fallback <VALUE>` | Replacement for IDs not found in other repo |
272	| `--id-len <N>` | Expected changeID length [default: 12] |
273	| `--title <TEXT>` | Replace commit title at the same time |
274	
275	Use `--help` for the full status label legend.
276	
277	### clone
278	
279	Clone an existing dual-repo project. Runs `git clone --recursive` to
280	get both repos, initializes `jj` in each, and creates the Claude Code
281	symlink.
282	
283	```
284	# Clone using GitHub shorthand
285	vc-x1 clone owner/my-project
286	
287	# Clone using full URL
288	vc-x1 clone git@github.com:owner/my-project.git
289	
290	# Clone with a custom directory name
291	vc-x1 clone owner/my-project my-local-name
292	
293	# Clone into a specific parent directory
294	vc-x1 clone owner/my-project --dir ~/projects
295	
296	# Preview without executing
297	vc-x1 clone owner/my-project --dry-run
298	```
299	
300	| Flag | Description |
301	|------|-------------|
302	| `--dir <PATH>` | Parent directory [default: cwd] |
303	| `--dry-run` | Show what would be done without executing |
304	| `-v, --verbose` | Verbose output |
305	
306	Requires `jj` to be installed. The `.claude` session repo is cloned
307	automatically via `git submodule` if the source project was created
308	with `vc-x1 init`.
309	
310	### init
311	
312	Create a new dual-repo project — a code repo with a `.claude` session
313	repo as a git submodule. Both repos are initialized with `git` and `jj`,
314	configured with `.vc-config.toml`, and pushed to GitHub. The session
315	repo is added as a submodule so `git clone --recursive` clones both.
316	
317	```
318	# Create public project in current directory
319	vc-x1 init my-project
320	
321	# Specify owner and parent directory
322	vc-x1 init my-project --owner myorg --dir ~/projects
323	
324	# Create private repos
325	vc-x1 init my-project --private
326	
327	# Preview without executing
328	vc-x1 init my-project --dry-run
329	
330	# Seed both repos from template directories (sibling layout)
331	vc-x1 init my-project --use-template ../vc-template-x1
332	# Equivalent to:
333	vc-x1 init my-project --use-template ../vc-template-x1,../vc-template-x1.claude
334	```
335	
336	| Flag | Description |
337	|------|-------------|
338	| `--owner <OWNER>` | GitHub user/org [default: current `gh` user] |
339	| `--dir <PATH>` | Parent directory [default: cwd] |
340	| `--private` | Create private GitHub repos [default: public] |
341	| `--dry-run` | Show what would be done without executing |
342	| `--push-retries <N>` | Max push retries after repo creation [default: 5] |
343	| `--push-retry-delay <N>` | Seconds between push retries [default: 3] |
344	| `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>` | Seed both repos from template dirs (see below) |
345	| `-v, --verbose` | Verbose output (show retry details) |
346	
347	**`--use-template`**. Value is `CODE[,BOT]`. If `BOT` is omitted, defaults
348	to the sibling directory `<CODE>.claude` (file-name concat, not path
349	join — the two templates are not nested). Non-hidden contents are
350	copied recursively into each target; hidden entries (names starting
351	with `.`) are skipped since init creates the repo's own hidden files
352	(`.vc-config.toml`, `.gitignore`, `.git/`, `.jj/`). If either template
353	has a `README.md` at its root, its first line is rewritten to
354	`# <repo-name>` — `<name>` for the code repo and `<name>.claude` for
355	the session repo. For local verification without hitting GitHub,
356	combine `--use-template` with `--repo-local <PARENT>`.
357	
358	Requires `gh` (authenticated) and `jj` to be installed (`gh` is
359	skipped under `--repo-local`).
360	
361	### symlink
362	
363	Create or verify the Claude Code project symlink. Claude Code stores
364	session data in `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-path>/`. This command
365	creates a symlink from that location to the local `.claude` directory.
366	
367	```
368	# Create symlink for current project (default target: .claude)
369	vc-x1 symlink
370	
371	# Specify a different target
372	vc-x1 symlink /path/to/session-dir
373	
374	# Replace existing symlink without prompting
375	vc-x1 symlink -y
376	
377	# List contents after creation
378	vc-x1 symlink -l
379	```
380	
381	| Flag | Description |
382	|------|-------------|
383	| `--symlink-dir <PATH>` | Override symlink parent [default: ~/.claude/projects] |
384	| `-l, --list` | List contents of symlinked directory after creation |
385	| `-y, --yes` | Replace existing symlink without prompting |
386	
387	### sync
388	
389	Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes in a single command.
390	Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and
391	`.claude`); narrow it with `-s` / `--scope`, or point at a different
392	workspace root or single repo with `-R` / `--repo`. Check-only by
393	default — re-run with `--no-check` to apply.
394	
395	Per repo, `sync` classifies the local bookmark against its remote:
396	
397	| State | Meaning | Action on `--no-check` |
398	|------|---------|--------------------------|
399	| up-to-date | local == remote | none |
400	| behind | local is ancestor of remote | `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` |
401	| ahead | remote is ancestor of local | none (push is a separate step) |
402	| diverged | neither is ancestor | `jj rebase -b <local-head> -d <b>@<remote>` |
403	| no remote | bookmark has no `@<remote>` counterpart | none — skip |
404	
405	After the bookmark action above, `sync` also rebases `@` onto the
406	(possibly advanced) bookmark when `@` isn't already a descendant —
407	without this step, `jj git fetch`'s auto-fast-forward would leave `@`
408	dangling off the pre-fetch bookmark commit. This matters for `.claude`,
409	where `/exit`'s trailing session writes always sit on `@`.
410	
411	On any failure — conflicted rebase, subprocess error, anything — `sync`
412	restores every repo to its starting state via `jj op restore`. Either
413	every repo advances or none do. Working-copy files are preserved
414	across the revert: jj rewinds the operation log but leaves disk
415	content untouched, and any conflicted commits introduced by the failed
416	rebase are abandoned on the way back.
417	
418	```
419	vc-x1 sync                            # workspace-default scope, --check
420	vc-x1 sync --check                    # explicit form of the default
421	vc-x1 sync --no-check                 # workspace-default scope, apply
422	vc-x1 sync --scope=code               # only the app repo
423	vc-x1 sync --scope=bot                # only the bot repo
424	vc-x1 sync --scope=code,bot           # both (explicit form of the dual default)
425	vc-x1 sync -R ../other                # sync ../other as a single repo
426	vc-x1 sync -R ../other --scope=code,bot   # ../other as workspace root
427	```
428	
429	Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`
430	explicitly rather than rely on the default — defaults can shift,
431	explicit flags lock in the contract. Interactive use can take the
432	default.
433	
434	**Repo set resolution.** `-R` and `--scope` compose:
435	
436	1. Neither — workspace-default scope: `code,bot` if
437	   `[workspace] other-repo` is non-empty, else `code`. POR (no
438	   `.vc-config.toml`) → `code` resolved to cwd.
439	2. `-R PATH` alone — sync just the repo at `PATH`.
440	3. `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` alone — workspace roles, resolved
441	   via the discovered workspace root's `.vc-config.toml`
442	   (`code` → root, `bot` → `root.join(other-repo)`).
443	4. `-R PATH --scope=ROLES` — roles resolved against `PATH` as the
444	   workspace root.
445	
446	Scope is cwd-portable: from `.claude/`, `vc-x1 sync` walks up to
447	the workspace root and resolves repos by absolute path.
448	
449	| Flag | Description |
450	|------|-------------|
451	| `-R, --repo <PATH>` | Workspace root, or a single repo to sync alone. Composes with `--scope` |
452	| `--scope <SCOPE>` | `code|bot|code,bot` — workspace roles to sync. Composes with `-R` |
453	| `--check` | Verify only — fetch + classify, error if any repo needs action (default) |
454	| `--no-check` | Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase/fast-forward as needed |
455	| `-q, --quiet` | Suppress all output; exit code signals result (for scripts) |
456	| `--bookmark <NAME>` | Bookmark to sync in each repo [default: main] |
457	| `--remote <NAME>` | Remote to sync against [default: origin] |
458	
459	**Output shape.** Sync collapses output based on what it finds:
460	
461	- **All up-to-date** — one-line summary:
462	  `sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`. Nothing else.
463	  Makes "sprinkle sync everywhere" genuinely cheap. Scope is
464	  bookmark-vs-remote tracking — `@` may have uncommitted
465	  working-copy changes; sync intentionally doesn't speak to
466	  that (use `jj st` for working-copy state).
467	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) — per-repo fetch +
468	  state lines, then **fatal in `--check` mode** with
469	  `sync: N repos need action — resolve with vc-x1 sync --no-check
470	  and re-run`. Under `--no-check`, the actions run instead.
471	- **`--quiet`** — no output at any level; exit code is the only
472	  signal. Intended for scripts that just need success/failure.
473	
474	**Note on the `behind` case.** jj's `git fetch` already fast-forwards a
475	tracked local bookmark when it's a strict ancestor of the incoming
476	remote, so in the common case `sync` reports `up-to-date` rather than
477	`behind`. The `behind` branch covers untracked bookmarks and edge
478	configs where auto-advance is disabled.
479	
480	### finalize
481	
482	Atomically squash, set bookmark, and push a jj repo. The primary use
483	case is the bot finalizing its own session repo (`.claude`) at the end
484	of a session. The bot can't just `jj commit` because the act of
485	committing generates more session data — files written after the commit
486	would be lost. `finalize` solves this by:
487	
488	1. **`--detach`**: spawning a background process so the bot session can
489	   end immediately (no more writes after the bot exits)
490	2. **`--delay`**: waiting for trailing writes to settle
491	3. **`--squash`**: squashing the working copy into the session commit
492	4. **`--bookmark`** + **`--push`**: advancing the bookmark and pushing
493	
494	Every behavior is opt-in — omit any flag to skip that step.
495	
496	The bot's last action in a session:
497	
498	```
499	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --bookmark main --delay 10 --detach --push
500	```
501	
502	If there is non-written session data after a session ends (e.g.
503	finalize failed or was skipped), run it manually:
504	
505	```
506	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --bookmark main --push
507	```
508	
509	This runs in the foreground, squashes, advances the bookmark, and pushes.
510	
511	Other uses — `finalize` composes freely:
512	
513	```
514	# Just set bookmark, no squash
515	vc-x1 finalize --bookmark main
516	
517	# Squash with custom source/target
518	vc-x1 finalize --squash @,@-- --bookmark main
519	```
520	
521	See [finalize subcommand](./notes/chores/chores-01.md#finalize-subcommand-for-session-repo-coherence)
522	for design details.
523	
524	### push
525	
526	Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command with two
527	interactive approval gates. Replaces the old multi-step manual
528	choreography (`jj commit` × 2 → `jj bookmark set` × 2 →
529	`jj git push` → `vc-x1 finalize`) with a single invocation.
530	
531	```bash
532	vc-x1 push main                                     # interactive
533	vc-x1 push main --title "..." --body "..."          # skip $EDITOR
534	vc-x1 push main --yes --title "..." --body "..."    # full non-interactive
535	vc-x1 push main --dry-run                           # preview
536	vc-x1 push main --from commit-app                   # resume at specific stage
537	vc-x1 push --status                                 # show saved state
538	```
539	
540	Stage machine (runs top-to-bottom; each stage's success persists
541	to `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` so interrupts resume mid-flow):
542	
543	| Stage | What it does |
544	|-------|--------------|
545	| `preflight` | `vc-x1 sync --check`, `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy -D warnings`, `cargo test` |
546	| `review` | Print `jj diff --stat` for both repos; prompt `[y/N]` (first approval gate) |
547	| `message` | Compose title+body from `--title`/`--body`, persisted state, or `$EDITOR` template; second approval gate |
548	| `commit-app` | `jj commit` app repo with ochid trailer pointing at `.claude` |
549	| `commit-claude` | `jj commit` `.claude` with ochid trailer pointing at app (skipped if `.claude` is clean) |
550	| `bookmark-both` | `jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .` and `-R .claude` |
551	| `push-app` | `jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .` |
552	| `finalize-claude` | `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach` |
553	
554	Failures in `commit-app` / `commit-claude` / `bookmark-both` roll
555	both repos back via `jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded at
556	the start of `commit-app`. Past `push-app` the remote boundary is
557	crossed and recovery is forward-only (see "Late changes after
558	push" in CLAUDE.md).
559	
560	| Flag | Description |
561	|------|-------------|
562	| `[BOOKMARK]` | Bookmark to advance; positional form of `--bookmark` |
563	| `--bookmark <NAME>` | Same as positional (mutually exclusive) |
564	| `-y, --yes` | Auto-approve both gates (non-interactive use) |
565	| `--title <STR>` / `--body <STR>` | Skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage |
566	| `--dry-run` | Print what would run, no side effects, no state written |
567	| `--step` | Pause after every stage for an extra continue-prompt |
568	| `--from <STAGE>` | Jump to a specific stage (advanced / resume) |
569	| `--status` | Print saved state's current stage and exit |
570	| `--restart` | Clear saved state; start from stage 1 |
571	| `--recheck` | Re-run preflight on resume (default: skip if last succeeded) |
572	| `--no-finalize` | Stop before `finalize-claude` (run it manually) |
573	
574	State file path is configurable via `.vc-config.toml`'s `[push]`
575	section:
576	
577	```toml
578	[push]
579	state-dir = ".vc-x1"          # default
580	state-file = "push-state.toml"  # default
581	```
582	
583	`push` warns (non-fatal) when the configured state dir isn't
584	matched in `.gitignore`.
585	
586	See [Add push subcommand (0.37.0)](./notes/chores/chores-05.md#add-push-subcommand-0370)
587	for the full design and [per-step record](./notes/chores/chores-05.md#per-step-record)
588	for what each `0.37.0-N` dev step shipped.
589	
590	### Testing push + finalize
591	
592	Always test against a throwaway fixture, never the live workspace.
593	Scaffold one with `vc-x1 init --repo-local <PARENT>` (no GitHub, no
594	network), then run the complete push + finalize flow end-to-end.
595	The code repo uses plain `jj git push`; the session repo uses
596	`vc-x1 finalize` to squash trailing writes and push in one shot.
597	
598	`--repo-local` lays out:
599	```
600	<PARENT>/
601	  remote-code.git/     bare git remote for code repo
602	  remote-claude.git/   bare git remote for .claude session repo
603	  <NAME>/              code repo (jj colocated, main tracks origin)
604	    .vc-config.toml    path="/",       other-repo=".claude"
605	    .gitignore         /.claude /.git /.jj /target /.vc-x1
606	    .claude/           session repo (jj colocated, main tracks origin)
607	      .vc-config.toml  path="/.claude", other-repo=".."
608	      .gitignore       .git .jj
609	```
610	
611	```bash
612	parent=$(mktemp -u /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
613	vc-x1 init work --repo-local "$parent"
614	work="$parent/work"
615	session="$parent/work/.claude"
616	
617	# 1. code repo: described commit → advance main → push
618	echo hello > "$work/hello.txt"
619	jj describe @ -R "$work" -m 'feat: add hello.txt'
620	jj bookmark set main -r @ -R "$work"
621	jj git push -R "$work"
622	
623	# 2. session repo: trailing writes → finalize (squash into @-, push)
624	echo notes > "$session/notes.md"
625	vc-x1 finalize --repo "$session" --squash --push main --detach \
626	    --log "$session/finalize.log"
627	
628	# 3. inspect the detached child's log once it's done (≈10s by default)
629	sleep 12 && cat "$session/finalize.log"
630	
631	# 4. cleanup when done
632	rm -rf "$parent"
633	```
634	
635	**Why `jj git push` for code but `finalize` for `.claude`?** The
636	code repo's workflow is a plain dev commit on `@-` that we push
637	directly. The session repo mirrors the bot's runtime pattern:
638	session writes land in `@` (above the last committed dev commit),
639	and `finalize --squash @,@-` folds those trailing writes into the
640	dev commit just before pushing, so one atomic state goes upstream.
641	
642	The log file shows timestamped (nanoseconds) entries with PIDs,
643	covering the full flow: `main` entry/exit, `finalize` entry/exit,
644	`detach` spawn, and `finalize_exec` in the child process.
645	
646	Example — detached finalize against a fresh fixture. What the user
647	sees in the terminal (the parent process) is the pre-flight plan and
648	the detach confirmation; the child's work continues in the background:
649	```
650	$ vc-x1 finalize --repo "$session" --squash --push main \
651	    --detach --delay 1 --log "$session/finalize.log"
652	finalize: squash @ → @- in /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8/work/.claude
653	finalize: set bookmark 'main' mwutluxv 31a49010 → mwutluxv 31a49010 (@-)
654	finalize: push 'main' to remote
655	finalize: detached (pid 103787), log: /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8/finalize.log
656	```
657	
658	A few seconds later the log file (authoritative when the caller
659	closes the child's pipes) shows the full run, including the child's
660	own squash/push output:
661	```
662	$ cat "$session/finalize.log"
663	[INFO ] vc_x1::finalize: finalize: squash @ → @- in /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8/work/.claude
664	[INFO ] vc_x1::finalize: finalize: set bookmark 'main' mwutluxv 31a49010 → mwutluxv 31a49010 (@-)
665	[INFO ] vc_x1::finalize: finalize: push 'main' to remote
666	[INFO ] vc_x1::finalize: finalize: detached (pid 103787), log: /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8/finalize.log
667	[INFO ] vc_x1::common: Working copy  (@) now at: ovumtpup fa1cb861 (empty) (no description set)
668	Parent commit (@-)      : mwutluxv 584571ba main* | initial commit
669	[INFO ] vc_x1::common: Nothing changed.
670	[INFO ] vc_x1::common: Changes to push to origin:
671	  Move sideways bookmark main from 31a490102db7 to 584571ba54af
672	```
673	
674	Pre-flight failures (bookmark missing, non-tracking remote, squash
675	revset unresolved, push target lacks a description) exit the parent
676	synchronously with a non-zero status and a pointed error on stderr,
677	before the child is ever spawned.
678	
679	## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
680	
681	Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
682	to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
683	(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
684	and jj changeID:
685	
686	```
687	ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
688	ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
689	```
690	
691	Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
692	Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
693	within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
694	
695	For full details see:
696	- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
697	  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
698	  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
699	  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
700	
701	## jj Tips for Git Users
702	
703	If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
704	tools like `gitk --all`.
705	
706	### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
707	
708	jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
709	When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
710	jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
711	undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
712	shows the current version of each change.
713	
714	### Useful commands
715	
716	| Command | Description |
717	|---------|-------------|
718	| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
719	| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
720	| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
721	| `jj obslog -r <change-id>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
722	| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
723	
724	In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
725	the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.
726	
727	## Testing
728	
729	The repo ships two flavors of tests:
730	
731	- **In-process tests** — `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { … }` blocks inside
732	  `src/*.rs`. These call library code directly (no subprocess spawn)
733	  and run fastest. The dual / POR fixture tests under
734	  `src/init.rs::tests` build throwaway workspaces by invoking
735	  `init::init` as a function.
736	- **CLI subprocess integration tests** — files under `tests/`. These
737	  spawn the `vc-x1` binary that Cargo built; its absolute path lives
738	  in the `CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1` env var that Cargo sets at compile
739	  time, which `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")` reads and bakes into the
740	  test binary as a string literal. Argument parsing, exit codes, and
741	  stdout/stderr are all exercised end-to-end. Each subprocess gets a
742	  `HOME` override so the user's real `~/.config/vc-x1/` can't leak in
743	  or get clobbered.
744	
745	Run everything with:
746	
747	```bash
748	cargo test                 # unit + integration
749	cargo test --bins          # binary unit tests only (in-process)
750	cargo test --test cli_init # one integration test crate
751	```
752	
753	### Test tempdir location
754	
755	Both test layers create throwaway fixtures under a tempdir. The
756	parent directory resolves in priority order:
757	
758	1. `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` — explicit env override.
759	2. `std::env::temp_dir()` — standard fallback (`$TMPDIR` on Unix,
760	   else `/tmp`).
761	
762	Useful when you want tests on a tmpfs / SSD / project-local path
763	without exporting `TMPDIR` globally:
764	
765	```bash
766	VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR=/dev/shm/vc-x1 cargo test
767	```
768	
769	Fixture directories are named `vc-x1-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` (in-process)
770	and `vc-x1-cli-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` (CLI). RAII drop removes them on
771	test exit; SIGKILLs / panics in `Drop` can leak — search and clean
772	manually:
773	
774	```bash
775	find "${VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR:-${TMPDIR:-/tmp}}" -maxdepth 1 \
776	     -name 'vc-x1-*test*' -mtime +1 -exec rm -rf {} +
777	```
778	
779	A future enhancement (tracked in `notes/todo.md`) extends the
780	priority chain through `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` and project-local
781	`.vc-config.toml`.
782	
783	### Preserving fixtures for debugging
784	
785	When a test fails and you want to inspect the workspace it built,
786	set `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`. The RAII `Drop` impls (`Fixture`,
787	`FixturePor`, `CliFixture`) skip `remove_dir_all` and print the
788	preserved path on stderr:
789	
790	```bash
791	VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 cargo test -- --nocapture 2>&1 | grep TEST_KEEP
792	```
793	
794	Two shell gotchas worth remembering:
795	
796	- The preservation message goes to **stderr**, so a plain
797	  `cargo test | grep ...` won't see it — use `2>&1 |` or write
798	  the full output to a file.
799	- `--nocapture` is needed to bypass libtest's stdout/stderr
800	  capture; without it, the messages get swallowed by the test
801	  runner's pretty-printer.
802	
803	`VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` is a debugging knob — every fixture-creating
804	test in the run leaks its tempdir while it's set. Clean up with
805	the `find` recipe above, or just `rm -rf` the announced paths.
806	
807	## Thoughts for the future
808	
809	Forward-looking design discussion lives in
810	[`notes/forks-multi-user.md`](notes/forks-multi-user.md) —
811	forking the dual-repo workspace, multi-user collaboration,
812	multi-line `ochid:` trailers, bot-repo size and scaling
813	thresholds, URL-shaped per-user repos for distributed
814	projects. Treat as a design reference, not a status doc;
815	most of what's there is forward-looking, not yet
816	implemented.
817	
818	## Contributing
819	
820	The app's internal structure — module map, the CLI-args /
821	ops-`Context`+`Params` split, the subcommand model, and the
822	two in-flight migrations — is described in
823	[ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md). Start there to orient.
824	
825	Bot-following workflow, commit conventions, and code style are
826	canonical in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md). Roughly in the order a
827	contributor encounters them:
828	
829	- [Versioning](CLAUDE.md#versioning) — `-N` pre-release suffix
830	  convention (single-step vs multi-step); cross-links to
831	  Sub-step Workflow for finer depths.
832	- [Sub-step Workflow](CLAUDE.md#sub-step-workflow) — sub-step
833	  (`X.Y.Z-N.M`) / sub-sub-step (`X.Y.Z-N.M-K`) decomposition,
834	  per-sub-step cargo cycle, commit-first review model, ochid
835	  + `.claude` cadence, cycle close-out (squash vs keep
836	  separate), reviewing committed sub-steps.
837	- [Pre-commit Requirements](CLAUDE.md#pre-commit-requirements) —
838	  user approval gates, review-before-commit-block, per-file
839	  review checkpoints (with sub-step exception).
840	- [Pre-commit checklist](CLAUDE.md#pre-commit-checklist) — fmt
841	  / clippy / test / install + todo / chores / README updates.
842	- [Commit Message Style](CLAUDE.md#commit-message-style) —
843	  Conventional Commits + `(version)` suffix; body shape per
844	  app vs `.claude` repo.
845	- [ochid Trailers](CLAUDE.md#ochid-trailers) — paired
846	  cross-references between app and `.claude` commits.
847	- [Commit-Push-Finalize Flow](CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) —
848	  `vc-x1 push` two-gate ceremony (review + message), preflight,
849	  finalize-claude.
850	- [Code Conventions](CLAUDE.md#code-conventions) — doc comments
851	  on every file / fn / method, `// OK: …` on `unwrap*` calls,
852	  ask-on-ambiguity, stuck detection.
853	
854	Task tracking and release details live under [notes/](notes/):
855	near-term tasks in [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md), per-release
856	details in `notes/chores/chores-*.md`, and notes-specific formatting
857	rules in [notes/README.md](notes/README.md).

## vc-x1 — orientation

**What it is.** A single Rust binary managing a jj-git **dual-repo workspace**: an app/code repo (`/`) plus a `.claude` bot-session repo. Commits cross-reference each other via `ochid:` trailers so you can later see *how and why* the code evolved alongside the bot conversation.

**Current state.**
- Version **0.54.0**, last commit `aaaeb09 refactor: scope CLI cleanup (0.54.0)`.
- Working copy `@` (moruztwp) is empty/clean, sitting on `main`.
- **No cycle in progress** — clean slate for new work.

**Architecture (two in-flight migrations).**
- *CLI layer* (clap-aware): `XxxArgs` derive structs; shared flags as leaves/bundles in `src/options_flags/`.
- *Subcommand layer* (clap-free): `fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams)`. The Context+Params port is **complete across all 12 subcommands** as of 0.49.0.
- Subcommands: `chid`, `desc`, `list`, `show`, `validate-desc`, `fix-desc`, `clone`, `init`, `symlink`, `sync`, `finalize`, `push`.

**Workflow I'll follow (from CLAUDE.md).**
- Dual jj repos; commit with `-R .` / `-R .claude`, paired `ochid:` trailers.
- Every plan starts with a version bump; single-step (`X.Y.Z`) vs multi-step (`X.Y.Z-N`).
- `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` for the commit→push→finalize flow, two approval gates.
- Per-file review checkpoints; never commit/push without explicit approval.
- Speculation gets a "The bot thinks …" marker.

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	r"""Renumber `notes/todo.md` entries in `## Todo` and `## Bugs`.
3	
4	- Matches any leading `N. ` prefix (not only `1. `), so the script
5	  closes gaps after deletions and absorbs insertions equally well.
6	- Shifts continuation-line indent by the delta in prefix width
7	  (e.g. 4→3 when a 2-digit entry becomes 1-digit, or +1 the other
8	  way). Empty lines pass through.
9	- Idempotent on already-renumbered content.
10	- Convention: intro paragraphs in `## Todo` and `## Bugs` begin
11	  every line with exactly 1 leading space so they don't match
12	  the entry regex (`^\d+\.\s`). Minimum is the principled choice
13	  here — the convention's job is to dodge the column-0 anchor,
14	  not to add visual emphasis. CommonMark strips up to 3 leading
15	  spaces on paragraphs, so the indent is invisible in the
16	  rendered output.
17	
18	Slated for replacement by a Rust `vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo`
19	subcommand pair in a future cycle (see notes/todo.md).
20	"""
21	
22	import re
23	
24	PATH = "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/todo.md"
25	
26	# Regex for a leading `N. ` on a line — `\d+` is one or more digits,
27	# `\.` is a literal dot, `\s` is a whitespace character.
28	NUM_RE = re.compile(r"^(\d+)\.\s")
29	
30	
31	def renumber(content: str) -> str:
32	    """Walk `## Todo` and `## Bugs`, renumber entries, shift continuations."""
33	    # Split the file into lines, walk them, build the new file line-by-line
34	    # in `out`, then rejoin at the end.
35	    lines = content.split("\n")
36	    out: list[str] = []
37	
38	    # State carried across the loop:
39	    section: str | None = None  # current `## Heading`, or None outside any
40	    entry_num = 0  # running entry count within `section`
41	    shift = 0  # for the most-recent entry's continuation
42	    # lines: how many spaces to add (positive)
43	    # or remove (negative) from their indent so
44	    # they stay aligned under the new prefix.
45	
46	    for line in lines:
47	        if line.startswith("## "):
48	            # Section heading — entering a new section; reset counters.
49	
50	            # Strip trailing spaces
51	            section = line.strip()
52	
53	            # Number start with 0 pre-incremented below
54	            entry_num = 0
55	
56	            # Actual value calcuated below  on a match
57	            # If it doesn't match no shifting will be performed
58	            shift = 0
59	
60	            # Output section line as is even trailing spaces(?)
61	            # I wonder if this should be "section" instead?
62	            out.append(line)
63	            continue
64	
65	        # Only the two numbered sections need renumbering.
66	        if section in ("## Todo", "## Bugs"):
67	            m = NUM_RE.match(line)
68	            if m:
69	                # Entry first line: starts with `N. `. Renumber to the next
70	                # sequential value and record the prefix-width delta for the
71	                # entry's continuation lines that follow.
72	                old_prefix = m.group(0)  # e.g. "9. "  or "10. "
73	                entry_num += 1
74	                new_prefix = f"{entry_num}. "  # e.g. "10. " or "9. "
75	                out.append(new_prefix + line[len(old_prefix) :])
76	                shift = len(new_prefix) - len(old_prefix)
77	                continue
78	
79	            if shift != 0 and line.strip() != "":
80	                # Assumes no empty lines between numbered items(?)
81	
82	                # Continuation line under the current entry. If the entry's
83	                # prefix changed width (shift != 0), pad or trim the leading
84	                # spaces by the same amount so the text stays aligned under
85	                # the new prefix. Blank lines pass through (no indent to fix).
86	                leading = len(line) - len(line.lstrip(" "))
87	                new_leading = max(0, leading + shift)
88	                out.append(" " * new_leading + line.lstrip(" "))
89	                continue
90	
91	        # Everything else (blank lines, lines outside the two numbered
92	        # sections, continuation lines when shift == 0) passes through
93	        # unchanged.
94	        out.append(line)
95	
96	    return "\n".join(out)
97	
98	
99	def main() -> None:
100	    with open(PATH, encoding="utf-8") as f:
101	        content = f.read()
102	    content = renumber(content)
103	    with open(PATH, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
104	        f.write(content)
105	    print("Renumbered todo.md")
106	
107	
108	if __name__ == "__main__":
109	    main()
110	
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	The chores-NN files are numbered in sequence; older ones are closed
9	(chores-09.md and earlier — through the 0.48.x cycles), and the
10	active one ([chores-10.md](chores-10.md), the 0.49.0 cycle onward)
11	also carries the **refactor-tracking** tables (the Context+Params
12	port and the options_flags extraction).
13	
14	App architecture — the CLI-args / `Context`+`Params` split, a
15	generic module map, the subcommand model, and what the
16	Context+Params port and the options_flags extraction *are* (the
17	*live status* is in chores-10.md) — lives at the repo root in
18	[`../ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md).
19	
20	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
21	database to better manage the information, TBD.
22	
23	Examples chore file:
24	```
25	# Chores-01.md
26	 
27	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
28	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
29	how and why of a particular chore.
30	
31	## Create a binary that lists jj info 
32	
33	This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
34	and using `jj-lib`
35	```
36	
37	## Workflow and conventions
38	
39	Bot-facing workflow, versioning, and code conventions live in
40	[`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md). Start there for:
41	
42	- **Versioning during development** — single-step vs multi-step,
43	  `-N` pre-release suffixes, done-marker discipline.
44	- **Code Conventions** — doc comments on every file / fn / method,
45	  `// OK: …` justifications on `unwrap*` calls, ask-on-ambiguity,
46	  stuck detection.
47	- **Commit-Push-Finalize Flow** — two-checkpoint per-step
48	  discipline with hard stop after finalize.
49	
50	## Todo format
51	
52	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
53	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
54	references.
55	
56	Todo items use lazy numbering — every entry begins with `1. ` and the
57	markdown renderer auto-numbers them. Reorder or insert items without
58	renumbering, and reference an entry by its displayed number ("let's
59	work on #3"). The Done section keeps `-` bullets — items aren't
60	referenced by number once completed.
61	
62	Reference citations are **double-bracketed** — `[[1]]`, or
63	`[[2]],[[3]]` for several — so the brackets show when rendered
64	(a bare `[1]` renders as just `1`, which doesn't read as a
65	reference). Separate multiple with a comma: `[[2]],[[3]]`, not
66	`[2,3]` (a single ref key — won't resolve) or `[[2]][[3]]`. The
67	`[N]:` definition lines and inline `[text](url)` / `[text](#anchor)`
68	links stay single-bracketed (their brackets already show in
69	source, and the link text reads as a link when rendered).
70	
71	Examples:
72	
73	# Todo
74	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
75	- Fix bug Y [[1]]
76	
77	# Done
78	- Fixed issue Z [[2]],[[3]]
79	
80	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
81	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
82	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
83	
84	## Reference numbering
85	
86	Every note file (`todo.md`, `chores-NN.md`, `done.md`) keeps a
87	file-local `# References` section at the bottom. Reference numbers
88	are scoped to that file — `[1]` in `chores-07.md` and `[1]` in
89	`chores-01.md` are independent slots that may point at completely
90	different URLs. New chores files start their numbering at `[1]`.
91	
92	Treat `[N]` like a **footnote**: the number is a local slot, only
93	meaningful within its file's `# References`. So a `[N]` *citation*
94	(bare `[N]`, or doubled `[[N]]` — see below) never reuses another
95	file's number; to cite a target a sibling file references, pick
96	your own next-local slot and define it (the same target may carry
97	a different number in each file). A `[N]` *inside a code span*
98	(`` `[72]` ``) is different — that's a quoted identifier: literal
99	text naming a ref-key (often from another file's namespace), data,
100	not a citation; it needs no definition here. To point at a section
101	of another file from prose, use an inline link with an anchor —
102	`[that section](../chores-07.md#…)` — not a bare number.
103	
104	A `chores-NN.md` `# References` entry is usually a
105	`/notes/<file>.md#anchor` (or `/ARCHITECTURE.md`) path, but may
106	also be a **commit reference** —
107	`[N]: <commit-url-with-12-hex-SHA> "<full-40-hex-SHA>"`, cited by
108	a section's `Commits:` line. See
109	[`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references) for the
110	why and the exact shape.
111	
112	A file's `# References` can be **re-packed** to a contiguous
113	`[1]..[N]` in first-citation-appearance order — walk the file's
114	prose in document order (`todo.md`: `## Todo` then `## Done`;
115	`chores-NN.md`: top to bottom) and number refs as their first
116	`[[N]]` citation appears. This is a file-local rewrite — only
117	that file's `[[N]]` citations and `[N]:` definitions move; every
118	target and sibling file is untouched. A `[[N]]` inside a `` ` ``
119	code span is a literal token, not a citation, and is left alone.
120	Do it opportunistically (when the namespace has drifted enough to
121	annoy), not on a schedule: `todo.md` fragments fastest (entries
122	land and get pruned every cycle) and is the usual candidate;
123	`chores-NN.md` / `done.md` are append-mostly and only need it
124	after an unusual event (e.g. a bulk retrofit that allocated slots
125	out of document order).
126	
127	## Retiring Done entries
128	
129	`todo.md`'s `## Done` section is a rolling buffer of recently shipped
130	work, not a permanent log. Move entries into `done.md` at two natural
131	beats:
132	
133	- **Closing a ladder** — when the final `X.Y.Z` (no suffix) commit
134	  ships, decide which prior entries are no longer needed for nearby
135	  context and migrate them.
136	- **Opening a new ladder** — at `X.Y.Z-0`, do the same sweep before
137	  bumping the version.
138	
139	Migration mechanics:
140	
141	- Move the bullet itself from `todo.md > ## Done` to
142	  `done.md` (preserving the original ref number).
143	- Copy any references the moved entries cite into
144	  `done.md`'s `# References` section (those refs are file-local,
145	  so coexisting with `todo.md`'s namespace is fine).
146	- Prune any references in `todo.md > # References` no longer cited
147	  by anything in `## In Progress` / `## Todo` / `## Done`. This
148	  frees the numbers for future reuse.
149	
```

1	//! The `validate-desc` subcommand: scan a revision range and check
2	//! each commit's `ochid:` trailer against the cross-referenced
3	//! repo — flagging missing, malformed, or dangling links.
4	//!
5	//! Read-only diagnostic; exits non-zero if any commit has issues.
6	//! The bot's "did the ochid wiring stay consistent?" check.
7	
8	use std::path::PathBuf;
9	
10	use clap::Args;
11	use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
12	use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
13	
14	use log::{debug, info};
15	
16	use crate::common;
17	use crate::context::Context;
18	use crate::desc_helpers::{
19	    DEFAULT_ID_LEN, TitleMatch, VC_CONFIG_FILE, extract_bare_id, find_matching_commit,
20	    ochid_prefix_from_config, other_repo_from_config, validate_ochid,
21	};
22	use crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;
23	use crate::toml_simple;
24	
25	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
26	pub struct ValidateDescArgs {
27	    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
28	    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
29	    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
30	
31	    /// Number of commits (per dotted side)
32	    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
33	    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
34	
35	    /// Revision(s) to scan
36	    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
37	    pub revision: String,
38	
39	    /// Number of commits to scan
40	    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "commits", value_name = "COMMITS")]
41	    pub limit: Option<usize>,
42	
43	    /// Path to jj repo
44	    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
45	    pub repo: PathBuf,
46	
47	    /// Path to the other repo [default: from .vc-config.toml]
48	    #[arg(long = "other-repo")]
49	    pub other_repo: Option<PathBuf>,
50	
51	    /// Expected changeID length
52	    #[arg(long = "id-len", default_value_t = DEFAULT_ID_LEN)]
53	    pub id_len: usize,
54	}
55	
56	/// Inputs to the validate-desc op, flat, owned, clap-free.
57	///
58	/// Mirrors `ValidateDescArgs`: positional `REVISION` / `COMMITS`
59	/// (`pos_rev` / `pos_count`), `--revision` / `--commits`
60	/// (`revision` / `limit`), `--repo`, `--other-repo`, `--id-len`.
61	pub struct ValidateDescParams {
62	    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
63	    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
64	    pub revision: String,
65	    pub limit: Option<usize>,
66	    pub repo: PathBuf,
67	    pub other_repo: Option<PathBuf>,
68	    pub id_len: usize,
69	}
70	
71	impl From<&ValidateDescArgs> for ValidateDescParams {
72	    /// Convert clap-derived `ValidateDescArgs` into the flat
73	    /// `ValidateDescParams` (total — every field copies straight over).
74	    fn from(a: &ValidateDescArgs) -> Self {
75	        Self {
76	            pos_rev: a.pos_rev.clone(),
77	            pos_count: a.pos_count,
78	            revision: a.revision.clone(),
79	            limit: a.limit,
80	            repo: a.repo.clone(),
81	            other_repo: a.other_repo.clone(),
82	            id_len: a.id_len,
83	        }
84	    }
85	}
86	
87	/// Status of a single commit's ochid validation.
88	enum CommitStatus {
89	    Ok,
90	    Lost,                     // ochid marked as "lost" (unrecoverable)
91	    None_,                    // ochid marked as "none" (no counterpart)
92	    NeedsFixed(String),       // summary of issues
93	    MissingNoTitle,           // no ochid, no title to match on
94	    MissingNoMatch,           // no ochid, no title match in other repo
95	    MissingAmbiguous(usize),  // no ochid, multiple title matches
96	    MissingWithMatch(String), // no ochid, unique title match found
97	}
98	
99	impl SubcommandRunner for ValidateDescArgs {
100	    type Params = ValidateDescParams;
101	
102	    /// Delegate to the existing `From<&ValidateDescArgs>` impl
103	    /// above (total — never fails).
104	    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String> {
105	        Ok(ValidateDescParams::from(self))
106	    }
107	
108	    /// Run the existing `validate_desc` op.
109	    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
110	        validate_desc(ctx, params)
111	    }
112	}
113	
114	/// Run the `validate-desc` subcommand: scan the resolved revision
115	/// range and report each commit's ochid status against the other
116	/// repo; errors if any commit has issues.
117	///
118	/// `ctx` is unused today (validate-desc reads `.vc-config.toml`,
119	/// not the user config, and doesn't touch the `--log` path); it's
120	/// present for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.
121	pub fn validate_desc(
122	    _ctx: &Context,
123	    params: &ValidateDescParams,
124	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
125	    debug!("validate-desc: enter");
126	    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&params.repo)?;
127	
128	    // Resolve other repo: --other-repo flag, or fall back to .vc-config.toml
129	    let other_repo_path = if let Some(ref p) = params.other_repo {
130	        p.clone()
131	    } else {
132	        let config = toml_simple::toml_load(&params.repo.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;
133	        params.repo.join(other_repo_from_config(&config)?)
134	    };
135	
136	    let (other_workspace, other_repo) = common::load_repo(&other_repo_path)?;
137	    let other_config = toml_simple::toml_load(&other_repo_path.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;
138	    let other_prefix = ochid_prefix_from_config(&other_config)?;
139	
140	    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
141	        params.pos_rev.as_deref(),
142	        params.pos_count,
143	        &params.revision,
144	        params.limit,
145	        "@",
146	    );
147	
148	    let (ids, _anchor_index) = common::collect_ids(
149	        &workspace,
150	        &repo,
151	        &spec.rev,
152	        spec.desc_count,
153	        spec.anc_count,
154	    )?;
155	    if ids.is_empty() {
156	        return Err(format!("no commits found for revision '{}'", params.revision).into());
157	    }
158	
159	    let root_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
160	    let mut valid = 0;
161	    let mut lost = 0;
162	    let mut none = 0;
163	    let mut issues_count = 0;
164	    let mut missing = 0;
165	
166	    let col_header = "STAT CHANGEID      TITLE";
167	    info!("{col_header}");
168	
169	    for commit_id in &ids {
170	        if *commit_id == root_id {
171	            continue;
172	        }
173	        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
174	        let desc = commit.description();
175	        let change_hex = jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
176	        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
177	        let first_line = desc.lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
178	        let display_title = if first_line.is_empty() {
179	            "(no description set)"
180	        } else {
181	            first_line
182	        };
183	
184	        let current_ochid = common::extract_ochid(&commit);
185	
186	        let status = if let Some(ref ochid_val) = current_ochid {
187	            let bare = extract_bare_id(ochid_val);
188	            if bare == "lost" {
189	                CommitStatus::Lost
190	            } else if bare == "none" {
191	                CommitStatus::None_
192	            } else {
193	                let issues = validate_ochid(
194	                    ochid_val,
195	                    &other_prefix,
196	                    params.id_len,
197	                    &other_workspace,
198	                    &other_repo,
199	                );
200	                if issues.any() {
201	                    CommitStatus::NeedsFixed(issues.summary())
202	                } else {
203	                    CommitStatus::Ok
204	                }
205	            }
206	        } else {
207	            match find_matching_commit(&commit, &other_workspace, &other_repo)? {
208	                TitleMatch::NoTitle => CommitStatus::MissingNoTitle,
209	                TitleMatch::One(id) => {
210	                    let short_id = &id[..id.len().min(params.id_len)];
211	                    CommitStatus::MissingWithMatch(format!("{other_prefix}{short_id}"))
212	                }
213	                TitleMatch::Ambiguous(n) => CommitStatus::MissingAmbiguous(n),
214	                TitleMatch::None => CommitStatus::MissingNoMatch,
215	            }
216	        };
217	
218	        match status {
219	            CommitStatus::Ok => {
220	                valid += 1;
221	                info!("ok   {change_short}  {display_title}");
222	            }
223	            CommitStatus::Lost => {
224	                lost += 1;
225	                info!("lost {change_short}  {display_title}");
226	            }
227	            CommitStatus::None_ => {
228	                none += 1;
229	                info!("none {change_short}  {display_title}");
230	            }
231	            CommitStatus::NeedsFixed(summary) => {
232	                issues_count += 1;
233	                info!("err  {change_short}  {display_title}  [{summary}]");
234	            }
235	            CommitStatus::MissingWithMatch(ochid) => {
236	                missing += 1;
237	                info!("miss {change_short}  {display_title}  [match: {ochid}]");
238	            }
239	            CommitStatus::MissingNoTitle => {
240	                missing += 1;
241	                info!("miss {change_short}  {display_title}");
242	            }
243	            CommitStatus::MissingAmbiguous(n) => {
244	                missing += 1;
245	                info!("miss {change_short}  {display_title}  [{n} title matches, ambiguous]");
246	            }
247	            CommitStatus::MissingNoMatch => {
248	                missing += 1;
249	                info!("miss {change_short}  {display_title}  [no matching title in other repo]");
250	            }
251	        }
252	    }
253	
254	    let total = valid + lost + none + issues_count + missing;
255	    if total > 10 {
256	        info!("{col_header}");
257	    }
258	    info!("");
259	    info!(
260	        "{valid} valid, {lost} lost, {none} none, {issues_count} issues, {missing} missing (of {} total)",
261	        ids.len()
262	    );
263	    if issues_count > 0 {
264	        debug!("validate-desc: exit with issues");
265	        Err(format!("{issues_count} commit(s) have issues").into())
266	    } else {
267	        debug!("validate-desc: exit");
268	        Ok(())
269	    }
270	}
271	
```
