136	/// Run the `fix-desc` subcommand: scan the resolved revision range
137	/// and repair each commit's ochid trailer against the other repo;
138	/// errors if any commit could not be fixed.
139	///
140	/// `ctx` is unused today (fix-desc reads `.vc-config.toml`, not the
141	/// user config, and doesn't touch the `--log` path); it's present
142	/// for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.
143	pub fn fix_desc(_ctx: &Context, params: &FixDescParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
144	    debug!("fix-desc: enter");
145	    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&params.repo)?;
146	
147	    // Resolve other repo: --other-repo flag, or fall back to .vc-config.toml
148	    let other_repo_path = if let Some(ref p) = params.other_repo {
149	        p.clone()
150	    } else {
151	        let config = toml_simple::toml_load(&params.repo.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;
152	        params.repo.join(other_repo_from_config(&config)?)
153	    };
154	
155	    let (other_workspace, other_repo) = common::load_repo(&other_repo_path)?;
156	    let other_config = toml_simple::toml_load(&other_repo_path.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;
157	    let other_prefix = ochid_prefix_from_config(&other_config)?;
158	
159	    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
160	        params.pos_rev.as_deref(),
161	        params.pos_count,
162	        &params.revision,
163	        params.limit,
164	        "@",
165	    );
166	
167	    let (ids, _anchor_index) = common::collect_ids(
168	        &workspace,
169	        &repo,
170	        &spec.rev,
171	        spec.desc_count,
172	        spec.anc_count,
173	    )?;
174	    if ids.is_empty() {
175	        return Err(format!("no commits found for revision '{}'", params.revision).into());
176	    }
177	
178	    let root_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
179	    let mut fixed = 0;
180	    let mut skipped = 0;
181	    let mut errors = 0;
182	
183	    for commit_id in &ids {
184	        if *commit_id == root_id {
185	            continue;
186	        }
187	        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
188	        let desc = commit.description();
189	        let change_hex = jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
190	        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
191	        let first_line = desc.lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
192	        let display_title = if first_line.is_empty() {
193	            "(no description set)"
194	        } else {
195	            first_line
196	        };
197	
198	        // Extract current ochid value
199	        let current_ochid = common::extract_ochid(&commit);
200	
201	        // Validate current ochid
202	        let issues = if let Some(ref ochid_val) = current_ochid {
203	            validate_ochid(
204	                ochid_val,
205	                &other_prefix,
206	                params.id_len,
207	                &other_workspace,
208	                &other_repo,
209	            )
210	        } else if params.add_missing {
211	            // Stop if we've hit the max-fixes limit
212	            if let Some(max) = params.max_fixes
213	                && fixed >= max
214	            {
215	                skipped += 1;
216	                if !params.no_dry_run {
217	                    info!("skip {change_short}  {display_title}  (max-fixes reached)");
218	                }
219	                continue;
220	            }
221	            // No ochid trailer — try to infer from the other repo
222	            match find_matching_commit(&commit, &other_workspace, &other_repo)? {
223	                TitleMatch::NoTitle => {
224	                    skipped += 1;
225	                    if !params.no_dry_run {
226	                        info!("skip {change_short}  {display_title}");
227	                    }
228	                    continue;
229	                }
230	                TitleMatch::One(matched_id) => {
231	                    let new_desc =
232	                        append_ochid_trailer(desc, &other_prefix, &matched_id, params.id_len);
233	                    let short_matched = &matched_id[..matched_id.len().min(params.id_len)];
234	                    if !params.no_dry_run {
235	                        info!("add  {change_short}  {display_title}  [missing]");
236	                        info!("     -> ochid: {other_prefix}{short_matched}");
237	                    } else {
238	                        jj_describe(commit_id, &new_desc, &params.repo, change_short)?;
239	                        info!("added {change_short}  {display_title}");
240	                    }
241	                    fixed += 1;
242	                    continue;
243	                }
244	                TitleMatch::Ambiguous(n) => {
245	                    skipped += 1;
246	                    if !params.no_dry_run {
247	                        info!(
248	                            "skip {change_short}  {display_title}  ({n} title matches, ambiguous)"
249	                        );
250	                    }
251	                    continue;
252	                }
253	                TitleMatch::None => {
254	                    skipped += 1;
255	                    if !params.no_dry_run {
256	                        info!(
257	                            "skip {change_short}  {display_title}  (no matching title in other repo)"
258	                        );
259	                    }
260	                    continue;
261	                }
262	            }
263	        } else {
264	            // No ochid trailer — nothing to fix
265	            skipped += 1;
266	            if !params.no_dry_run {
267	                info!("skip {change_short}  {display_title}  (no ochid trailer)");
268	            }
269	            continue;
270	        };
271	
272	        if !issues.any() {
273	            skipped += 1;
274	            if !params.no_dry_run {
275	                info!("ok   {change_short}  {display_title}");
276	            }
277	            continue;
278	        }
279	
280	        // Stop if we've hit the max-fixes limit
281	        if let Some(max) = params.max_fixes
282	            && fixed >= max
283	        {
284	            skipped += 1;
285	            if !params.no_dry_run {
286	                info!("skip {change_short}  {display_title}  (max-fixes reached)");
287	            }
288	            continue;
289	        }
290	
291	        // Resolve the full change ID from the other repo when length is wrong
292	        let resolved_id = if issues.wrong_length.is_some() {
293	            let ochid_val = current_ochid.as_deref().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
294	            let bare_id = extract_bare_id(ochid_val);
295	            resolve_full_change_id(bare_id, &other_workspace, &other_repo)?
296	        } else {
297	            None
298	        };
299	
300	        // Build the fixed description
301	        let mut new_desc = fix_ochid_in_description(
302	            desc,
303	            &other_prefix,
304	            params.id_len,
305	            params.title.as_deref(),
306	            resolved_id.as_deref(),
307	        );
308	
309	        // Re-extract the fixed ochid value for post-validation
310	        let mut fixed_ochid = extract_ochid_from_desc(&new_desc);
311	
312	        let post_issues = if let Some(ref v) = fixed_ochid {
313	            validate_ochid(
314	                v,
315	                &other_prefix,
316	                params.id_len,
317	                &other_workspace,
318	                &other_repo,
319	            )
320	        } else {
321	            OchidIssues {
322	                wrong_prefix: None,
323	                wrong_length: None,
324	                not_found: true,
325	                bare_id: String::new(),
326	            }
327	        };
328	
329	        if post_issues.not_found {
330	            if let Some(ref fallback) = params.fallback {
331	                // Replace the ochid line with the fallback value
332	                let mut fb_lines: Vec<String> = new_desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();
333	                for line in &mut fb_lines {
334	                    if line.trim().starts_with("ochid:") {
335	                        *line = format!("ochid: {fallback}");
336	                    }
337	                }
338	                let fb_desc = if new_desc.ends_with('\n') {
339	                    format!("{}\n", fb_lines.join("\n"))
340	                } else {
341	                    fb_lines.join("\n")
342	                };
343	                new_desc = fb_desc;
344	                fixed_ochid = Some(fallback.clone());
345	            } else {
346	                errors += 1;
347	                info!(
348	                    "err  {change_short}  {display_title}  (ID not found, ochid: {})",
349	                    fixed_ochid.as_deref().unwrap_or("?") // OK: "?" placeholder when ochid unresolved
350	                );
351	                continue;
352	            }
353	        }
354	
355	        if !params.no_dry_run {
356	            info!(
357	                "fix  {change_short}  {display_title}  [{}]",
358	                issues.summary()
359	            );
360	            if let Some(ref v) = fixed_ochid {
361	                info!("     -> ochid: {v}");
362	            }
363	        } else {
364	            jj_describe(commit_id, &new_desc, &params.repo, change_short)?;
365	            let fixed_title = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
366	            info!("fixed {change_short}  {fixed_title}");
367	        }
368	        fixed += 1;
369	    }
370	
371	    info!("");
372	    info!(
373	        "{fixed} fixed, {skipped} skipped, {errors} errors (of {} total)",
374	        ids.len()
375	    );
376	    if errors > 0 {
377	        debug!("fix-desc: exit with errors");
378	        Err(format!("{errors} commit(s) could not be fixed").into())
379	    } else {
380	        debug!("fix-desc: exit");
381	        Ok(())
382	    }
383	}
384	
385	/// Run `jj describe` to rewrite a commit's description.
386	fn jj_describe(
387	    commit_id: &jj_lib::backend::CommitId,
388	    new_desc: &str,
389	    repo_path: &std::path::Path,
390	    _change_short: &str,
391	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
392	    crate::common::run(
393	        "jj",
394	        &[
395	            "describe",
396	            "-m",
397	            new_desc,
398	            "-r",
399	            &commit_id.hex(),
400	            "-R",
401	            &repo_path.to_string_lossy(),
402	            "--ignore-immutable",
403	        ],
404	        repo_path,
405	    )?;
406	    Ok(())
407	}
408

28	use std::path::Path;

31	use clap::{CommandFactory, FromArgMatches, Parser, Subcommand};

35	use crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;
36	
37	/// Banner string emitted as the first line of normal command runs
38	/// and shown at the top of subcommand `--help` output. Built from
39	/// `Cargo.toml`'s name + version at compile time so it stays in
40	/// sync with the bumped version.
41	const BANNER: &str = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
42	
43	/// Top-level about line — name, version, and the project tagline
44	/// on a single line. Used as the top-level `about` so `vc-x1 -h`
45	/// reads as one banner-plus-tagline header instead of two stacked
46	/// lines.
47	const TOP_ABOUT: &str = concat!(
48	    env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"),
49	    " ",
50	    env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
51	    " - jj workspace tooling"
52	);
53	
54	/// Build the clap command tree with `BANNER` set as `before_help`
55	/// on every subcommand (transitively). Top-level skips `before_help`
56	/// because its own `about` already carries the name+version+tagline.
57	/// Walks via `mut_subcommand` so individual subcommand
58	/// `#[command(long_about = ...)]` blocks don't have to repeat the
59	/// banner text.
60	fn cli_with_banner() -> clap::Command {
61	    fn add_to_subs(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
62	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
63	            .get_subcommands()
64	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
65	            .collect();
66	        for name in names {
67	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
68	        }
69	        cmd
70	    }
71	    fn add_with_banner(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
72	        cmd = cmd.before_help(BANNER);
73	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
74	            .get_subcommands()
75	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
76	            .collect();
77	        for name in names {
78	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
79	        }
80	        cmd
81	    }
82	    add_to_subs(Cli::command())
83	}
84	
85	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
86	#[command(about = TOP_ABOUT, max_term_width = 80)]
87	pub struct Cli {
88	    /// Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then
89	    /// continue. With no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
90	    ///
91	    /// Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after
92	    /// printing): the banner now rides along with normal
93	    /// subcommand execution rather than gating it, so scripts
94	    /// can capture the version *and* the command's output in
95	    /// one invocation.
96	    #[arg(short = 'V', long = "version", global = true, action = clap::ArgAction::SetTrue)]
97	    pub version: bool,
98	
99	    /// Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace
100	    #[arg(short, long, global = true, action = clap::ArgAction::Count)]
101	    pub verbose: u8,
102	
103	    /// Log file path (captures all levels)
104	    #[arg(long, global = true)]
105	    pub log: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,
106	
107	    #[command(subcommand)]
108	    pub(crate) command: Option<Commands>,
109	}
110	
111	#[derive(Subcommand, Debug)]
112	pub(crate) enum Commands {
113	    /// Print the changeID for a revision
114	    Chid(chid::ChidArgs),
115	
116	    /// Show full description of a commit
117	    Desc(desc::DescArgs),
118	
119	    /// List commits in a jj repo
120	    List(list::ListArgs),
121	
122	    /// Show commit details and diff summary
123	    Show(show::ShowArgs),
124	
125	    /// Validate commit descriptions against the other repo
126	    #[command(
127	        long_about = "Validate commit descriptions against the other repo.\n\n\
128	        Output columns: STATUS  CHANGEID  TITLE  [DETAILS]\n\n\
129	        Status labels:\n  \
130	          ok   — ochid trailer is valid\n  \
131	          err  — ochid has issues (wrong prefix, wrong length, ID not found)\n  \
132	          miss — no ochid trailer; shows match from other repo if found"
133	    )]
134	    ValidateDesc(validate_desc::ValidateDescArgs),
135	
136	    /// Fix commit descriptions against the other repo (dry-run by default)
137	    #[command(long_about = "Fix commit descriptions against the other repo.\n\n\
138	        Default is dry-run; use --no-dry-run to write changes.\n\n\
139	        Output columns: STATUS  CHANGEID  TITLE  [DETAILS]\n\n\
140	        Status labels:\n  \
141	          ok    — ochid trailer is valid (no change)\n  \
142	          fix   — ochid has issues, shows proposed fix (dry-run)\n  \
143	          fixed — ochid was rewritten (--no-dry-run)\n  \
144	          add   — missing ochid, match found, shows proposed addition (dry-run)\n  \
145	          added — missing ochid was added (--no-dry-run)\n  \
146	          skip  — skipped (no ochid, no match, or max-fixes reached)\n  \
147	          err   — ID not found and no --fallback provided")]
148	    FixDesc(fix_desc::FixDescArgs),
149	
150	    /// Clone a dual-repo project
151	    Clone(clone::CloneArgs),
152	
153	    /// Create a new dual-repo project
154	    Init(init::InitArgs),
155	
156	    /// Create Claude Code project symlink
157	    Symlink(symlink::SymlinkArgs),
158	
159	    /// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes
160	    #[command(long_about = "Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.\n\n\
161	        Repo set is resolved (in order):\n  \
162	          - `-R` / `--repo`     exact list (back-compat / arbitrary multi-repo)\n  \
163	          - `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` dual-repo roles via `.vc-config.toml`\n  \
164	          - neither             default: `code,bot` when dual, else `code`\n\n\
165	        Default is --check (verify only) — fatal if any repo needs\n\
166	        action. Re-run with --no-check to apply (rebase / fast-forward).\n\
167	        Per repo:\n  \
168	          - up-to-date        nothing to do\n  \
169	          - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote\n  \
170	          - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)\n  \
171	          - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts\n  \
172	          - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip\n\n\
173	        Scripts and automation should pass --check or --no-check\n\
174	        explicitly — defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the\n\
175	        contract. Interactive use can rely on the default.\n\n\
176	        After the bookmark action, `@` is rebased onto the (possibly\n\
177	        advanced) bookmark if it isn't already a descendant, so trailing\n\
178	        working-copy writes (e.g. `.claude`'s `/exit` tail) don't end up\n\
179	        orphaned on a stale branch.\n\n\
180	        On any failure, every repo is reverted to its starting state via\n\
181	        `jj op restore`. Working-copy files are preserved across the\n\
182	        revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.\n\n\
183	        Output shape:\n  \
184	          - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)\n  \
185	          - action needed (--check):  per-repo fetch + state + fatal error\n  \
186	          - action needed (--no-check): per-repo fetch + state + actions\n  \
187	          - --quiet:        no output; exit code signals success")]
188	    Sync(sync::SyncArgs),
189	
190	    /// Squash, set bookmark, and/or push a jj repo
191	    #[command(long_about = "Squash, set bookmark, and/or push a jj repo.\n\n\
192	        Designed for the bot to atomically finalize its session repo:\n\
193	        --detach exits immediately, --delay waits for trailing writes,\n\
194	        --squash folds them in, --bookmark + --push sends it upstream.\n\
195	        Every flag is opt-in. See README.md for details.")]
196	    Finalize(finalize::FinalizeArgs),
197	
198	    /// Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command
199	    #[command(
200	        long_about = "Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.\n\n\
201	        Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a\n\
202	        single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a\n\
203	        state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can\n\
204	        resume without re-doing completed stages.\n\n\
205	        Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)\n\
206	        → message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →\n\
207	        commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both\n\
208	        → push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /\n\
209	        commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via\n\
210	        `jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.\n\
211	        After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and\n\
212	        recovery is forward-only.\n\n\
213	        Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes\n\
214	        to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body\n\
215	        across resumes so only the first invocation needs them."
216	    )]
217	    Push(push::PushArgs),
218	}
219	
220	/// Permanent sanity check for the `main`-bookmark tracking state
221	/// in both repos of the dual-repo workspace. Emits one line on
222	/// entry and one on exit of every command. If entry and exit
223	/// differ, the executing command is the culprit; if entry differs
224	/// from the previous command's exit, something *between*
225	/// invocations broke it. Originally added in 0.37.2 as a temporary
226	/// diagnostic; promoted to permanent in 0.37.4 after the user
227	/// reported "happens more than once".
228	///
229	/// Emits at `log::debug!` (since 0.52.0-1) — default runs stay
230	/// quiet, and the signal remains available under `-v` when
231	/// investigating. The detached `finalize --exec` child runs at
232	/// default verbosity so it stays silent without needing a special
233	/// gate at the call site.
234	///
235	/// Walks up from cwd to locate the workspace root (the directory
236	/// whose `.vc-config.toml` has `path = "/"`), then probes `<root>`
237	/// and `<root>/.claude`. Same labeling whether the user runs from
238	/// the app root, from `.claude`, or from any subdir.
239	pub fn bm_track(phase: &str, command_name: &str) {
240	    let header = format!("bm-track {phase} vc-x1 {command_name}");
241	    let root = match common::find_workspace_root() {
242	        Some(r) => r,
243	        None => {
244	            log::debug!("{header}: no-workspace");
245	            return;
246	        }
247	    };
248	    let repos: [(std::path::PathBuf, &str); 2] =
249	        [(root.clone(), "app"), (root.join(".claude"), ".claude")];
250	    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
251	    for (repo, label) in repos {
252	        if !repo.join(".jj").exists() {
253	            parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=no-jj"));
254	            continue;
255	        }
256	        match bm_track_one(&repo, "main", "origin") {
257	            Ok(true) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=tracked")),
258	            Ok(false) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=NOT_TRACKED")),
259	            Err(e) => parts.push(format!(
260	                "{label}(main)=err({})",
261	                e.lines().next().unwrap_or("")
262	            )),
263	        }
264	    }
265	    log::debug!("{header}: {}", parts.join(", "));
266	}
267	
268	/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.
269	/// Returns `Ok(true)` if the tracked-list entry for `bookmark` shows
270	/// an `@<remote>` line (in any form — `@origin:` when synced, or
271	/// `@origin (ahead by N commits):` / similar when divergent — both
272	/// still count as tracking). `Ok(false)` when no such line exists
273	/// (bookmark isn't tracking this remote or doesn't exist). `Err` on
274	/// subprocess failure.
275	fn bm_track_one(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str, remote: &str) -> Result<bool, String> {
276	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
277	    let output = std::process::Command::new("jj")
278	        .args(["bookmark", "list", "--tracked", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str])
279	        .output()
280	        .map_err(|e| format!("spawn: {e}"))?;
281	    if !output.status.success() {
282	        return Err(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string());
283	    }
284	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
285	    // Accept both the synced form (`@origin:`) and the decorated
286	    // divergent form (`@origin (ahead by N commits):`), which still
287	    // represents a tracking relationship.
288	    let colon = format!("@{remote}:");
289	    let paren = format!("@{remote} ");
290	    Ok(stdout.lines().any(|l| {
291	        let t = l.trim_start();
292	        t.starts_with(&colon) || t.starts_with(&paren)
293	    }))
294	}
295	
296	fn main() -> ExitCode {
297	    CompleteEnv::with_factory(cli_with_banner).complete();
298	    let matches = cli_with_banner().get_matches();
299	    let cli = match Cli::from_arg_matches(&matches) {
300	        Ok(cli) => cli,
301	        Err(e) => {
302	            e.exit();
303	        }
304	    };
305	
306	    let log_path = cli.log.as_ref().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().to_string());
307	    logging::CliLogger::init(cli.verbose, log_path.as_deref());
308	
309	    // `-V` / `--version`: emit the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the
310	    // first line and continue. With no subcommand, the banner is
311	    // the whole invocation. Uniform `BANNER` (not the
312	    // `vc-x1-<sub>` form clap's `propagate_version` would print)
313	    // — the version is the binary's regardless of which
314	    // subcommand it routes to.
315	    if cli.version {
316	        log::info!("{BANNER}");
317	    }
318	
319	    let Some(cmd) = cli.command else {
320	        // No subcommand. If `-V` was set the banner has already
321	        // printed, so exit success; otherwise mirror clap's "a
322	        // subcommand is required" error by printing usage and
323	        // exiting non-zero.
324	        if cli.version {
325	            return ExitCode::SUCCESS;
326	        }
327	        let mut cmd = cli_with_banner();
328	        let _ = cmd.print_help();
329	        return ExitCode::FAILURE;
330	    };
331	
332	    // Surface any failure markers left by previous detached
333	    // `finalize --exec` children before running the next command.
334	    // Skip when this *is* the exec child — surfacing destroys the
335	    // markers, and the child's log isn't where the user looks for
336	    // them. This gate covers one race (detached child eating its
337	    // own prior markers); the broader surfacing model has more
338	    // gaps (stale-forever, concurrent double-print, mid-write torn
339	    // read, no notify-at-failure) tracked at
340	    // [../notes/todo.md#bugs] — see the
341	    // `finalize::surface_previous_failures is racy` entry.
342	    match &cmd {
343	        Commands::Finalize(args) => {
344	            if !args.exec {
345	                finalize::surface_previous_failures();
346	            }
347	        }
348	        _ => finalize::surface_previous_failures(),
349	    }
350	
351	    let ctx = match context::Context::load(cli.log) {
352	        Ok(c) => c,
353	        Err(e) => {
354	            error!("{e}");
355	            return ExitCode::FAILURE;
356	        }
357	    };
358	
359	    match cmd {
360	        Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
361	        Commands::Desc(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
362	        Commands::List(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
363	        Commands::Show(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
364	        Commands::ValidateDesc(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
365	        Commands::FixDesc(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
366	        Commands::Clone(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
367	        Commands::Init(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
368	        Commands::Symlink(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
369	        Commands::Sync(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
370	        Commands::Finalize(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
371	        Commands::Push(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),
372	    }
373	}
374	
375	#[cfg(test)]
376	mod tests {
377	    use super::*;
378	
379	    #[test]
380	    fn unknown_command() {
381	        let err = Cli::try_parse_from(["vc-x1", "bogus"])
382	            .unwrap_err()
383	            .to_string();
384	        assert!(err.contains("bogus"));
385	    }
386	}

1	//! `SubcommandRunner` trait
2	//!
3	//! The purpose is to simplify invoking a subcommand so `main`
4	//! can invoke any subcommand with one line:
5	//!
6	//! `Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),`
7	//!
8	//! Instead of the ~11 lines that the default `dispatch` below
9	//! now encapsulates.
10	//!
11	//! Implementors provide:
12	//!
13	//! - [`SubcommandRunner::to_params`] — build the clap-free
14	//!   `Params` from the clap `Args` (covers both `From` /
15	//!   `TryFrom` shapes uniformly via `Result<_, String>`).
16	//! - [`SubcommandRunner::run`] — the subcommand body.
17	//!
18	//! The default [`SubcommandRunner::dispatch`] builds `Params`
19	//! via `to_params`, runs via `run`, and maps the result to
20	//! `ExitCode`. Errors at any stage log via `error!` and return
21	//! `ExitCode::FAILURE`. `Context` is loaded once in `main` and
22	//! passed in by reference so a single `Context` is shared across
23	//! the (one) match arm that actually runs.
24	//!
25	//! ## See also
26	//!
27	//! - The wider CLI-args / subcommand-layer split this trait
28	//!   formalizes: [`ARCHITECTURE.md > args → Context + Params`][arch].
29	//! - Cycle design, ladder, evaluation gate:
30	//!   [`chores-10.md > 0.50.0-0`][open].
31	//! - Worked example, trait-shape decisions, naming:
32	//!   [`chores-10.md > 0.50.0-1`][port].
33	//! - First implementor: [`crate::chid`].
34	//!
35	//! [arch]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/ARCHITECTURE.md#args--context--params
36	//! [open]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores/chores-10.md#chore-open-subcommand-trait-sweep-0500-0
37	//! [port]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores/chores-10.md#refactor-subcommandrunner-trait--chid-0500-1
38	
39	use std::process::ExitCode;
40	
41	use log::error;
42	
43	use crate::context::Context;
44	
45	/// Trait implemented by every subcommand's clap `Args` type so
46	/// `main.rs` can dispatch via a single `args.dispatch(&ctx)`
47	/// call.
48	pub trait SubcommandRunner {
49	    /// The clap-free `Params` struct the subcommand body
50	    /// consumes.
51	    type Params;
52	
53	    /// Build `Params` from `Args`. `Result<_, String>` absorbs
54	    /// both `From` (return `Ok(...)`) and `TryFrom` (forward
55	    /// the error) shapes uniformly.
56	    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;
57	
58	    /// Run the subcommand body.
59	    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;
60	
61	    /// Default dispatch: build `Params` via `to_params`, bracket
62	    /// the run with `crate::bm_track` enter/exit, execute via
63	    /// `run`, and map the result to `ExitCode`. Errors at any
64	    /// stage log via `error!` and return `ExitCode::FAILURE`.
65	    /// `bm_track` itself emits at `debug!`, so default runs stay
66	    /// quiet and the detached `finalize --exec` child (which runs
67	    /// without `-v`) silently no-ops — no per-call gate is needed
68	    /// here. `surface_previous_failures` for finalize's failure
69	    /// markers is handled in `main` before dispatch, so dispatch
70	    /// has no session-chrome responsibility at all anymore.
71	    fn dispatch(&self, ctx: &Context) -> ExitCode {
72	        let params = match self.to_params() {
73	            Ok(p) => p,
74	            Err(e) => {
75	                error!("{e}");
76	                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
77	            }
78	        };
79	
80	        // Command name is the first positional after the binary;
81	        // clap has already validated it by the time we reach
82	        // dispatch (top-level parse errors exit earlier).
83	        let command_name = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "?".to_string()); // OK: default when somehow invoked without a subcommand
84	
85	        crate::bm_track("enter", &command_name);
86	        let exit_code = match Self::run(ctx, &params) {
87	            Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,
88	            Err(e) => {
89	                error!("{e}");
90	                ExitCode::FAILURE
91	            }
92	        };
93	        crate::bm_track("exit ", &command_name);
94	        exit_code
95	    }
96	}
97

1	# Chores-11
2	
3	Continuation of `chores-10.md` (which is closed at `0.50.0` —
4	the Subcommand trait sweep). This file covers the `0.51.0`
5	cycle onward. Reference numbering is file-local — see
6	[`README.md`](../README.md#reference-numbering); chores-11
7	starts at `[1]`.
8	
9	## chore: move chores under notes/chores/ (0.51.0)
10	
11	Commits: [[1]]
12	
13	Single-step. `notes/` was dominated by ten chores files plus
14	the freshly opened `chores-11.md`; the eleven files now move
15	under `notes/chores/`, leaving `notes/` for the live notes
16	(`todo.md`, `done.md`, `README.md`, design docs). Filenames
17	stay `chores-NN.md` — preserving them keeps section-anchor
18	slugs unchanged and reduces the cross-file ref update to a
19	single mechanical substitution.
20	
21	Done.md hygiene rides along: the eleven `0.44.0`–`0.50.0`
22	entries that had been sitting in `todo.md > ## Done` migrate
23	into `done.md` (renumbered `[85]`–`[95]`), since the 0.50.0
24	close-out marks a natural batching point and keeps `todo.md`
25	focused on what's actually in flight.
26	
27	### Substitutions applied
28	
29	External callers (everything outside `notes/chores/`):
30	
31	- `/notes/chores-NN.md` → `/notes/chores/chores-NN.md` —
32	  absolute-path refs in `notes/todo.md`, `notes/done.md`,
33	  `notes/README.md`, the sibling design notes, `CLAUDE.md`,
34	  `ARCHITECTURE.md`, the root `README.md`, and the
35	  GitHub-URL fragments in `src/clone.rs` /
36	  `src/config.rs` / `src/init.rs` / `src/init/params.rs`
37	  / `src/push.rs` / `src/scope.rs` / `src/subcommand.rs` /
38	  `src/sync.rs`.
39	
40	Within-chores siblings (now under `notes/chores/`):
41	
42	- `(../ARCHITECTURE.md` → `(../../ARCHITECTURE.md` (the
43	  root `ARCHITECTURE.md` is two `..` away now).
44	- `(../src/…` → `(../../src/…`.
45	- `(README.md` → `(../README.md` (sibling
46	  `notes/README.md`).
47	- `(forks-multi-user.md` → `(../forks-multi-user.md` and
48	  the `[1]: forks-multi-user.md` ref def in
49	  `chores-08.md` (similar fixups for
50	  `vc-x1-init.md` / `cargo-locked-issue.md`).
51	
52	In-chores cross-refs to other chores files (now siblings
53	in the same `chores/` dir) — `[chores-NN.md](chores-NN.md#…)`
54	style — are unchanged.
55	
56	### Why this shape
57	
58	`/notes/chores/` (under notes/) rather than top-level
59	`/chores/` keeps the notes umbrella intact —
60	`todo.md` / `done.md` / `README.md` are conceptually
61	adjacent to chores and benefit from sharing the
62	directory. The `chores-` filename prefix is technically
63	redundant once the files live in `chores/` (could become
64	`01.md` / `02.md` / …), but the rename was rejected to
65	keep the diff focused: section-anchor slugs are
66	filename-independent, so the rename would buy no
67	ref-resolution simplification and would force renumbering
68	of every `[N]:` URL slug in `todo.md`, `done.md`, and the
69	src `.rs` GitHub fragments.
70	
71	## chore: open sb_ide elimination (0.52.0-0)
72	
73	Commits: [[2]]
74	
75	Multi-step. The `0.50.0` cycle landed every subcommand on
76	`SubcommandRunner::dispatch` with two trait peek methods
77	(`suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec`) and one free
78	function (`sb_ide`) carrying per-run "session chrome" —
79	the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner, `finalize::surface_previous_failures`,
80	and the `bm_track` enter/exit pair. This cycle eliminates
81	all three by relocating each behavior to where it actually
82	belongs:
83	
84	- The `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner already lands on `--help` via
85	  clap's `before_help`; printing it again at the top of
86	  every normal run is duplicate chatter the user can
87	  recover from `vc-x1 -V`. Drop the on-every-run banner.
88	- `finalize::surface_previous_failures` is a finalize-side
89	  concern; moving its trigger into `finalize` itself
90	  (rather than into a generic dispatch hook) cleans up the
91	  gate.
92	- `bm_track` enter/exit is a diagnostic; demoting it to
93	  `debug!` matches the "permanent sanity check, but quiet
94	  by default" intent already recorded in its module doc.
95	
96	After these relocations the trait's `suppress_banner` /
97	`is_detached_exec` methods have no remaining consumers,
98	and `sb_ide` has no body left to keep — all three
99	disappear. Plan-only opener; the work lands in the
100	substeps below.
101	
102	### What goes away — and the reasoning
103	
104	- **`bm_track` info-level emission.** Currently emits
105	  `bm-track enter vc-x1 X: app(main)=tracked, …` on every
106	  run. The module doc already notes "a future refinement
107	  would remove the steady-state noise without losing
108	  detection value" — `debug!` accomplishes exactly that.
109	  Detection value is preserved (run with `-v` when
110	  investigating); steady-state output gets cleaner.
111	- **`sb_ide`'s banner-on-every-run.** `clap::Command::before_help`
112	  already shows the banner on `--help`. The on-every-run
113	  emission is duplicate; users who want it explicitly have
114	  `vc-x1 -V`. Removing it shrinks the default output by one
115	  line per command.
116	- **`finalize::surface_previous_failures` gated in `sb_ide`.**
117	  This is finalize machinery — it reads
118	  `.vc-x1/finalize-fail-*` markers and surfaces them. The
119	  "don't surface from the detached child" gate is
120	  finalize's concern, not the trait's; moving the trigger
121	  into `finalize` itself lets the gate live where the
122	  marker logic lives.
123	- **`SubcommandRunner::is_detached_exec`.** With `bm_track`
124	  at `debug!` and `surface_previous_failures` relocated,
125	  no remaining consumer needs the trait's detached-exec
126	  peek. Drop the method; `finalize` reads `params.exec`
127	  directly as a finalize-private concern.
128	- **`SubcommandRunner::suppress_banner`.** With the banner
129	  gone from `sb_ide`, the `-L` flag still suppresses
130	  bookmark labels in the listing subcommands' output
131	  (chid/desc/list/show via `CommonParams::header`) — that
132	  already lives in the op layer, not the chrome layer.
133	  The trait peek has no remaining consumer; drop it,
134	  along with the `suppress_banner` field on `ChidParams`
135	  / `DescParams` / `ListParams` / `ShowParams`.
136	
137	### Ladder
138	
139	- 0.52.0-0 plan + version bump + this section + todo
140	  ladder (current)
141	- 0.52.0-1 `bm_track` → `debug!`; drop the bm_track gates
142	  in `SubcommandRunner::dispatch`
143	- 0.52.0-2..N eliminate `sb_ide`:
144	  - Drop banner-on-every-run from `sb_ide`
145	  - Move `finalize::surface_previous_failures` trigger
146	    into `finalize` itself
147	  - Delete `sb_ide` once both bodies have moved
148	- 0.52.0-K drop `SubcommandRunner::is_detached_exec` and
149	  `SubcommandRunner::suppress_banner` (no remaining
150	  consumers); drop `suppress_banner` fields from the
151	  four `Params` types
152	- 0.52.0 close-out
153	
154	### Per-step evaluation
155	
156	Effectiveness is evaluated after each substep. Possible
157	outcomes at any step boundary:
158	
159	- Continue as planned.
160	- Significantly modify the shape (e.g. keep the banner
161	  somewhere else; preserve `is_detached_exec` for a use
162	  case discovered mid-cycle) — recorded in a new chores
163	  subsection at the next step.
164	- Abandon: revert the cycle to a non-eliminated
165	  baseline, closing as a no-op with an `### Outcome` note
166	  capturing what didn't fit.
167	
168	## refactor: bm_track → debug! (0.52.0-1)
169	
170	Commits: [[3]]
171	
172	First substep. `bm_track` enter/exit lines were
173	emitted at `log::info!`, which lit up on every
174	command run as `bm-track enter vc-x1 X: app(main)=tracked,
175	.claude(main)=tracked` plus its matching exit line.
176	The doc comment recorded the intent to silence the
177	steady-state noise "at which point a 'silent when
178	clean' refinement would remove the noise without
179	losing detection value" — `log::debug!` accomplishes
180	exactly that: default runs go quiet, `-v` brings the
181	signal back when investigating.
182	
183	With the demotion, the `if !is_detached { bm_track(…) }`
184	gates inside `SubcommandRunner::dispatch` are
185	unnecessary — the detached `finalize --exec` child
186	runs at default verbosity, so debug-level output
187	silently no-ops in that path too. The gates collapse;
188	`is_detached_exec` survives the substep purely for
189	`sb_ide`'s remaining banner / `surface_previous_failures`
190	gate, which the next substeps will dismantle.
191	
192	- `Cargo.toml`: `0.52.0-0` → `0.52.0-1`.
193	- `src/main.rs`: `bm_track`'s two `log::info!` calls
194	  → `log::debug!`; module doc-comment reworded
195	  (drop the "future refinement" paragraph, note the
196	  new debug-level emission + why the gate isn't
197	  needed at the call site).
198	- `src/subcommand.rs`: `dispatch` drops the two `if
199	  !is_detached { crate::bm_track(…) }` gates around
200	  enter/exit; the local `let is_detached = …`
201	  binding disappears (its remaining consumer,
202	  `sb_ide`, reads the trait peek inline);
203	  doc-comment updated accordingly.
204	- `notes/chores/chores-11.md`: backfilled
205	  `Commits: [[2]]` on the 0.52.0-0 opener.
206	- `notes/todo.md`: 0.52.0-0 → `(done)`; 0.52.0-1
207	  marked `(current)`.

209	## feat: -V toggles version banner (0.52.0-2)
210	
211	Commits: [[4]]
212	
213	Second substep. `sb_ide` no longer emits the
214	`vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner on every command run, and `-V`
215	becomes a banner-toggle rather than print-and-exit:
216	
217	- `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot` — runs silently (no banner).
218	- `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -V` — prints
219	  `vc-x1 0.52.0-2` as the first line, then runs
220	  `chid` normally.
221	- `vc-x1 -V` — prints `vc-x1 0.52.0-2` and exits
222	  success (no subcommand to run).
223	- `vc-x1` (no flags, no subcommand) — prints help and
224	  exits non-zero (mirroring clap's old
225	  required-subcommand error path).
226	
227	Replacing clap's auto-version means scripts can capture
228	the version *and* the command's output in one
229	invocation. The banner is the uniform `vc-x1 X.Y.Z`
230	regardless of subcommand — the version is the binary's
231	either way, and `propagate_version`'s
232	`vc-x1-<sub> X.Y.Z` form added noise without
233	information.
234	
235	With the banner gone from the on-every-run path,
236	`sb_ide`'s `suppress_banner` parameter loses its only
237	consumer; the `SubcommandRunner::suppress_banner`
238	trait method and the `suppress_banner` field on the
239	four listing `Params` types (`ChidParams`,
240	`DescParams`, `ListParams`, `ShowParams`) cascade out
241	— they have no remaining callers once `sb_ide` stops
242	asking.
243	
244	`-L` / `--no-label` still works — it suppresses the
245	per-repo header label in `chid` / `desc` / `list` /
246	`show` output, a concern that lives in the op layer
247	(`CommonParams::header`) and was always separate from
248	banner suppression. The trait peek was a duplicate
249	spelling of `-L` that survived only as long as
250	`sb_ide` asked for it.
251	
252	`sb_ide` retains one remaining responsibility: gating
253	`finalize::surface_previous_failures` on
254	`!is_detached_exec`. The next substep relocates that
255	trigger into finalize itself, after which `sb_ide`
256	and `SubcommandRunner::is_detached_exec` both
257	disappear.
258	
259	- `Cargo.toml`: `0.52.0-1` → `0.52.0-2`.
260	- `src/main.rs`: `Cli` drops `version,
261	  propagate_version = true` from `#[command(...)]`
262	  and adds a global `pub version: bool` arg (with
263	  `short = 'V'` / `long = "version"`);
264	  `command: Commands` → `command: Option<Commands>`;
265	  `fn main` emits the banner ahead of dispatch when
266	  `cli.version` is set, and handles the no-subcommand
267	  paths (print + exit on `-V`, print help + exit on
268	  no flags). `sb_ide` body drops the banner emission
269	  and its `if !suppress_banner` wrapper; signature
270	  drops the `suppress_banner: bool` parameter; doc
271	  rewritten.
272	- `src/subcommand.rs`: `dispatch` call updated to
273	  `crate::sb_ide(Self::is_detached_exec(&params))`;
274	  trait method `SubcommandRunner::suppress_banner`
275	  removed (no remaining consumer).
276	- `src/chid.rs` / `src/desc.rs` / `src/list.rs` /
277	  `src/show.rs`: `suppress_banner: bool` field on
278	  each `Params` struct removed; `TryFrom` impls drop
279	  the `suppress_banner: a.common.no_label,`
280	  assignment; trait `suppress_banner` overrides
281	  removed; doc-comments updated.
282	- `src/clone.rs` / `src/finalize.rs` / `src/init/tests.rs`:
283	  test helpers updated for the `Option<Commands>`
284	  shape (`match cli.command { Commands::X(a) => … }`
285	  → `Some(Commands::X(a)) => …`).
286	- `notes/chores/chores-11.md`: backfilled

291	## refactor: remove is_detached_exec from trait (0.52.0-3)
292	
293	Commits: [[5]]
294	
295	Third substep. The `SubcommandRunner::is_detached_exec`
296	trait method goes away, taking `sb_ide` with it. The
297	detached-exec gate is finalize-specific machinery —
298	`finalize::surface_previous_failures` is the only
299	behavior it ever protected, and `FinalizeArgs.exec` is
300	the only field that ever set it. Lifting the gate into
301	`main` (one `matches!` against `Commands::Finalize`
302	with `args.exec`) cuts the trait peek + its sole
303	override + the free function that consumed it.
304	
305	After this substep:
306	
307	- The `SubcommandRunner` trait surface is the
308	  load-bearing minimum: `to_params`, `run`, and the
309	  default `dispatch`. No more peek methods.
310	- `main` owns the surface-previous-failures call,
311	  with the exec-child skip inline at the call site.
312	  Single home for the gate; no indirection through
313	  `sb_ide` or the trait.
314	- `dispatch` shrinks to "build params, bracket with
315	  `bm_track`, run, map exit code." No session-chrome
316	  responsibility at all.
317	
318	The cycle's stated goal — eliminate `sb_ide` and both
319	trait peeks — is met after this substep. Close-out
320	lands separately as `0.52.0` (todo→done, no code).
321	
322	- `Cargo.toml`: `0.52.0-2` → `0.52.0-3`.
323	- `src/subcommand.rs`: `SubcommandRunner::is_detached_exec`
324	  removed; `dispatch` drops its `crate::sb_ide(…)`
325	  call; doc rewritten.
326	- `src/finalize.rs`: `is_detached_exec` override on
327	  `FinalizeArgs` removed.
328	- `src/main.rs`: `pub fn sb_ide` deleted (no
329	  callers); `main` adds an inline
330	  `matches!(&cmd, Commands::Finalize(args) if args.exec)`
331	  peek and calls `finalize::surface_previous_failures`
332	  before loading `Context`.
333	- `notes/chores/chores-11.md`: backfilled
334	  `Commits: [[4]]` on the 0.52.0-2 section; new
335	  0.52.0-3 section.
336	- `notes/todo.md`: 0.52.0-2 → `(done)`; 0.52.0-3
337	  marked `(current)`.
338	
339	## chore: close sb_ide elimination (0.52.0)
340	
341	Commits: [[6]]
342	
343	Cycle close-out. The `0.50.0` Subcommand trait sweep
344	left three pieces of per-run "session chrome" hanging
345	off `SubcommandRunner` — `sb_ide` (free function) and
346	the trait peek methods `suppress_banner` /
347	`is_detached_exec`. All three are gone after this
348	cycle:
349	
350	- The on-every-run `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner is replaced by
351	  an opt-in `-V` flag that prints the banner as the
352	  first line and continues (rather than clap's
353	  exit-on-print default). Scripts can capture version
354	  *and* output in one invocation.
355	- `bm_track` enter/exit emit at `log::debug!` —
356	  default runs stay quiet, the signal is available
357	  under `-v`, and the detached `finalize --exec`
358	  child stays silent without needing a per-call gate.
359	- `finalize::surface_previous_failures` moved to a
360	  single inline call in `main` with an exec-child
361	  skip; `sb_ide` had no body left to keep and is
362	  deleted; the `is_detached_exec` trait peek (its
363	  only consumer) goes with it.
364	- `SubcommandRunner::suppress_banner` and the
365	  `suppress_banner: bool` field on the four listing
366	  `Params` types cascaded out during 0.52.0-2 when
367	  the banner emission left.
368	
369	`SubcommandRunner`'s trait surface is now the
370	load-bearing minimum: `to_params`, `run`, and the
371	default `dispatch` (which builds params, brackets
372	`bm_track` enter/exit, runs, maps exit code). No
373	peek methods, no chrome responsibility.
374	
375	### As-built ladder
376	
377	- 0.52.0-0 plan + version bump + chores-11 opener
378	  section + todo ladder
379	- 0.52.0-1 `bm_track` → `debug!`; drop bm_track
380	  gates in `dispatch`
381	- 0.52.0-2 `-V` toggles version banner (replaces
382	  clap's auto-version); banner-on-every-run gone;
383	  `suppress_banner` trait method + `Params` fields
384	  cascade out
385	- 0.52.0-3 remove `is_detached_exec` from trait;
386	  gate moves to `main` inline; `sb_ide` deleted
387	- 0.52.0 close-out
388	
389	### Outcome
390	
391	Three subtractive substeps after the opener; no
392	evaluation-gate detours. The cycle's stated goal — a
393	trait whose surface only carries what every
394	subcommand actually needs — landed cleanly. The
395	`-V`-toggles-banner shape (instead of the original
396	"drop banner entirely" sketch) emerged mid-cycle when
397	the user pointed out that opting into the version on
398	demand is more useful than removing the signal
399	altogether.
400	
401	While we were at it, the broader
402	`surface_previous_failures` design gaps (stale-forever
403	markers, concurrent surfacing double-print, mid-write
404	torn reads, no notify-at-failure path) got captured
405	as a new `## Bugs` section in `notes/todo.md`. The
406	exec-child gate covers one race; the rest is queued.
407

409	  dropped — cycle close marker).
410	- `notes/chores/chores-11.md`: backfilled

472	## refactor: scope CLI cleanup (0.54.0-0)
473	
474	Commits: [[8]]
475	
476	Multi-step. Today, two `--scope` flags coexist with
477	different value grammars: query-side `-s code|bot|code,bot`
478	(which sides of an existing workspace) and creation-side
479	`-s code,bot|por` (workspace topology). The cleanup factors
480	the three concerns apart onto three orthogonal flags:
481	
482	- **Path** — `-R/--repo PATH`. Always present (defaults to
483	  cwd). Independent of role selection.
484	- **Roles** — `-s/--scope code|bot|code,bot|bot,code`.
485	  Single-purpose: which side(s) of an existing dual
486	  workspace.
487	- **Topology** — `--por` boolean. Creation-only (init,
488	  clone). The `code,bot|por` grammar collapses to "do
489	  you want a `.claude` companion or not."
490	
491	After this cycle:
492	
493	- `Scope::Single(PathBuf)` — gone. `-R PATH` is the
494	  path-only entry point.
495	- `parse_scope_roles` — gone. Its job (reject path forms
496	  in the roles slot) is moot once paths aren't a roles
497	  form.
498	- `options_flags/scope.rs` (`ScopeKind`, `ScopeOption`,
499	  `parse_scope_kind`, `ScopeParser`) — deleted. A new
500	  `PorFlag` boolean leaf in `options_flags/` takes over
501	  the topology-selection job.
502	- `src/scope.rs` — relocated into
503	  `options_flags/scope.rs` (no longer ambiguous with the
504	  deleted file). `Scope` becomes a newtype
505	  `pub struct Scope(pub Vec<Side>)`; the `Single` variant
506	  goes away.
507	- `vc-x1 sync` gains `-R/--repo` for single-repo or
508	  workspace-root override, parallel to `CommonArgs`'s
509	  `-R`. Preserves today's `sync --scope <path>`
510	  capability after path-in-scope drops.
511	
512	The design was captured during `0.53.0`'s opener (committed
513	under the working title `refactor: clean up scope + todo
514	list` then re-scoped) and carries forward here. The deeper
515	symmetric-`.vc-config.toml` refactor (todo #11) is
516	independent and lands in a later cycle.
517	
518	### Ladder
519	
520	- 0.54.0-0 plan + version bump + chores opener (current)
521	- 0.54.0-1 add new `--por` boolean flag leaf in
522	  `options_flags/`; delete `options_flags/scope.rs`
523	  (`ScopeKind`, `ScopeOption`, etc.); migrate init / clone
524	  / test_helpers
525	- 0.54.0-2 collapse `Scope` to roles-only (newtype over
526	  `Vec<Side>`); relocate `src/scope.rs` into
527	  `options_flags/scope.rs`; drop `parse_scope_roles`;
528	  add `-R/--repo` to sync
529	- 0.54.0-3 docs/script sweep — README, ARCHITECTURE,
530	  substep-test.sh, notes references to the old
531	  `--scope code,bot|por` form; delete
532	  `notes/fix-todo.py`
533	- 0.54.0 close-out
534	
535	### Per-step evaluation
536	
537	Effectiveness is evaluated after each substep. Possible
538	outcomes at any step boundary:
539	
540	- Continue as planned.
541	- Significantly modify the shape — recorded in a new
542	  chores subsection at the next step.
543	- Abandon: revert the cycle to a no-op with an
544	  `### Outcome` note capturing what didn't fit.

546	## refactor: add --por flag leaf (0.54.0-1)
547	
548	Commits: [[9]]
549	
550	First substep. The creation-side topology question — "dual
551	workspace or single repo?" — was previously a value of
552	`--scope` (`code,bot|por`) parsed into a `ScopeKind` enum.
553	It's more naturally a boolean: `--por` or not. The substep
554	introduces a `PorFlag` boolean leaf in `options_flags/por.rs`
555	and migrates `init` / `clone` / `test_helpers` to consume it;
556	the old `options_flags/scope.rs` (`ScopeKind`, `ScopeOption`,
557	`parse_scope_kind`, `ScopeParser`) is deleted.
558	
559	- `PorFlag` is the first leaf written to the `value`-field
560	  convention from
561	  [options_flags/README.md](README.md#adding-a-new-leaf):
562	  the inner field is `value`, the flag is declared via
563	  `#[arg(long = "por")]`, and consumers read `args.por.value`
564	  rather than the doubled `args.por.por`. Existing
565	  single-field leaves (`dry_run`, `private`, `account`,
566	  `config`, `use_template`, `repo`) predate this and are
567	  tracked for migration in `notes/todo.md > #2`.
568	- Consumer dispatch collapses from a two-arm `match scope
569	  { CodeBot / Por }` to a single boolean check. Both the
570	  dry-run print path and the real dispatch path in `init`
571	  / `clone` shrink; the error messages follow the same
572	  rename (`--scope=por` → `--por`,
573	  `--config is only valid with --scope=por` →
574	  `--config is only valid with --por`).
575	- Tests prune the obsolete `--scope=*` / `-s *` parses —
576	  no value space left for `--scope` to error on. Net unit
577	  test count drops 10: 5 clone `scope_*` tests + 1 init
578	  `scope_short_flag_por` + 6 `options_flags::scope_kind_*`
579	  tests deleted; 2 new `por_flag*` smoke tests added.
580	  366 unit + 14 integration tests pass.

===options_flags/README===
# options_flags — Reusable CLI Options and Flags

Per-flag CLI surfaces shared across multiple subcommands. Each
shared option or flag (OF) lives in its own submodule so its
type, parser, and tests stay together.

This module is the CLI-layer leaf store for the options_flags
extraction in [`../../ARCHITECTURE.md`](../../ARCHITECTURE.md);
see there for how the leaves fit the wider clap-args /
`Context`+`Params` split.

## Architecture

Three composition patterns:

- **Leaf** — a `#[derive(Args)]` struct holding the flag(s),
  `value_parser`, default, and help text (via the field
  doc-comment). One `#[derive(Args)]` per logical OF — single
  flag or a small fixed pair (e.g. `push_retry`'s two fields).
  Help text aims to be generic enough for any reasonable
  consumer.
- **Bundle** — a `#[derive(Args)]` shared by N subcommands,
  picked up with one `#[command(flatten)]` line. Two forms:
  - *flatten-of-leaves* — composes existing leaves into a named
    role (e.g. `ProvisionOptionFlagBundle` = `dry_run` +
    `private` + `push_retry`). Use this when the constituent
    flags are themselves reused elsewhere.
  - *inline-fields* — holds the `#[arg]` fields directly (e.g.
    `common_args::CommonArgs` — the read-only commit-query arg
    set). Use this when a whole *set* of args is shared but the
    individual flags aren't reused outside the bundle —
    extracting per-flag leaves would buy no reuse. If one of the
    fields ever *is* reused (a future command wants just that
    flag), extract it into a leaf then and
    `#[command(flatten)]` it into the bundle.
- **Pattern A escape hatch** — when a consumer needs unique help
  text (or different defaults), it skips the leaf's flatten and
  inlines its own `#[arg(value_parser = …)]` field, reusing the
  leaf's typed value and parser.

> DEVELOPER

dry-run is a good idea but some of the other --dry-run's are quite superficial and I'm not so sure useful. But with this I think it should output the associated numbered line to stdout so the user can actaully see the result. Thoughts?

> AGENT

Agreed — a dry-run that just narrates "would renumber entry 6 → 5" is low-value chatter. The useful thing is to show the **actual corrected line**, so the dry-run output *is* the result, not a description of it.

Here's what I'd propose for `vc-x1 fix-todo`:

**Dry-run (default):** for every entry that would change, print its **corrected numbered line** to stdout — the exact line that `--no-dry-run` would write — with a terse annotation of *what* changed:

```
$ vc-x1 fix-todo
## Todo
1. **`vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo` subcommands.** ...   [was 2]
2. **single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.**  [was 3]
...
9. **Symmetric `.vc-config.toml` schema.** Add `code = "/"`  [was 10, indent 4→3]
...

## Bugs
(no changes)

42 entries renumbered, 1 indent fix — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply
```

- The line itself is the real output; `[was N]` / `[indent 4→3]` is the diff context.
- Only **changed** entries print — clean signal. (After deleting a `## Todo` item, that's most of the list, which is fine — that *is* the result.)
- An indent-only change still prints its numbered line as the anchor, tagged `[indent 4→3]`.
- Nothing to do → one line: `fix-todo: notes/todo.md already normalized`.

**`--no-dry-run`:** applies the rewrite in place, prints the same summary.

A couple of points to settle:

1. **Continuation lines.** When a prefix width changes (`10.`→`9.`), continuation lines reindent too. I'd show that as the `[indent 4→3]` tag on the entry's numbered line rather than echoing every continuation line — the numbered line stays the user-facing anchor. Want the continuation lines themselves echoed too, or is the tag enough?

2. **Print all vs. changed-only.** I lean changed-only. If you'd rather always see the full corrected `## Todo` + `## Bugs` block (unchanged lines included) for a complete picture, say so.

Your broader point about superficial dry-runs is fair — `fix-desc` / `clone` / `init` could use the same "show the real result" treatment — but I'd keep that out of this cycle's scope.

Does this output shape match what you had in mind?

Plan approved in shape — here's the full cycle for your sign-off.

## Plan: `validate-todo` / `fix-todo` subcommands

**Approach:** Multi-step, `0.54.0 → 0.55.0`. Parallel to the `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` pair — two subcommand modules over a shared `todo_helpers.rs`, both born in the `(ctx, params)` + `SubcommandRunner` shape.

### Design

- **`src/todo_helpers.rs`** — shared core. Parses `notes/todo.md`, walks `## Todo` / `## Bugs`, identifies entry first-lines via a hand-rolled `^\d+\. ` match at column 0 (no `regex` dep). Produces a `Vec<Change>` — per entry: section, old/new number, old/new continuation-indent base, corrected first line. `const TODO_FILE = "notes/todo.md"`.
- **Continuation indent** — normalize against each entry's *measured* base indent (the minimum non-blank continuation indent), shifting the whole block so base = prefix width. Preserves nested `- ` sub-bullets (e.g. `## Bugs` entry #1's indent-5 lines). Reported via the `[indent 4→3]` tag only.
- **`src/validate_todo.rs`** — `vc-x1 validate-todo [FILE]` (default `notes/todo.md`). Read-only; reports each mis-numbered / mis-indented entry, exits non-zero if any, one-line OK summary otherwise.
- **`src/fix_todo.rs`** — `vc-x1 fix-todo [FILE]`. Dry-run default: prints changed-only corrected numbered lines + tags. `--no-dry-run` writes in place.
- `main.rs`: two `mod` decls + helper, two `Commands` variants, two dispatch arms.
- Tests: unit tests in `todo_helpers.rs` (renumber, indent shift, idempotency, nested-bullet preservation) + per-module smoke tests.

### Ladder

- **0.55.0-0** — plan + version bump; chores-11 opener section + `todo.md` In Progress ladder; **delete `## Todo` item #1** and renumber (via `fix-todo.py`, still present); backfill `0.54.0` chores `Commits:` ref.
- **0.55.0-1** — `feat: add validate-todo subcommand` — `todo_helpers.rs` (`analyze`) + `validate_todo.rs` + wiring + tests.
- **0.55.0-2** — `feat: add fix-todo subcommand` — `fix_todo.rs` + helper render path + wiring + tests; **delete `notes/fix-todo.py`**; docs: root `README.md` (usage + sections), `ARCHITECTURE.md` module map, `todo.md` intro reference, and fix the stale `notes/README.md > ## Todo format` ("lazy numbering" → manual sequential, which 0.53.0 actually changed).
- **0.55.0** — close-out: `todo.md` → `## Done`, chores close-out section.

### Notes
- `[FILE]` defaults to `notes/todo.md` cwd-relative (run from project root, per CLAUDE.md).
- `validate-todo` / `fix-todo` are new subcommands born ported — no refactor-tracking row needed.

If this looks right, I'll start with **0.55.0-0**: bump `Cargo.toml` to `0.55.0-0`, write the chores opener, set up the `todo.md` ladder, delete item #1, renumber. I'll pause for review before proposing that commit.

> DEVELOPER

Thinking about it `vc-x1 validate-todo` is exactly `vc-x1 fix-todo --dry-run`, maybe?

> AGENT

Good catch — and yes, I think you're right. Let me push that further.

`validate-desc` / `fix-desc` earn being separate because `fix-desc` does **much more** than `validate-desc` — `--add-missing`, `--fallback`, `--title`, resolving change IDs from the other repo. The validate/fix split there reflects a real difference in capability.

`fix-todo` has none of that. Renumber + reindent is **one computation**. "Validate" and "fix" would run the identical analysis — the only difference is whether you write the result and what exit code you return. A separate `validate-todo` is then pure boilerplate: a second module, `Commands` variant, dispatch arm, help page, all for zero behavioral gain.

So I'd recommend **collapsing to one subcommand** and dropping `validate-todo`:

- **`vc-x1 fix-todo`** — dry-run (default). Prints changed-only corrected lines + tags. **Exits non-zero if the file isn't already normalized**, zero if it is. This *is* the validator.
- **`vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run`** — writes in place, exits zero on success.

The exit-non-zero-on-diff is what makes the dry-run double as a CI gate — `vc-x1 fix-todo && echo clean`. That's a well-worn idiom (`cargo fmt --check`, `git diff --exit-code`), and this project already folds check+apply into one subcommand with `vc-x1 sync --check` / `--no-check`. It also makes the dry-run genuinely load-bearing rather than superficial — exactly the principle from the last exchange.

The reusable check for a future `validate-repo` (todo #29) is the `todo_helpers` **library function**, not a CLI subcommand — so collapsing costs nothing there.

One deliberate divergence to note: `fix-desc`'s dry-run exits 0; `fix-todo`'s exits non-zero on diff. Justified — there's no `validate-todo` to carry the gate, so the dry-run carries it. I'll record that in the chores section. I'll also revise todo item #1's "parallel shape to validate-desc / fix-desc" wording, since it's the item I'm picking up.

**This also argues for single-step.** With one subcommand, the cycle is a focused change — `todo_helpers.rs` + `fix_todo.rs` + wiring + tests + docs + delete `fix-todo.py`. I'd switch from the multi-step ladder to **single-step `0.55.0`** (one commit; it still deletes `## Todo` item #1 + renumbers, as the version-bump commit must).

Revised plan:

- **`0.55.0`** (single commit): bump `Cargo.toml`; `src/todo_helpers.rs` (parse + `Vec<Change>` + new-content render) + `src/fix_todo.rs` (`FixTodoArgs`/`Params`/`SubcommandRunner`); `main.rs` wiring; unit tests; delete `notes/fix-todo.py`; docs (root `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` module map, `todo.md` intro reference, fix the stale `notes/README.md > ## Todo format`); delete `todo.md` item #1, renumber, move to `## Done`; chores-11 close-out section + backfill `0.54.0`'s `Commits:` ref.

Shall I go with the collapsed single-subcommand, single-step design?

> DEVELOPER

Either way, but for command orthognality I think we should still have validate-todo and fix-todo and if this is single-pass like the script there is a state-machine so it's not trivial, so your call.

> AGENT

Decision: **keep `validate-todo` and `fix-todo` separate.** You're right on orthogonality — the `validate-*` / `fix-*` family stays consistent across the CLI (and fits a future `validate-repo` cleanly), and a named `validate-todo` reads as intent. The single-pass state machine you mention (section / entry-num / indent-shift carried across the line walk) lives **once** in `todo_helpers.rs`; the two subcommands are thin presenters over it. So splitting the *commands* doesn't split the *logic* — the duplication is just a small clap `Args` struct + dispatch arm each.

That puts us back at the original shape. Final plan, locked:

## Plan — `validate-todo` / `fix-todo` (multi-step, `0.54.0 → 0.55.0`)

**Design**
- `src/todo_helpers.rs` — shared core. Single-pass walk of `notes/todo.md`; tracks section / entry number / continuation block; hand-rolled `^\d+\. ` match (no `regex` dep). Produces `Vec<Change>` (per entry: section, old/new number, old/new indent base, corrected first line). Continuation indent normalized against each entry's *measured* base — preserves nested `- ` sub-bullets.
- `src/validate_todo.rs` — `vc-x1 validate-todo [FILE]`, read-only, reports mis-numbered / mis-indented entries, exits non-zero on issues.
- `src/fix_todo.rs` — `vc-x1 fix-todo [FILE]`, dry-run default (prints changed-only corrected lines + `[was N]` / `[indent 4→3]` tags, exits 0), `--no-dry-run` writes.
- Both born in `(ctx, params)` + `SubcommandRunner` shape; `[FILE]` defaults to `notes/todo.md`.

**Ladder**
- **0.55.0-0** — version bump; chores-11 opener + `todo.md` In Progress ladder; delete `## Todo` item #1 + renumber (via `fix-todo.py`, still present); backfill `0.54.0`'s `Commits:` ref.
- **0.55.0-1** — `feat: add validate-todo subcommand` — `todo_helpers.rs` (`analyze`) + `validate_todo.rs` + wiring + unit tests.
- **0.55.0-2** — `feat: add fix-todo subcommand` — `fix_todo.rs` + helper render path + wiring + tests; delete `notes/fix-todo.py`; docs (root `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` module map, `todo.md` intro reference, fix stale `notes/README.md > ## Todo format`).
- **0.55.0** — close-out: `todo.md` → `## Done`, chores close-out section.

I'll start with **0.55.0-0**: bump `Cargo.toml` to `0.55.0-0`, write the chores-11 opener section, set up the `todo.md` In Progress ladder, delete item #1, renumber, backfill the `0.54.0` `Commits:` ref. I'll pause for review before proposing the commit.

> AGENT

Looking at your tweak in `## In Progress`:

```
[#1](link-to-#1) // bot fix this so it's a link to #1 below
```

I hit a snag making this a working link — two things:

1. **There's no item #1 to link to.** `0.55.0-0` deleted `## Todo` item #1 (the delete-on-pickup rule — and CLAUDE.md calls a `## Todo` entry duplicating `## In Progress` work a "process bug"). `## Todo` now starts at `2.`. The current first item is "single-field `options_flags` leaves" — unrelated to this cycle.

2. **Markdown can't anchor-link to a list item anyway.** GitHub auto-generates anchors only for headings (`## Todo` → `#todo`), not for `1.`/`2.` list entries. A `[text](#1)`-style link to a numbered entry needs an explicit HTML anchor manually placed at that entry.

So `[#1]` has no valid target as written. What did you intend? My read of the options:

- **Link to this cycle's design** — replace the line with a link to the chores-11 opener section (`[validate-todo / fix-todo](chores/chores-11.md#feat-open-validate-todo--fix-todo-0550-0)`). This is the meaningful "what is this In Progress work" pointer. *(My recommendation.)*
- **Drop the line** — the ladder bullets already name the cycle (`0.55.0-0 … chores opener`); In Progress doesn't need a separate header link.
- **Keep item #1 in `## Todo`** and link the In Progress section to it — but that re-introduces the Todo/In-Progress duplication CLAUDE.md flags as a process bug.

Which way do you want it — or did you mean something else by "#1 below"?

> DEVELOPER

It's the first item in the todo list "**`vc-x1 fix-to` / `validate-todo` ..."

> DEVELOPER

It's the first item in the todo list "1. **`vc-x1 fix-to` / `validate-todo` ..."

> AGENT

The item you're pointing at — `1. **`vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo` subcommands.**` — is exactly the one `0.55.0-0` **deleted** from `## Todo` a few steps ago (the delete-on-pickup rule). It's not in the file anymore, so `[#1]` has nothing to resolve to.

So this is a workflow fork, and I'd rather you pick than guess:

- **Keep item #1 in `## Todo`** — the task description stays as a Todo entry; `## In Progress` holds the ladder + a link to it. This *reverses* CLAUDE.md's current rule, which says delete the Todo entry on pickup and explicitly calls a `## Todo` entry duplicating `## In Progress` work a "process bug." If you want this, I'll restore item #1, wire the link, and update that CLAUDE.md rule to the keep-and-link model.
- **Leave it deleted** — `## In Progress` stays the sole record; the `[#1]` line instead links to this cycle's chores design section, or gets dropped.

On link mechanics (either way): markdown auto-anchors only headings, so the working form is `[#1](#todo)` — it lands on the `## Todo` heading, and since #1 is the first entry it's right there. A precise per-item link would need a hand-placed `<a id="todo-1">` anchor.

> AGENT

I read the current file — line 44 is now `### 1. **`vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo` subcommands.** Parallel`.

It half-works. `###` does generate a GitHub anchor, so a link *can* resolve — but as written it breaks in three ways:

1. **The heading is just line 44.** `### ` makes the whole physical line the heading, so the heading text ends `…subcommands.** Parallel` — cut off mid-sentence — and lines 45–50 become an orphaned indented block under it.
2. **`1.` is still in the heading.** `### 1. …` doesn't match the renumber regex `^\d+\. ` (starts with `#`), so `fix-todo` won't treat it as entry 1 — it'll renumber the `2.` entry to `1.`, and you get two "#1"s.
3. **A heading inside the numbered list breaks the section contract.** `## Todo` is defined as a uniform manually-numbered list — and that list is exactly what this cycle's `validate-todo`/`fix-todo` operates on. A `###` item mixed in is a special case the tooling and every reader now has to handle.

So `###` buys an anchor at the cost of the list model. My recommendation instead:

- **Drop the `### `** — restore item #1 as a plain `1.` entry, so `## Todo` stays a uniform `1.`…`44.` list.
- In `## In Progress`, make the link **`[#1](#todo)`** — `#todo` is the auto-anchor for the `## Todo` heading; since #1 is the first entry, the link lands right on it. No HTML hacks, list stays uniform, renumber tooling stays simple.

If you specifically need the link to land *exactly* on item 1's line rather than the section top, the only clean way is to pull item 1 out of the numbered list entirely into a proper standalone `### `vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo` subcommands` heading + paragraph — but that takes the active task out of the list, and I'd want to think through close-out and the tooling for it.

Either way I'll update CLAUDE.md's delete-on-pickup rule to the keep-#1-and-link model.

Which do you want — plain `1.` + `[#1](#todo)`, or the standalone-heading restructure?

Yes. Any `###` heading gets an auto-generated GitHub anchor, so it's a valid link target.

For that exact line, the rendered heading text is `1. vc-x1 fix-todo / validate-todo subcommands. Parallel`, which slugs to:

```
1-vc-x1-fix-todo--validate-todo-subcommands-parallel
```

(lowercase; `.` and `/` dropped; spaces → hyphens; the `--` is the ` / ` with the slash removed). So the link from `## In Progress` would be:

```
[#1](#1-vc-x1-fix-todo--validate-todo-subcommands-parallel)
```

That resolves and works. Two caveats on *that specific line*, though:

- The anchor carries `parallel` and a `--`, because `### ` makes the **whole physical line** the heading — including the trailing word "Parallel" — and keeps the leading `1.`. Ugly but valid.
- It still collides with renumbering: `### 1. …` isn't seen as entry 1 by `fix-todo`, so the `2.` entry below renumbers to `1.` → two "#1"s.

If you want a clean anchor, trimming the heading to just `### `vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo` subcommands` gives `#vc-x1-fix-todo--validate-todo-subcommands`. Want me to go ahead and wire it up — and if so, with the line as-is or trimmed?

258	
259	### Versioning
260	
261	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based
262	on scope:
263	
264	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump
265	  directly to `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
266	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`,
267	  implement across multiple commits incrementing the numeric
268	  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix.
269	
270	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before
271	starting.
272	
273	A plan that picks up a `## Todo` item — or *re-scopes* an
274	existing `## In Progress` ladder to absorb one — **deletes that
275	`## Todo` entry in the same commit** (the version-bump / plan
276	commit). `## In Progress` is the sole record of that work until
277	close-out, when it moves to `## Done`; a cycle that merges
278	several entangled `## Todo` items deletes all of them. A `## Todo`
279	entry that duplicates current `## In Progress` work is a process
280	bug.
281	
282	For multi-step:
283	
284	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-0` with the plan and commit as a chore
285	   marker.
286	2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
287	   needed).
288	3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix), updates
289	   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores/chores-*.md` — this is the "done"
290	   marker.
291	
292	Multi-step cycles surface the ladder at the top of
293	`notes/todo.md > ## In Progress` as a bullet list with `(done)` /
294	`(current)` markers — see the file's intro paragraph for the
295	format. When starting a new step, the *first* edit is to mark
296	that step `(current)` in `notes/todo.md` — before any code/doc
297	work — so the In Progress view reflects what's actually
298	happening. The flip back to `(done)` is part of the pre-commit
299	checklist (item 6).
300	
301	**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
302	semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
303	component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
304	`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the dev ladder
305	below the done marker. Cargo accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix
306	adds no information the git log doesn't already convey and
307	doubles typing per commit.
308	
309	The final release commit (no suffix) signals completion rather than
```

436	[Versioning](#versioning).
437	
438	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
439	
440	1. `cargo fmt`
441	2. `cargo clippy`
442	3. `cargo test`
443	4. `cargo install --path . --locked` (if applicable) — `--locked`
444	   is required: without it, `cargo install` ignores `Cargo.lock`
445	   and re-resolves from scratch, which can pick incompatible
446	   versions even when `cargo build` / `cargo test` succeed.
447	5. Retest after install
448	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — for multi-step cycles, flip the
449	   just-completed step's marker from `(current)` to `(done)`
450	   **before** running `vc-x1 push`. The commit being pushed
451	   should reflect the new state. (The next step's `(current)`
452	   marker is set later, at the *start* of that step — see the
453	   Versioning multi-step section.)
454	7. Update `notes/todo.md` — at cycle close-out (final commit),
455	   move the entry from `## In Progress` to `## Done`.
456	8. Update `notes/chores/chores-*.md` — add a section (header =
457	   provisional commit title; intro paragraph + any `###` design
458	   subsections; **no** per-file edit list — that's the commit
459	   body).
460	9. Sync the chores section header to the **final** commit title
461	   and update every markdown anchor back-reference to it.
462	10. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new
463	    flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
464	
465	## Sub-step Workflow
```

`## In Progress` ladder via the stable `#current-in-progress`
anchor.

  cycle; `### Current In Progress` heading above item 1 with
  the `[Current in progress]` link from the ladder.
