{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/todo_helpers.rs",
  "content": "//! Shared core for the `validate-todo` / `fix-todo` subcommands:\n//! parse `notes/todo.md`'s numbered sections and compute the\n//! renumber / re-indent plan.\n//!\n//! - `## Todo` and `## Bugs` hold manually-numbered entries\n//!   (`1.` `2.` … at column 0); every other section is skipped.\n//! - `analyze` walks the file once and returns one `Change` per\n//!   entry whose number or continuation indent is off.\n//! - Continuation indent is normalized against each entry's\n//!   *measured* base indent, so nested `- ` sub-bullets keep\n//!   their relative depth.\n\n/// Default todo file, relative to the workspace root.\npub const TODO_FILE: &str = \"notes/todo.md\";\n\n/// A manually-numbered section of the todo file.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]\npub enum Section {\n    /// The `## Todo` backlog list.\n    Todo,\n    /// The `## Bugs` list.\n    Bugs,\n}\n\nimpl Section {\n    /// The section's markdown header text (`## Todo` / `## Bugs`).\n    pub fn header(self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            Section::Todo => \"## Todo\",\n            Section::Bugs => \"## Bugs\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// One entry whose number and/or continuation indent needs to\n/// change for the section to be sequentially numbered.\n///\n/// - `num_old` / `num_new` — the displayed number and its\n///   sequential (1-based) position; equal when only the indent is\n///   off.\n/// - `indent_old` / `indent_new` — the measured continuation-base\n///   indent and the expected one (the number prefix's width);\n///   both `None` when the entry has no continuation lines.\npub struct Change {\n    pub section: Section,\n    pub line_no: usize,\n    pub first_line: String,\n    pub num_old: usize,\n    pub num_new: usize,\n    pub indent_old: Option<usize>,\n    pub indent_new: Option<usize>,\n}\n\n/// Result of scanning a todo file: per-section entry counts and\n/// the list of entries needing a fix.\npub struct Analysis {\n    pub todo_count: usize,\n    pub bugs_count: usize,\n    pub changes: Vec<Change>,\n}\n\n/// An entry being accumulated during the `analyze` walk — its\n/// first line plus the indents of its continuation lines.\nstruct PendingEntry {\n    section: Section,\n    line_no: usize,\n    first_line: String,\n    num_old: usize,\n    num_new: usize,\n    cont_indents: Vec<usize>,\n}\n\n/// Number of decimal digits in `n` (entry numbers are ≥ 1).\nfn digit_count(n: usize) -> usize {\n    n.to_string().len()\n}\n\n/// Classify a line as a `## ` section header.\n///\n/// - `None` — not a level-2 header (covers `# `, `### `, body\n///   text).\n/// - `Some(None)` — a `## ` header that isn't `## Todo` /\n///   `## Bugs` (e.g. `## In Progress`, `## Done`).\n/// - `Some(Some(section))` — the `## Todo` or `## Bugs` header.\nfn section_header(line: &str) -> Option<Option<Section>> {\n    if !line.starts_with(\"## \") {\n        return None;\n    }\n    Some(match line.trim_end() {\n        \"## Todo\" => Some(Section::Todo),\n        \"## Bugs\" => Some(Section::Bugs),\n        _ => None,\n    })\n}\n\n/// Parse a leading `N. ` entry prefix at column 0.\n///\n/// Returns the entry number when `line` begins with one or more\n/// ASCII digits followed by `\". \"` — the renumber convention's\n/// `^\\d+\\. ` anchor. Returns `None` for intro lines (which carry\n/// a leading space), continuation lines, and headings.\nfn parse_entry_prefix(line: &str) -> Option<usize> {\n    let bytes = line.as_bytes();\n    let mut i = 0;\n    while i < bytes.len() && bytes[i].is_ascii_digit() {\n        i += 1;\n    }\n    if i == 0 || bytes.get(i) != Some(&b'.') || bytes.get(i + 1) != Some(&b' ') {\n        return None;\n    }\n    line[..i].parse().ok()\n}\n\n/// Finalize a pending entry: measure its continuation-indent base\n/// and, when the number or indent needs to change, push a\n/// `Change`.\nfn finish_entry(p: PendingEntry, changes: &mut Vec<Change>) {\n    let prefix_width = digit_count(p.num_new) + 2; // \". \" is two chars\n    let indent_old = p.cont_indents.iter().copied().min();\n    let indent_new = indent_old.map(|_| prefix_width);\n    if p.num_old != p.num_new || indent_old != indent_new {\n        changes.push(Change {\n            section: p.section,\n            line_no: p.line_no,\n            first_line: p.first_line,\n            num_old: p.num_old,\n            num_new: p.num_new,\n            indent_old,\n            indent_new,\n        });\n    }\n}\n\n/// Scan a todo file's `## Todo` and `## Bugs` sections and report\n/// every entry whose number or continuation indent needs to\n/// change.\n///\n/// - Walks lines once, tracking the current `## ` section and the\n///   entry being accumulated.\n/// - An entry first-line is `N. ` at column 0; expected numbering\n///   is `1..N` in document order.\n/// - Any heading (`#`, `###`, …) ends the current entry's\n///   continuation block; `## Todo` / `## Bugs` headers also reset\n///   the section.\n/// - An entry's continuation indent is reported against the\n///   *measured* base (the minimum non-blank continuation indent),\n///   so nested sub-bullets keep their relative depth.\npub fn analyze(content: &str) -> Analysis {\n    let mut changes = Vec::new();\n    let mut todo_count = 0;\n    let mut bugs_count = 0;\n    let mut section: Option<Section> = None;\n    let mut entry_idx = 0usize;\n    let mut pending: Option<PendingEntry> = None;\n\n    for (i, line) in content.lines().enumerate() {\n        if let Some(kind) = section_header(line) {\n            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {\n                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);\n            }\n            section = kind;\n            entry_idx = 0;\n            continue;\n        }\n        // Any other heading (`#`, `###`, …) ends the current\n        // entry's continuation block without changing the section.\n        if line.starts_with('#') {\n            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {\n                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);\n            }\n            continue;\n        }\n        let Some(sec) = section else {\n            continue;\n        };\n        if let Some(num) = parse_entry_prefix(line) {\n            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {\n                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);\n            }\n            entry_idx += 1;\n            match sec {\n                Section::Todo => todo_count += 1,\n                Section::Bugs => bugs_count += 1,\n            }\n            pending = Some(PendingEntry {\n                section: sec,\n                line_no: i + 1,\n                first_line: line.to_string(),\n                num_old: num,\n                num_new: entry_idx,\n                cont_indents: Vec::new(),\n            });\n        } else if let Some(p) = pending.as_mut() {\n            // Continuation line: record the leading-space count of\n            // non-blank lines; blank lines carry no indent.\n            if !line.trim().is_empty() {\n                let indent = line.len() - line.trim_start_matches(' ').len();\n                p.cont_indents.push(indent);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    if let Some(p) = pending.take() {\n        finish_entry(p, &mut changes);\n    }\n\n    Analysis {\n        todo_count,\n        bugs_count,\n        changes,\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// A correctly-numbered, correctly-indented file: no changes.\n    #[test]\n    fn clean_file_has_no_changes() {\n        let doc = \"# Todo\\n\\n## Todo\\n\\n intro\\n\\n1. First.\\n   cont.\\n2. Second.\\n3. Third.\\n   more.\\n\\n## Bugs\\n\\n bugs intro\\n\\n1. A bug.\\n\";\n        let a = analyze(doc);\n        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 3);\n        assert_eq!(a.bugs_count, 1);\n        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());\n    }\n\n    /// Entries numbered 2, 3 renumber to 1, 2.\n    #[test]\n    fn gap_renumbers() {\n        let a = analyze(\"## Todo\\n\\n2. a.\\n3. b.\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(a.changes.len(), 2);\n        assert_eq!((a.changes[0].num_old, a.changes[0].num_new), (2, 1));\n        assert_eq!(a.changes[0].first_line, \"2. a.\");\n        assert_eq!(a.changes[0].line_no, 3);\n        assert_eq!((a.changes[1].num_old, a.changes[1].num_new), (3, 2));\n    }\n\n    /// A two-digit entry renumbered to one digit shifts its\n    /// continuation indent 4 → 3.\n    #[test]\n    fn indent_shifts_with_prefix_width() {\n        let a = analyze(\"## Todo\\n\\n10. x.\\n    cont.\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(a.changes.len(), 1);\n        let c = &a.changes[0];\n        assert_eq!((c.num_old, c.num_new), (10, 1));\n        assert_eq!((c.indent_old, c.indent_new), (Some(4), Some(3)));\n    }\n\n    /// A correctly-numbered entry with the wrong continuation\n    /// indent is still flagged.\n    #[test]\n    fn indent_only_issue_flagged() {\n        let a = analyze(\"## Todo\\n\\n1. x.\\n  cont.\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(a.changes.len(), 1);\n        let c = &a.changes[0];\n        assert_eq!((c.num_old, c.num_new), (1, 1));\n        assert_eq!((c.indent_old, c.indent_new), (Some(2), Some(3)));\n    }\n\n    /// Continuation indent is measured against the minimum\n    /// (base) indent, so nested sub-bullets don't trip it.\n    #[test]\n    fn nested_sub_bullets_use_min_indent() {\n        let a = analyze(\"## Todo\\n\\n1. x.\\n   - sub.\\n     deep.\\n\");\n        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());\n    }\n\n    /// `## Bugs` is numbered independently of `## Todo`.\n    #[test]\n    fn bugs_numbered_independently() {\n        let a = analyze(\"## Todo\\n\\n1. t.\\n\\n## Bugs\\n\\n2. b.\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 1);\n        assert_eq!(a.bugs_count, 1);\n        assert_eq!(a.changes.len(), 1);\n        assert_eq!(a.changes[0].section, Section::Bugs);\n        assert_eq!((a.changes[0].num_old, a.changes[0].num_new), (2, 1));\n    }\n\n    /// A `### ` heading inside `## Todo` ends the prior entry and\n    /// is not itself counted.\n    #[test]\n    fn heading_in_todo_section_ignored() {\n        let a = analyze(\"## Todo\\n\\n intro\\n\\n### Current In Progress\\n1. x.\\n2. y.\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 2);\n        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());\n    }\n\n    /// A column-0 `N. ` line in `## Done` is not scanned.\n    #[test]\n    fn done_section_not_scanned() {\n        let a = analyze(\"## Todo\\n\\n1. x.\\n\\n## Done\\n\\n9. done thing\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 1);\n        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());\n    }\n\n    /// Blank lines within an entry's continuation block carry no\n    /// indent and don't affect the measured base.\n    #[test]\n    fn blank_continuation_lines_ignored() {\n        let a = analyze(\"## Todo\\n\\n1. x.\\n   cont.\\n\\n   more.\\n2. y.\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 2);\n        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

**0.55.0-1, `src/todo_helpers.rs` created** — the shared core:

- `Section` enum (`Todo` / `Bugs`), `Change` (per-entry: old/new number, old/new continuation-indent base), `Analysis` (entry counts + changes).
- `analyze(content)` — single-pass walk: tracks the `## ` section, accumulates each entry's continuation block, and emits a `Change` for any entry whose number or measured-base indent is off. Hand-rolled `^\d+\. ` prefix parse, no `regex` dep.
- Headings (`#`/`###`) end an entry's continuation block — so `### Current In Progress` inside `## Todo` is handled cleanly. `## Done` / `## In Progress` / `# References` are skipped.
- Continuation indent measured against each entry's *minimum* non-blank indent (nested `- ` sub-bullets preserved).
- 9 unit tests: clean file, gap renumber, prefix-width indent shift, indent-only issue, nested sub-bullets, independent `## Bugs` numbering, `### ` heading ignored, `## Done` not scanned, blank continuation lines.

Next: `src/validate_todo.rs` + `main.rs` wiring. Review this first?

  "content": "//! The `validate-todo` subcommand: check that the `## Todo` and\n//! `## Bugs` sections of a todo file are numbered `1..N` in\n//! document order, with continuation-line indent matching each\n//! entry's number-prefix width.\n//!\n//! Read-only diagnostic; exits non-zero if any entry needs\n//! fixing. The check half of the `validate-todo` / `fix-todo`\n//! pair — `fix-todo` is the rewrite.\n\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse log::{debug, info};\n\nuse crate::context::Context;\nuse crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;\nuse crate::todo_helpers::{self, Section, TODO_FILE};\n\n/// Clap-derived args for `validate-todo`.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct ValidateTodoArgs {\n    /// Todo markdown file to check\n    #[arg(value_name = \"FILE\", default_value = TODO_FILE)]\n    pub file: PathBuf,\n}\n\n/// Inputs to the validate-todo op, flat, owned, clap-free.\n///\n/// Mirrors `ValidateTodoArgs`: the positional `FILE` path.\npub struct ValidateTodoParams {\n    pub file: PathBuf,\n}\n\nimpl From<&ValidateTodoArgs> for ValidateTodoParams {\n    /// Convert clap-derived `ValidateTodoArgs` into the flat\n    /// `ValidateTodoParams` (total — the single field copies over).\n    fn from(a: &ValidateTodoArgs) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            file: a.file.clone(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl SubcommandRunner for ValidateTodoArgs {\n    type Params = ValidateTodoParams;\n\n    /// Delegate to the `From<&ValidateTodoArgs>` impl (total).\n    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String> {\n        Ok(ValidateTodoParams::from(self))\n    }\n\n    /// Run the `validate_todo` op.\n    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n        validate_todo(ctx, params)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Truncate `line` to a single-line snippet for the report,\n/// counting by `char` so a multi-byte character is never split.\nfn snippet(line: &str) -> String {\n    const MAX: usize = 56;\n    let chars: Vec<char> = line.chars().collect();\n    if chars.len() <= MAX {\n        line.to_string()\n    } else {\n        let head: String = chars[..MAX].iter().collect();\n        format!(\"{head}…\")\n    }\n}\n\n/// Run the `validate-todo` subcommand: scan the todo file and\n/// report each `## Todo` / `## Bugs` entry whose number or\n/// continuation indent is off; errors if any are found.\n///\n/// `ctx` is unused — validate-todo reads a plain file and neither\n/// the user config nor the `--log` path applies; it's present for\n/// the uniform subcommand-layer signature.\npub fn validate_todo(\n    _ctx: &Context,\n    params: &ValidateTodoParams,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"validate-todo: enter\");\n    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&params.file)\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"cannot read {}: {e}\", params.file.display()))?;\n    let analysis = todo_helpers::analyze(&content);\n\n    info!(\"validate-todo: {}\", params.file.display());\n\n    let mut last_section: Option<Section> = None;\n    for c in &analysis.changes {\n        if last_section != Some(c.section) {\n            info!(\"\");\n            info!(\"{}\", c.section.header());\n            last_section = Some(c.section);\n        }\n        let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n        if c.num_old != c.num_new {\n            parts.push(format!(\"number {} → {}\", c.num_old, c.num_new));\n        }\n        if let (Some(o), Some(n)) = (c.indent_old, c.indent_new)\n            && o != n\n        {\n            parts.push(format!(\"indent {o} → {n}\"));\n        }\n        info!(\n            \"  line {}: {} — {}\",\n            c.line_no,\n            parts.join(\", \"),\n            snippet(&c.first_line)\n        );\n    }\n\n    info!(\"\");\n    let total = analysis.todo_count + analysis.bugs_count;\n    if analysis.changes.is_empty() {\n        info!(\n            \"{total} entries checked ({} Todo, {} Bugs) — all sequential\",\n            analysis.todo_count, analysis.bugs_count\n        );\n        debug!(\"validate-todo: exit\");\n        Ok(())\n    } else {\n        info!(\n            \"{total} entries checked ({} Todo, {} Bugs) — {} need fixing\",\n            analysis.todo_count,\n            analysis.bugs_count,\n            analysis.changes.len()\n        );\n        debug!(\"validate-todo: exit with issues\");\n        Err(format!(\n            \"{} todo entry/entries need fixing — run `vc-x1 fix-todo`\",\n            analysis.changes.len()\n        )\n        .into())\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// A line within the limit is returned unchanged.\n    #[test]\n    fn snippet_keeps_short_lines() {\n        assert_eq!(snippet(\"1. short entry\"), \"1. short entry\");\n    }\n\n    /// A long line is cut on a `char` boundary and gets an\n    /// ellipsis — 56 chars plus `…`.\n    #[test]\n    fn snippet_truncates_on_char_boundary() {\n        let s = snippet(&\"→\".repeat(100));\n        assert_eq!(s.chars().count(), 57);\n        assert!(s.ends_with('…'));\n    }\n}\n"
}

**Body:**
```
First step of the 0.55.0 cycle: the `validate-todo`
subcommand and the shared `todo_helpers` core it scans

148	pub fn analyze(content: &str) -> Analysis {
149	    let mut changes = Vec::new();
150	    let mut todo_count = 0;
151	    let mut bugs_count = 0;
152	    let mut section: Option<Section> = None;
153	    let mut entry_idx = 0usize;
154	    let mut pending: Option<PendingEntry> = None;
155	
156	    for (i, line) in content.lines().enumerate() {
157	        if let Some(kind) = section_header(line) {
158	            // We have a new section we're going to number it's entries
159	            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {
160	                // Flush any pending entries
161	                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);
162	            }
163	            section = kind;
164	            entry_idx = 0;
165	            continue;
166	        }
167	        // Any other heading (`#`, `###`, …) ends the current
168	        // entry's continuation block without changing the section.
169	        if line.starts_with('#') {
170	            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {
171	                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);
172	            }
173	            continue;
174	        }
175	        let Some(sec) = section else {
176	            // We're not processing any section yet
177	            continue;
178	        };
179	        if let Some(num) = parse_entry_prefix(line) {
180	            // New entry for the current section
181	            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {
182	                // Flush any previous entry we were working on
183	                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);
184	            }
185	
186	            // Update information for this entry
187	            entry_idx += 1;
188	            match sec {
189	                Section::Todo => todo_count += 1,
190	                Section::Bugs => bugs_count += 1,
191	            }
192	            pending = Some(PendingEntry {
193	                section: sec,
194	                line_no: i + 1,
195	                first_line: line.to_string(),
196	                num_old: num,
197	                num_new: entry_idx,
198	                cont_indents: Vec::new(),
199	            });
200	        } else if let Some(p) = pending.as_mut() {
201	            // Continuation line: record the leading-space count of
202	            // non-blank lines; blank lines carry no indent.
203	            if !line.trim().is_empty() {
204	                let indent = line.len() - line.trim_start_matches(' ').len();
205	                p.cont_indents.push(indent);
206	            }
207	        }
208	    }
209	
210	    // Completed processing finsh any last pending line
211	    if let Some(p) = pending.take() {
212	        finish_entry(p, &mut changes);
213	    }
214	
215	    // Return the Analysis
216	    Analysis {
217	        todo_count,
218	        bugs_count,
219	        changes,
220	    }
221	}
222

> AGENT

Reviewed the inline comments you added to `analyze`. Findings:

**Typos — should fix:**
- L158 `number it's entries` → `its` (possessive, not "it is").
- L210 `finsh` → `finish`.

**Accuracy:**
- L176 `// We're not processing any section yet` — "yet" is misleading: `section` is `None` not just *before* `## Todo`/`## Bugs` but also *after* them (inside `## In Progress`, `## Done`, `# References`). Better: `// Not inside a ## Todo / ## Bugs section`.
- L210 `…any last pending line` — it's a pending **entry** (`PendingEntry`), not a line.
- L160 `// Flush any pending entries` — `pending` is `Option<PendingEntry>`, so at most one — "entry" singular.

**Convention:** CLAUDE.md's Code Conventions say inline `//` comments inside function bodies are "discouraged unless they capture a non-obvious WHY." A few of these narrate WHAT the code already states plainly — `// Flush any pending entries`, `// Update information for this entry`, `// Return the Analysis`. By the project's own rule those would be dropped; the WHY-bearing ones (heading-ends-continuation, the continuation-line note) are the kind it keeps. The `analyze` state machine isn't trivial, though, so light signposting at the three branch points (new section / other heading / entry vs continuation) is a defensible exception if you want it.

How do you want to land this:
1. Fix typos + accuracy wording, **trim** the pure-WHAT comments (strict CLAUDE.md).
2. Fix typos + accuracy wording, **keep** the signposting comments as-is otherwise.

Either way I'll fix the typos. Which do you want — trim or keep?

```
1	//! Shared core for the `validate-todo` / `fix-todo` subcommands:
2	//! parse `notes/todo.md`'s numbered sections and compute the
3	//! renumber / re-indent plan.
4	//!
5	//! - `## Todo` and `## Bugs` hold manually-numbered entries
6	//!   (`1.` `2.` … at column 0); every other section is skipped.
7	//! - `analyze` walks the file once and returns one `Change` per
8	//!   entry whose number or continuation indent is off.
9	//! - Continuation indent is normalized against each entry's
10	//!   *measured* base indent, so nested `- ` sub-bullets keep
11	//!   their relative depth.
12	
13	/// Default todo file, relative to the workspace root.
14	pub const TODO_FILE: &str = "notes/todo.md";
15	
16	/// A manually-numbered section of the todo file.
17	#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
18	pub enum Section {
19	    /// The `## Todo` backlog list.
20	    Todo,
21	    /// The `## Bugs` list.
22	    Bugs,
23	}
24	
25	impl Section {
26	    /// The section's markdown header text (`## Todo` / `## Bugs`).
27	    pub fn header(self) -> &'static str {
28	        match self {
29	            Section::Todo => "## Todo",
30	            Section::Bugs => "## Bugs",
31	        }
32	    }
33	}
34	
35	/// One entry whose number and/or continuation indent needs to
36	/// change for the section to be sequentially numbered.
37	///
38	/// - `num_old` / `num_new` — the displayed number and its
39	///   sequential (1-based) position; equal when only the indent is
40	///   off.
41	/// - `indent_old` / `indent_new` — the measured continuation-base
42	///   indent and the expected one (the number prefix's width);
43	///   both `None` when the entry has no continuation lines.
44	pub struct Change {
45	    pub section: Section,
46	    pub line_no: usize,
47	    pub first_line: String,
48	    pub num_old: usize,
49	    pub num_new: usize,
50	    pub indent_old: Option<usize>,
51	    pub indent_new: Option<usize>,
52	}
53	
54	/// Result of scanning a todo file: per-section entry counts and
55	/// the list of entries needing a fix.
56	pub struct Analysis {
57	    pub todo_count: usize,
58	    pub bugs_count: usize,
59	    pub changes: Vec<Change>,
60	}
61	
62	/// An entry being accumulated during the `analyze` walk — its
63	/// first line plus the indents of its continuation lines.
64	struct PendingEntry {
65	    section: Section,
66	    line_no: usize,
67	    first_line: String,
68	    num_old: usize,
69	    num_new: usize,
70	    cont_indents: Vec<usize>,
71	}
72	
73	/// Number of decimal digits in `n` (entry numbers are ≥ 1).
74	fn digit_count(n: usize) -> usize {
75	    n.to_string().len()
76	}
77	
78	/// Classify a line as a `## ` section header.
79	///
80	/// - `None` — not a level-2 header (covers `# `, `### `, body
81	///   text).
82	/// - `Some(None)` — a `## ` header that isn't `## Todo` /
83	///   `## Bugs` (e.g. `## In Progress`, `## Done`).
84	/// - `Some(Some(section))` — the `## Todo` or `## Bugs` header.
85	fn section_header(line: &str) -> Option<Option<Section>> {
86	    if !line.starts_with("## ") {
87	        return None;
88	    }
89	    Some(match line.trim_end() {
90	        "## Todo" => Some(Section::Todo),
91	        "## Bugs" => Some(Section::Bugs),
92	        _ => None,
93	    })
94	}
95	
96	/// Parse a leading `N. ` entry prefix at column 0.
97	///
98	/// Returns the entry number when `line` begins with one or more
99	/// ASCII digits followed by `". "` — the renumber convention's
100	/// `^\d+\. ` anchor. Returns `None` for intro lines (which carry
101	/// a leading space), continuation lines, and headings.
102	fn parse_entry_prefix(line: &str) -> Option<usize> {
103	    let bytes = line.as_bytes();
104	    let mut i = 0;
105	    while i < bytes.len() && bytes[i].is_ascii_digit() {
106	        i += 1;
107	    }
108	    if i == 0 || bytes.get(i) != Some(&b'.') || bytes.get(i + 1) != Some(&b' ') {
109	        return None;
110	    }
111	    line[..i].parse().ok()
112	}
113	
114	/// Finalize a pending entry: measure its continuation-indent base
115	/// and, when the number or indent needs to change, push a
116	/// `Change`.
117	fn finish_entry(p: PendingEntry, changes: &mut Vec<Change>) {
118	    let prefix_width = digit_count(p.num_new) + 2; // ". " is two chars
119	    let indent_old = p.cont_indents.iter().copied().min();
120	    let indent_new = indent_old.map(|_| prefix_width);
121	    if p.num_old != p.num_new || indent_old != indent_new {
122	        changes.push(Change {
123	            section: p.section,
124	            line_no: p.line_no,
125	            first_line: p.first_line,
126	            num_old: p.num_old,
127	            num_new: p.num_new,
128	            indent_old,
129	            indent_new,
130	        });
131	    }
132	}
133	
134	/// Scan a todo file's `## Todo` and `## Bugs` sections and report
135	/// every entry whose number or continuation indent needs to
136	/// change.
137	///
138	/// - Walks lines once, tracking the current `## ` section and the
139	///   entry being accumulated.
140	/// - An entry first-line is `N. ` at column 0; expected numbering
141	///   is `1..N` in document order.
142	/// - Any heading (`#`, `###`, …) ends the current entry's
143	///   continuation block; `## Todo` / `## Bugs` headers also reset
144	///   the section.
145	/// - An entry's continuation indent is reported against the
146	///   *measured* base (the minimum non-blank continuation indent),
147	///   so nested sub-bullets keep their relative depth.
148	pub fn analyze(content: &str) -> Analysis {
149	    let mut changes = Vec::new();
150	    let mut todo_count = 0;
151	    let mut bugs_count = 0;
152	    let mut section: Option<Section> = None;
153	    let mut entry_idx = 0usize;
154	    let mut pending: Option<PendingEntry> = None;
155	
156	    for (i, line) in content.lines().enumerate() {
157	        if let Some(kind) = section_header(line) {
158	            // We have a new section we're going to number its entries
159	            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {
160	                // Flush any pending entry
161	                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);
162	            }
163	            section = kind;
164	            entry_idx = 0;
165	            continue;
166	        }
167	        // Any other heading (`#`, `###`, …) ends the current
168	        // entry's continuation block without changing the section.
169	        if line.starts_with('#') {
170	            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {
171	                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);
172	            }
173	            continue;
174	        }
175	        let Some(sec) = section else {
176	            // We're not processing a section
177	            continue;
178	        };
179	        if let Some(num) = parse_entry_prefix(line) {
180	            // New entry for the current section
181	            if let Some(p) = pending.take() {
182	                // Flush any previous entry we were working on
183	                finish_entry(p, &mut changes);
184	            }
185	
186	            // Update information for this entry
187	            entry_idx += 1;
188	            match sec {
189	                Section::Todo => todo_count += 1,
190	                Section::Bugs => bugs_count += 1,
191	            }
192	            pending = Some(PendingEntry {
193	                section: sec,
194	                line_no: i + 1,
195	                first_line: line.to_string(),
196	                num_old: num,
197	                num_new: entry_idx,
198	                cont_indents: Vec::new(),
199	            });
200	        } else if let Some(p) = pending.as_mut() {
201	            // Continuation line: record the leading-space count of
202	            // non-blank lines; blank lines carry no indent.
203	            if !line.trim().is_empty() {
204	                let indent = line.len() - line.trim_start_matches(' ').len();
205	                p.cont_indents.push(indent);
206	            }
207	        }
208	    }
209	
210	    if let Some(p) = pending.take() {
211	        finish_entry(p, &mut changes);
212	    }
213	
214	    Analysis {
215	        todo_count,
216	        bugs_count,
217	        changes,
218	    }
219	}
220	
221	#[cfg(test)]
222	mod tests {
223	    use super::*;
224	
225	    /// A correctly-numbered, correctly-indented file: no changes.
226	    #[test]
227	    fn clean_file_has_no_changes() {
228	        let doc = "# Todo\n\n## Todo\n\n intro\n\n1. First.\n   cont.\n2. Second.\n3. Third.\n   more.\n\n## Bugs\n\n bugs intro\n\n1. A bug.\n";
229	        let a = analyze(doc);
230	        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 3);
231	        assert_eq!(a.bugs_count, 1);
232	        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());
233	    }
234	
235	    /// Entries numbered 2, 3 renumber to 1, 2.
236	    #[test]
237	    fn gap_renumbers() {
238	        let a = analyze("## Todo\n\n2. a.\n3. b.\n");
239	        assert_eq!(a.changes.len(), 2);
240	        assert_eq!((a.changes[0].num_old, a.changes[0].num_new), (2, 1));
241	        assert_eq!(a.changes[0].first_line, "2. a.");
242	        assert_eq!(a.changes[0].line_no, 3);
243	        assert_eq!((a.changes[1].num_old, a.changes[1].num_new), (3, 2));
244	    }
245	
246	    /// A two-digit entry renumbered to one digit shifts its
247	    /// continuation indent 4 → 3.
248	    #[test]
249	    fn indent_shifts_with_prefix_width() {
250	        let a = analyze("## Todo\n\n10. x.\n    cont.\n");
251	        assert_eq!(a.changes.len(), 1);
252	        let c = &a.changes[0];
253	        assert_eq!((c.num_old, c.num_new), (10, 1));
254	        assert_eq!((c.indent_old, c.indent_new), (Some(4), Some(3)));
255	    }
256	
257	    /// A correctly-numbered entry with the wrong continuation
258	    /// indent is still flagged.
259	    #[test]
260	    fn indent_only_issue_flagged() {
261	        let a = analyze("## Todo\n\n1. x.\n  cont.\n");
262	        assert_eq!(a.changes.len(), 1);
263	        let c = &a.changes[0];
264	        assert_eq!((c.num_old, c.num_new), (1, 1));
265	        assert_eq!((c.indent_old, c.indent_new), (Some(2), Some(3)));
266	    }
267	
268	    /// Continuation indent is measured against the minimum
269	    /// (base) indent, so nested sub-bullets don't trip it.
270	    #[test]
271	    fn nested_sub_bullets_use_min_indent() {
272	        let a = analyze("## Todo\n\n1. x.\n   - sub.\n     deep.\n");
273	        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());
274	    }
275	
276	    /// `## Bugs` is numbered independently of `## Todo`.
277	    #[test]
278	    fn bugs_numbered_independently() {
279	        let a = analyze("## Todo\n\n1. t.\n\n## Bugs\n\n2. b.\n");
280	        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 1);
281	        assert_eq!(a.bugs_count, 1);
282	        assert_eq!(a.changes.len(), 1);
283	        assert_eq!(a.changes[0].section, Section::Bugs);
284	        assert_eq!((a.changes[0].num_old, a.changes[0].num_new), (2, 1));
285	    }
286	
287	    /// A `### ` heading inside `## Todo` ends the prior entry and
288	    /// is not itself counted.
289	    #[test]
290	    fn heading_in_todo_section_ignored() {
291	        let a = analyze("## Todo\n\n intro\n\n### Current In Progress\n1. x.\n2. y.\n");
292	        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 2);
293	        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());
294	    }
295	
296	    /// A column-0 `N. ` line in `## Done` is not scanned.
297	    #[test]
298	    fn done_section_not_scanned() {
299	        let a = analyze("## Todo\n\n1. x.\n\n## Done\n\n9. done thing\n");
300	        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 1);
301	        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());
302	    }
303	
304	    /// Blank lines within an entry's continuation block carry no
305	    /// indent and don't affect the measured base.
306	    #[test]
307	    fn blank_continuation_lines_ignored() {
308	        let a = analyze("## Todo\n\n1. x.\n   cont.\n\n   more.\n2. y.\n");
309	        assert_eq!(a.todo_count, 2);
310	        assert!(a.changes.is_empty());
311	    }

Continuation indent is normalized against each entry's\n//!   *measured* base indent, so nested `- ` sub-bullets keep\n//!   their relative depth.\n\n/// Default todo file, relative to the workspace root.\npub const TODO_FILE: &str = \"notes/todo.md\";\n\n/// A manually-numbered section of the todo file.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]\npub enum Section {\n    /// The `## Todo` backlog list.\n    Todo,\n    /// The `## Bugs` list.\n    Bugs,\n}\n\nimpl Section {\n    /// The section's markdown header text (`## Todo` / `## Bugs`).\n    pub fn header(self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            Section::Todo => \"## Todo\",\n            Section::Bugs => \"## Bugs\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// One entry whose number and/or continuation indent needs to\n/// change for the section to be sequentially numbered.\n///\n/// - `indent_old` / `indent_new` — the measured continuation-base\n///   indent and the expected one (the number prefix's width);\n///   both `None` when the entry has no continuation lines.\npub struct Change {\n    pub section: Section,\n    pub line_no: usize,\n    pub first_line: String,\n    pub new_first_line: String,\n    pub num_old: usize,\n    pub num_new: usize,\n    pub indent_old: Option<usize>,\n    pub indent_new: Option<usize>,\n}\n\n/// Result of scanning a todo file: per-section entry counts, the\n/// entries needing a fix, and the fully corrected file content.\npub struct Analysis {\n    pub todo_count: usize,\n    pub bugs_count: usize,\n    pub changes: Vec<Change>,\n    pub fixed: String,\n}\n\n/// An entry being accumulated during the `analyze` walk — its\n/// first line, that line minus its `N. ` prefix, and the raw\n/// continuation lines (blank lines included).\nstruct PendingEntry {\n    section: Section,\n    line_no: usize,\n    num_old: usize,\n    num_new: usize,\n    first_line: String,\n    rest: String,\n    cont: Vec<String>,\n}\n\n/// Number of decimal digits in `n` (entry numbers are ≥ 1).\nfn digit_count(n: usize) -> usize {\n    n.to_string().len()\n}\n\n/// Count of leading ASCII spaces on `line`.\nfn leading_spaces(line: &str) -> usize {\n    line.len() - line.trim_start_matches(' ').len()\n}\n\n/// Classify a line as a `## ` section header.\n///\n/// - `None` — not a level-2 header (covers `# `, `### `, body\n///   text).\n/// - `Some(None)` — a `## ` header that isn't `## Todo` /\n///   `## Bugs` (e.g. `## In Progress`, `## Done`).\n/// - `Some(Some(section))` — the `## Todo` or `## Bugs` header.\nfn section_header(line: &str) -> Option<Option<Section>> {\n    if !line.starts_with(\"## \") {\n        return None;\n    }\n    Some(match line.trim_end() {\n        \"## Todo\" => Some(Section::Todo),\n        \"## Bugs\" => Some(Section::Bugs),\n        _ => None,\n    })\n}\n\n/// Parse a leading `N. ` entry prefix at column 0.\n///\n/// Returns `(number, prefix_len)` — the entry number and the byte\n/// length of the `N. ` prefix — when `line` begins with one or\n/// more ASCII digits followed by `\". \"` (the renumber\n/// convention's `^\\d+\\. ` anchor). Returns `None` for intro lines\n/// (which carry a leading space), continuation lines, and\n/// headings.\nfn parse_entry_prefix(line: &str) -> Option<(usize, usize)> {\n    let bytes = line.as_bytes();\n    let mut i = 0;\n    while i < bytes.len() && bytes[i].is_ascii_digit() {\n        i += 1;\n    }\n    if i == 0 || bytes.get(i) != Some(&b'.') || bytes.get(i + 1) != Some(&b' ') depth relative to the entry's base.\nfn finish_entry(p: PendingEntry, changes: &mut Vec<Change>, out: &mut Vec<String>) {\n    let prefix_width = digit_count(p.num_new) + 2; // \". \" is two chars\n    let new_first_line = format!(\"{}. {}\", p.num_new, p.rest);\n\n    // Measured base = minimum indent of the non-blank continuation\n    // lines; `None` when the entry has no continuation text.\n as usize;\n            out.push(format!(\"{}{}\", \" \".repeat(shifted), line.trim_start_matches(' ')));\n        }\n    }\n\n    if p.num_old != p.num_new || indent_old != indent_new {\n out.push(line.to_string());\n        }\n    }\n    // End of file — finish any entry still open.\n    if let Some(p) = pending.take() {\n        finish_entry(p, &mut changes, &mut out);\n    }\n\n > AGENT

**0.55.0-2, group 2** — `src/todo_helpers.rs` extended with the rewrite path:

- `analyze` now also produces **`Analysis.fixed`** — the file content with every `## Todo` / `## Bugs` entry renumbered and re-indented. It round-trips an already-correct file byte-for-byte (switched to `split('\n')` so a trailing newline survives).
- `Change` gains **`new_first_line`** — the entry's first line with the corrected number (for `fix-todo`'s dry-run output).
- The walk now accumulates each entry's raw continuation lines and emits a corrected block on finalize: the block shifts as a whole by `prefix_width − measured_base`, so nested sub-bullets keep their depth.

/// `FixTodoParams` (total — every field copies straight over).\n    fn from(a: &FixTodoArgs) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            file: a.file.clone(),\n            no_dry_run: a.no_dry_run,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl SubcommandRunner for FixTodoArgs {\n    type Params = FixTodoParams;\n\n    /// Delegate to the `From<&FixTodoArgs>` impl (total).\n    fn to_params(&self) -> std::error::Error>> {\n        fix_todo(ctx, params)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Build the `[…]` tag describing what changed for an entry —\n/// `[was N]`, `[indent A → B]`, or both.\nfn change_tag(c: &Change) -> String {\n    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    if c.num_old != c.num_new {\n        parts.push(format!(\"was {}\", c.num_old));\n    }\n    if let (Some(o), Some(n)) = (c.indent_old, c.indent_new)\n        && o != n\n    {\n        parts.push(format!(\"indent {o} → {n}\"));\n    }\n    format!(\"[{}]\", parts.join(\", \"))\n}\n\n/// Run the `fix-todo` subcommand: renumber the todo file's\n/// `## Todo` / `## Bugs` sections and normalize continuation\n/// indent. Dry-run prints the corrected line of each changed\n/// entry; `--no-dry-run` writes the file.\n///\n/// `ctx` is unused — fix-todo reads and writes a plain file and\n/// neither the user config nor the `--log` path applies; it's\n/// present for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.\npub fn fix_todo(_ctx: &Context, params: &FixTodoParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"fix-todo: enter\");\n    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&params.file)\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"cannot read {}: {e}\", params.file.display()))?;\n    let analysis = todo_helpers::analyze(&content);\n\n    info!(\"fix-todo: {}\", params.file.display());\n\n    let total = analysis.todo_count + analysis.bugs_count;\n    if analysis.changes.is_empty() {\n        info!(\n            \"{total} entries checked ({} Todo, {} Bugs) — already normalized\",\n            analysis.todo_count, analysis.bugs_count\n        );\n        debug!(\"fix-todo: exit\");\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    // Print the corrected line of every changed entry — the\n    // output is the result, not a description of it.\n    let mut last_section: Option<Section> = None;\n    for c in &analysis.changes {\n        if last_section != Some(c.section) {\n            info!(\"\");\n            info!(\"{}\", c.section.header());\n            last_section = Some(c.section);\n        }\n        info!(\"{}  {}\", c.new_first_line, change_tag(c));\n    }\n\n    info!(\"\");\n    if params.no_dry_run {\n        std::fs::write(&params.file, &analysis.fixed)\n            .map_err(|e| format!(\"cannot write {}: {e}\", params.file.display()))?;\n        info!(\n            \"{} entries renumbered — wrote {}\",\n            analysis.changes.len(),\n            params.file.display()\n        );\n    } else {\n        info!(\n            \"{} entries to renumber — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply\",\n            analysis.changes.len()\n        );\n    }\n    debug!(\"fix-todo: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Build a `Change` for tag tests.\n    fn change(num_old: usize, num_new: usize, indent: Option<(usize, usize)>) -> Change {\n        Change {\n            section: Section::Todo,\n            line_no: 1,\n            first_line: String::new(),\n            new_first_line: String::new(),\n            num_old,\n            num_new,\n            indent_old: indent.map(|(o, _)| o),\n            indent_new: indent.map(|(_, n)| n),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// A pure renumber tags only the old number.\n    #[test]\n    fn tag_number_only() {\n        assert_eq!(change_tag(&change(3, 1, None)), \"[was 3]\");\n    }\n\n    /// A pure re-indent tags only the indent shift.\n    #[test]\n    fn tag_indent_only() {\n        assert_eq!(change_tag(&change(1, 1, Some((2, 3)))), \"[indent 2 → 3]\");\n    }\n\n    /// A renumber that also shifts indent tags both.\n    #[test]\n    fn tag_number_and_indent() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            change_tag(&change(10, 9, Some((4, 3)))),\n            \"[was 10, indent 4 → 3]\"\n        );\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Ran it. Here's what's happening:

**Diagnosis.** Your test inserted a bare `0` at column 0 *inside* entry #1's continuation block:
```
   Renumber `## Todo` and `## Bugs` entries in `notes/todo.md`,
0
   normalize continuation-line indent to match prefix width.
```
The parser checks `0` for an entry prefix — `^\d+\. ` — and it fails (no `. ` after the `0`), and it's not a heading. So `0` falls through to "continuation line of entry #1." Its indent is 0. The measured base is the **minimum** non-blank continuation indent → `0` drags the base from 3 down to **0**. So `fix-todo` computes `delta = prefix_width − base = 3 − 0 = +3` and shifts the *whole* block: the real continuations 3→6, the `0` itself 0→3. Hence `[indent 0 → 3]` and the over-indented "odd" source. Markdown still renders it because it's tolerant of over-indented list continuations.

**Root cause.** A real continuation line *must* be indented — a column-0 line can't be a list continuation. The measured-base algorithm assumes no continuation line is shallower than the true base; a stray column-0 line breaks that. Your `0` is malformed input — a column-0 line in `## Todo` that's neither `N. ` nor a heading. (It's a test artifact — it has to come out of `todo.md` before the `-2` commit regardless.)

**Options:**

1. **Just remove the test `0`** — no code change. The algorithm stays naive about malformed input.
2. **Harden (small, recommended)** — in `finish_entry`, exclude column-0 lines from the base measurement and emit them verbatim. A continuation is by definition indented, so a col-0 line shouldn't define the base. Result: base stays 3, no false `[indent 0 → 3]`, real continuations untouched, the stray line passes through unchanged. ~2 lines.
3. **Harden + flag** — option 2, plus `validate-todo`/`fix-todo` *report* a column-0 non-entry line as malformed input. A new diagnostic category — bigger; better as a follow-up todo than `-2` scope.

I'd do **2** for this cycle (and note "flag malformed lines" as a future todo). It makes the tool robust against garbage without corrupting good content, and doesn't balloon `-2`.

(Minor, separate: the summary reads "1 entries" — I'll pluralize that while I'm in there.)

Which option do you want?

  "old_string": "    // Measured base = minimum indent of the non-blank continuation\n    // lines; `None` when the entry has no continuation text.\n    let base = p\n        .cont\n        .iter()\n        .filter(|l| !l.trim().is_empty())\n        .map(|l| leading_spaces(l))\n        .min();\n    let (indent_old, indent_new) = match base {\n        Some(b) => (Some(b), Some(prefix_width)),\n        None => (None, None),\n    };\n\n    out.push(new_first_line.clone());\n    let delta: isize = base.map_or(0, |b| prefix_width as isize - b as isize);\n    for line in &p.cont {\n        if line.trim().is_empty() {\n            out.push(line.clone());\n        } else {\n            let shifted = (leading_spaces(line) as isize + delta).max(0) as usize;\n            out.push(format!(\n                \"{}{}\",\n                \" \".repeat(shifted),\n                line.trim_start_matches(' ')\n            ));\n        }\n    }",
  "new_string": "    // Measured base = minimum indent of the *indented* non-blank\n    // continuation lines. A column-0 line can't be a real list\n    // continuation (continuations must be indented), so it's\n    // excluded from the base and emitted verbatim below — a stray\n    // one doesn't drag the base down to 0.\n    let base = p\n        .cont\n        .iter()\n        .filter(|l| !l.trim().is_empty())\n        .map(|l| leading_spaces(l))\n        .filter(|&n| n > 0)\n        .min();\n    let (indent_old, indent_new) = match base {\n        Some(b) => (Some(b), Some(prefix_width)),\n        None => (None, None),\n    };\n\n    out.push(new_first_line.clone());\n    let delta: isize = base.map_or(0, |b| prefix_width as isize - b as isize);\n    for line in &p.cont {\n        let indent = leading_spaces(line);\n        if line.trim().is_empty() || indent == 0 {\n            // Blank line, or a column-0 line that isn't a real\n            // continuation — pass through unchanged.\n            out.push(line.clone());\n        } else {\n            let shifted = (indent as isize + delta).max(0) as usize;\n            out.push(format!(\n                \"{}{}\",\n                \" \".repeat(shifted),\n                line.trim_start_matches(' ')\n            ));\n        }\n    }"

Verified — `fix-todo` now reports **"46 entries checked (45 Todo, 1 Bugs) — already normalized"**: the test `0` is gone (no false `[indent 0 → 3]`), and the count pluralizes.

**Column-0 hardening + pluralization fix-up:**

- `src/todo_helpers.rs` — `finish_entry` excludes column-0 lines from the base measurement (`.filter(|&n| n > 0)`) and emits them verbatim; a stray column-0 line no longer drags the base to 0. Added `entry_word(n)` helper; added `column_zero_line_is_not_a_continuation` test (15 tests total).
- `src/fix_todo.rs` / `src/validate_todo.rs` — use `entry_word` so counts read "1 entry" / "N entries"; reworded validate's "{n} need fixing" → "{n} to fix" (dodges verb agreement).
- `notes/todo.md` — removed the test `0`; added `## Todo` #45 ("flag malformed lines" — the option-3 follow-up).

> AGENT

Agreed — `validate-todo`'s `number 2 → 1 — ` prefix is redundant: the corrected line plus a compact tag already say it. Making it match `fix-todo` is the right call (and you noted early on validate is essentially `fix-todo --dry-run`).

Proposal:

- **Identical per-change line in both:** `  line N: {corrected line}  [tag]`. So `validate-todo` drops `number X → Y — <truncated original>` and shows the corrected line + `[was N]` / `[indent A → B]` tag, exactly like `fix-todo`.
- **Share `change_tag`** — move it from `fix_todo.rs` to `todo_helpers` (pub); both subcommands call it.
- `validate-todo` shows the full corrected line, so its `snippet()` truncation helper goes away.
- `Change.first_line` (the original line) then has no remaining reader — **drop it** from the struct.

Result — `validate-todo`:
```
validate-todo: notes/todo.md

## Todo
  line 45: 1. **single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.**  [was 2]
  line 57: 2. **Investigate `linkme` for subcommand registration.**  [was 3]

46 entries checked (45 Todo, 1 Bugs) — 2 to fix
```

The two commands then differ only by header, summary line, and exit code (validate non-zero). Net change also *removes* code (`snippet` + the `first_line` field). Go?

1	//! The `fix-todo` subcommand: renumber the `## Todo` / `## Bugs`
2	//! sections of a todo file to `1..N` and normalize each entry's
3	//! continuation-line indent.
4	//!
5	//! Dry-run by default — prints each changed entry's corrected
6	//! line so the output *is* the result; `--no-dry-run` writes the
7	//! file in place. The rewrite half of the `validate-todo` /
8	//! `fix-todo` pair.
9	
10	use std::path::PathBuf;
11	
12	use clap::Args;
13	use log::{debug, info};
14	
15	use crate::context::Context;
16	use crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;
17	use crate::todo_helpers::{self, Change, Section, TODO_FILE};
18	
19	/// Clap-derived args for `fix-todo`.
20	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
21	pub struct FixTodoArgs {
22	    /// Todo markdown file to renumber
23	    #[arg(value_name = "FILE", default_value = TODO_FILE)]
24	    pub file: PathBuf,
25	
26	    /// Write the renumbered file in place [default: dry-run]
27	    #[arg(long = "no-dry-run")]
28	    pub no_dry_run: bool,
29	}
30	
31	/// Inputs to the fix-todo op, flat, owned, clap-free.
32	///
33	/// Mirrors `FixTodoArgs`: the positional `FILE` path and the
34	/// `--no-dry-run` write toggle.
35	pub struct FixTodoParams {
36	    pub file: PathBuf,
37	    pub no_dry_run: bool,
38	}
39	
40	impl From<&FixTodoArgs> for FixTodoParams {
41	    /// Convert clap-derived `FixTodoArgs` into the flat
42	    /// `FixTodoParams` (total — every field copies straight over).
43	    fn from(a: &FixTodoArgs) -> Self {
44	        Self {
45	            file: a.file.clone(),
46	            no_dry_run: a.no_dry_run,
47	        }
48	    }
49	}
50	
51	impl SubcommandRunner for FixTodoArgs {
52	    type Params = FixTodoParams;
53	
54	    /// Delegate to the `From<&FixTodoArgs>` impl (total).
55	    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String> {
56	        Ok(FixTodoParams::from(self))
57	    }
58	
59	    /// Run the `fix_todo` op.
60	    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
61	        fix_todo(ctx, params)
62	    }
63	}
64	
65	/// Build the `[…]` tag describing what changed for an entry —
66	/// `[was N]`, `[indent A → B]`, or both.
67	fn change_tag(c: &Change) -> String {
68	    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
69	    if c.num_old != c.num_new {
70	        parts.push(format!("was {}", c.num_old));
71	    }
72	    if let (Some(o), Some(n)) = (c.indent_old, c.indent_new)
73	        && o != n
74	    {
75	        parts.push(format!("indent {o} → {n}"));
76	    }
77	    format!("[{}]", parts.join(", "))
78	}
79	
80	/// Run the `fix-todo` subcommand: renumber the todo file's
81	/// `## Todo` / `## Bugs` sections and normalize continuation
82	/// indent. Dry-run prints the corrected line of each changed
83	/// entry; `--no-dry-run` writes the file.
84	///
85	/// `ctx` is unused — fix-todo reads and writes a plain file and
86	/// neither the user config nor the `--log` path applies; it's
87	/// present for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.
88	pub fn fix_todo(_ctx: &Context, params: &FixTodoParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
89	    debug!("fix-todo: enter");
90	    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&params.file)
91	        .map_err(|e| format!("cannot read {}: {e}", params.file.display()))?;
92	    let analysis = todo_helpers::analyze(&content);
93	
94	    info!("fix-todo: {}", params.file.display());
95	
96	    let total = analysis.todo_count + analysis.bugs_count;
97	    if analysis.changes.is_empty() {
98	        info!(
99	            "{total} {} checked ({} Todo, {} Bugs) — already normalized",
100	            todo_helpers::entry_word(total),
101	            analysis.todo_count,
102	            analysis.bugs_count
103	        );
104	        debug!("fix-todo: exit");
105	        return Ok(());
106	    }
107	
108	    // Print the corrected line of every changed entry — the
109	    // output is the result, not a description of it.
110	    let mut last_section: Option<Section> = None;
111	    for c in &analysis.changes {
112	        if last_section != Some(c.section) {
113	            info!("");
114	            info!("{}", c.section.header());
115	            last_section = Some(c.section);
116	        }
117	        info!("  line {}: {}  {}", c.line_no, c.new_first_line, change_tag(c));
118	    }
119	
120	    let n = analysis.changes.len();
121	    info!("");
122	    if params.no_dry_run {
123	        std::fs::write(&params.file, &analysis.fixed)
124	            .map_err(|e| format!("cannot write {}: {e}", params.file.display()))?;
125	        info!(
126	            "{n} {} renumbered — wrote {}",
127	            todo_helpers::entry_word(n),
128	            params.file.display()
129	        );
130	    } else {
131	        info!(
132	            "{n} {} to renumber — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply",
133	            todo_helpers::entry_word(n)
134	        );
135	    }
136	    debug!("fix-todo: exit");
137	    Ok(())
138	}
139	
140	#[cfg(test)]
141	mod tests {
142	    use super::*;
143	
144	    /// Build a `Change` for tag tests.
145	    fn change(num_old: usize, num_new: usize, indent: Option<(usize, usize)>) -> Change {
146	        Change {
147	            section: Section::Todo,
148	            line_no: 1,
149	            first_line: String::new(),
150	            new_first_line: String::new(),
151	            num_old,
152	            num_new,
153	            indent_old: indent.map(|(o, _)| o),
154	            indent_new: indent.map(|(_, n)| n),
155	        }
156	    }
157	
158	    /// A pure renumber tags only the old number.
159	    #[test]
160	    fn tag_number_only() {
161	        assert_eq!(change_tag(&change(3, 1, None)), "[was 3]");
162	    }
163	
164	    /// A pure re-indent tags only the indent shift.
165	    #[test]
166	    fn tag_indent_only() {
167	        assert_eq!(change_tag(&change(1, 1, Some((2, 3)))), "[indent 2 → 3]");
168	    }
169	
170	    /// A renumber that also shifts indent tags both.
171	    #[test]
172	    fn tag_number_and_indent() {
173	        assert_eq!(
174	            change_tag(&change(10, 9, Some((4, 3)))),
175	            "[was 10, indent 4 → 3]"
176	        );
177	    }
178	}
179

1	//! The `validate-todo` subcommand: check that the `## Todo` and
2	//! `## Bugs` sections of a todo file are numbered `1..N` in
3	//! document order, with continuation-line indent matching each
4	//! entry's number-prefix width.
5	//!
6	//! Read-only diagnostic; exits non-zero if any entry needs
7	//! fixing. The check half of the `validate-todo` / `fix-todo`
8	//! pair — `fix-todo` is the rewrite.
9	
10	use std::path::PathBuf;
11	
12	use clap::Args;
13	use log::{debug, info};
14	
15	use crate::context::Context;
16	use crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;
17	use crate::todo_helpers::{self, Section, TODO_FILE};
18	
19	/// Clap-derived args for `validate-todo`.
20	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
21	pub struct ValidateTodoArgs {
22	    /// Todo markdown file to check
23	    #[arg(value_name = "FILE", default_value = TODO_FILE)]
24	    pub file: PathBuf,
25	}
26	
27	/// Inputs to the validate-todo op, flat, owned, clap-free.
28	///
29	/// Mirrors `ValidateTodoArgs`: the positional `FILE` path.
30	pub struct ValidateTodoParams {
31	    pub file: PathBuf,
32	}
33	
34	impl From<&ValidateTodoArgs> for ValidateTodoParams {
35	    /// Convert clap-derived `ValidateTodoArgs` into the flat
36	    /// `ValidateTodoParams` (total — the single field copies over).
37	    fn from(a: &ValidateTodoArgs) -> Self {
38	        Self {
39	            file: a.file.clone(),
40	        }
41	    }
42	}
43	
44	impl SubcommandRunner for ValidateTodoArgs {
45	    type Params = ValidateTodoParams;
46	
47	    /// Delegate to the `From<&ValidateTodoArgs>` impl (total).
48	    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String> {
49	        Ok(ValidateTodoParams::from(self))
50	    }
51	
52	    /// Run the `validate_todo` op.
53	    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
54	        validate_todo(ctx, params)
55	    }
56	}
57	
58	/// Truncate `line` to a single-line snippet for the report,
59	/// counting by `char` so a multi-byte character is never split.
60	fn snippet(line: &str) -> String {
61	    const MAX: usize = 56;
62	    let chars: Vec<char> = line.chars().collect();
63	    if chars.len() <= MAX {
64	        line.to_string()
65	    } else {
66	        let head: String = chars[..MAX].iter().collect();
67	        format!("{head}…")
68	    }
69	}
70	
71	/// Run the `validate-todo` subcommand: scan the todo file and
72	/// report each `## Todo` / `## Bugs` entry whose number or
73	/// continuation indent is off; errors if any are found.
74	///
75	/// `ctx` is unused — validate-todo reads a plain file and neither
76	/// the user config nor the `--log` path applies; it's present for
77	/// the uniform subcommand-layer signature.
78	pub fn validate_todo(
79	    _ctx: &Context,
80	    params: &ValidateTodoParams,
81	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
82	    debug!("validate-todo: enter");
83	    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&params.file)
84	        .map_err(|e| format!("cannot read {}: {e}", params.file.display()))?;
85	    let analysis = todo_helpers::analyze(&content);
86	
87	    info!("validate-todo: {}", params.file.display());
88	
89	    let mut last_section: Option<Section> = None;
90	    for c in &analysis.changes {
91	        if last_section != Some(c.section) {
92	            info!("");
93	            info!("{}", c.section.header());
94	            last_section = Some(c.section);
95	        }
96	        let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
97	        if c.num_old != c.num_new {
98	            parts.push(format!("number {} → {}", c.num_old, c.num_new));
99	        }
100	        if let (Some(o), Some(n)) = (c.indent_old, c.indent_new)
101	            && o != n
102	        {
103	            parts.push(format!("indent {o} → {n}"));
104	        }
105	        info!(
106	            "  line {}: {} — {}",
107	            c.line_no,
108	            parts.join(", "),
109	            snippet(&c.first_line)
110	        );
111	    }
112	
113	    info!("");
114	    let total = analysis.todo_count + analysis.bugs_count;
115	    let n = analysis.changes.len();
116	    if n == 0 {
117	        info!(
118	            "{total} {} checked ({} Todo, {} Bugs) — all sequential",
119	            todo_helpers::entry_word(total),
120	            analysis.todo_count,
121	            analysis.bugs_count
122	        );
123	        debug!("validate-todo: exit");
124	        Ok(())
125	    } else {
126	        info!(
127	            "{total} {} checked ({} Todo, {} Bugs) — {n} to fix",
128	            todo_helpers::entry_word(total),
129	            analysis.todo_count,
130	            analysis.bugs_count
131	        );
132	        debug!("validate-todo: exit with issues");
133	        Err(format!(
134	            "{n} todo {} to fix — run `vc-x1 fix-todo`",
135	            todo_helpers::entry_word(n)
136	        )
137	        .into())
138	    }
139	}
140	
141	#[cfg(test)]
142	mod tests {
143	    use super::*;
144	
145	    /// A line within the limit is returned unchanged.
146	    #[test]
147	    fn snippet_keeps_short_lines() {
148	        assert_eq!(snippet("1. short entry"), "1. short entry");
149	    }
150	
151	    /// A long line is cut on a `char` boundary and gets an
152	    /// ellipsis — 56 chars plus `…`.
153	    #[test]
154	    fn snippet_truncates_on_char_boundary() {
155	        let s = snippet(&"→".repeat(100));
156	        assert_eq!(s.chars().count(), 57);
157	        assert!(s.ends_with('…'));
158	    }
159	}
160

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "new_string": "/// Returns `entry` / `entries` — the count noun for `n`.\npub fn entry_word(n: usize) -> &'static str {\n    if n == 1 { \"entry\" } else { \"entries\" }\n}\n\n/// Build the `[…]` tag describing what changed for an entry —\n/// `[was N]`, `[indent A → B]`, or both. Used by `validate-todo`\n/// and `fix-todo` for the same per-entry report line.\npub fn change_tag(c: &Change) -> String {\n    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    if c.num_old != c.num_new {\n        parts.push(format!(\"was {}\", c.num_old));\n    }\n    if let (Some(o), Some(n)) = (c.indent_old, c.indent_new)\n        && o != n\n    {\n        parts.push(format!(\"indent {o} → {n}\"));\n    }\n    format!(\"[{}]\", parts.join(\", \"))\n}"
}

}

Used by `validate-todo`\n/// and `fix-todo` for the same per-entry report line.\npub fn change_tag(c: &Change) -> String {\n    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    if c.num_old != c.num_new {\n        parts.push(format!(\"was {}\", c.num_old));\n    }\n    if let (Some(o), Some(n)) = (c.indent_old, c.indent_new)\n        && o != n\n    {\n        parts.push(format!(\"indent {o} → {n}\"));\n    }\n    format!(\"[{}]\", parts.join(\", \"))\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Build the `[line: …]` tag for an entry — its line number,\n/// then what changed (`was N`, `indent A → B`, or both). Used by\n/// `validate-todo` and `fix-todo` for the same per-entry line.\npub fn change_tag(c: &Change) -> String {\n    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    if c.num_old != c.num_new {\n        parts.push(format!(\"was {}\", c.num_old));\n    }\n    if let (Some(o), Some(n)) = (c.indent_old, c.indent_new)\n        && o != n\n    {\n        parts.push(format!(\"indent {o} → {n}\"));\n    }\n    format!(\"[{}: {}]\", c.line_no, parts.join(\", \"))\n}"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

Could validate-todo "easily" be made to just invoke `fix-todo --dry-run` so save some lines of code?

> AGENT

Short answer: not cleanly — the "easily" instinct is right to be in quotes.

**The blocker is the exit code.** `fix-todo --dry-run` returns success (exit 0) — it's a preview that succeeded. `validate-todo` must exit **non-zero** when the file needs fixing — that's its whole reason to exist as a separate command (the CI gate: `vc-x1 validate-todo && …`). If `validate_todo()` just called `fix_todo()`, it'd always get `Ok(())` back and couldn't gate. You'd have to change `fix_todo`'s return to report "had changes" — at which point validate isn't "just invoking" it, and the *checker* now depends on the *rewriter* module, which is backwards (a read-only check shouldn't pull in the thing that writes files).

**And the duplication is already small.** The genuinely shared *logic* — `analyze`, `change_tag`, `entry_word` — is in `todo_helpers`. What's left duplicated between `validate_todo()` and `fix_todo()` is ~10 lines of thin orchestration: read the file, call `analyze`, and the 8-line per-change print loop. Each subcommand's own part (summary wording, exit code, the write) genuinely differs.

The only non-trivial duplicated bit is that print loop. It *could* move into a shared function — but the only sensible home is `todo_helpers`, and that would make the pure parsing core do `info!` output. `todo_helpers` today is clean: it parses and returns data; `change_tag`/`entry_word` return strings, they don't print. Putting `info!` in there to save 8 lines is a worse trade than the 8 lines.

This mirrors `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` — separate, self-contained subcommands that share `desc_helpers` for logic and don't call each other.

My recommendation: **leave it.** The shared logic is already factored; the remaining duplication is thin and the cleanest, and shrinking it would either make the checker depend on the rewriter or make `todo_helpers` impure. Not worth it for ~10 lines.

51	
52	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
53	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
54	references.
55	
56	Todo items use lazy numbering — every entry begins with `1. ` and the
57	markdown renderer auto-numbers them. Reorder or insert items without
58	renumbering, and reference an entry by its displayed number ("let's
59	work on #3"). The Done section keeps `-` bullets — items aren't
60	referenced by number once completed.
61	
62	Reference citations are **double-bracketed** — `[[1]]`, or
63	`[[2]],[[3]]` for several — so the brackets show when rendered
64	(a bare `[1]` renders as just `1`, which doesn't read as a
65	reference). Separate multiple with a comma: `[[2]],[[3]]`, not
66	`[2,3]` (a single ref key — won't resolve) or `[[2]][[3]]`. The
67	`[N]:` definition lines and inline `[text](url)` / `[text](#anchor)`
68	links stay single-bracketed (their brackets already show in
69	source, and the link text reads as a link when rendered).
70	
71	Examples:
72	
73	# Todo
74	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
75	- Fix bug Y [[1]]
76	
77	# Done
78	- Fixed issue Z [[2]],[[3]]
79	
80	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
81	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
82	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z

> DEVELOPER

You say intro paragraphs being with "exactly 1 leading space" but an experiment indicates it renders the same with 1 to 3 spaces?

> AGENT

Your experiment is correct. Two separate limits are in play:

- **The parser** (`parse_entry_prefix`) skips any line that doesn't start with a digit at column 0 — so **≥ 1 leading space** is all it takes to dodge being read as an entry.
- **The renderer** (CommonMark) strips up to 3 leading spaces before a paragraph — so **1, 2, or 3** spaces all render flush-left, identically. (4+ would turn the first line into an indented code block.)

So "exactly 1" is **not a functional requirement** — it's a *style choice*. And a convention's whole job is to pick one, so the raw source has a single uniform left edge. Your experiment lines are the proof of what relaxing it costs — `## Todo`'s intro is now ragged at 3/3/2/1 spaces; renders fine, reads messy in source.

My recommendation: **keep "exactly 1"**, but make the convention text honest about *why* it's 1 rather than stating it as if it were magic. Proposed rewrite of the `todo.md` intro:

```
Intro paragraphs in `## Todo` and `## Bugs` below begin every
line with exactly 1 leading space so they don't match the
`^\d+\. ` entry pattern `vc-x1 validate-todo` / `fix-todo` use
to find numbered entries. Any indent ≥ 1 dodges that pattern,
and CommonMark strips up to 3 leading spaces before a
paragraph — so 1 to 3 render identically; the convention
fixes it at 1, the minimum, to keep the raw source uniform.
```

And I'd normalize your experiment's ragged `## Todo` intro lines (32–35) back to 1 space each.

Two notes: the tooling deliberately leaves intro lines alone (they're not entries — `fix-todo` passes them through), so it won't catch raggedness; that stays a documented convention, not an enforced one. Go with the rewrite + normalize?

> DEVELOPER

I think a little simpler maybe: "Intro paragraphs in `## Todo` and `## Bugs` should begin with 1 leading space so as not to match the regx for locating numbered entries, but 2 or 3 also work.", thoughts?

> AGENT

Simpler is good. Two tweaks to your sentence:

- **"begin every line"** — keep "every line": a paragraph is multiple lines, and any line starting `\d. ` at column 0 (not just the first) would be misread as an entry.
- **"regx"** — typo, and since `fix-todo.py` is gone the matcher is now hand-rolled Rust, not an actual regex. Better to name the pattern directly.

Proposed:

```
Intro paragraphs in `## Todo` and `## Bugs` should begin every
line with 1 leading space so they don't match the `^\d+\. `
pattern that locates numbered entries; 2 or 3 spaces also work.
```

One consequence to be aware of: this wording **relaxes** the old "exactly 1" — it now says 1 is preferred but 2–3 are fine. So your experiment's ragged `## Todo` intro (lines 32–35, at 3/3/2/1) technically conforms now. I'd still tidy those back to 1 space so the section reads uniform in source — but with this wording it's optional, your call.

Go with this sentence? And tidy the experiment lines, or leave them?

4	and reference links to more details.
5	
6	Intro paragraphs in `## Todo` and `## Bugs` below begin every
7	line with exactly 1 leading space so they don't accidentally
8	match the `^\d+\. ` entry pattern that `vc-x1 validate-todo` /
9	`vc-x1 fix-todo` use to find numbered entries. Minimum is the
10	principled choice — the convention's job is to dodge the
11	column-0 anchor, not to add visual emphasis.
12	
13	## In Progress
14	
15	A bulleted list of the in-progress task's development "ladder":
16	   - 0.xx.y-0 blah (done)
17	   - 0.xx.y-1 blah blah (current)
18	   - 0.xx.y-2 blah blah blah
19	   - 0.xx.y close-out and validation

29	
30	## Todo
31	
32	A markdown list of tasks to do in the near future, ordered
33	   highest-priority first. Keep entries brief — 1-3 lines.
34	  Detailed motivation, safety requirements, and ordering belong
35	 in `notes/chores/chores-NN.md` design subsections; link via `[N]` ref.
36	
37	 Entries are manually numbered (`1.` `2.` …) so the index is
38	 readable in the raw markdown source. Reorder/insert renumbers the
39	 entries below — accept the diff churn in exchange for being
40	 able to count without rendering. Reference by entry number
41	 ("let's work on #3"). Continuation lines under a two-digit
42	 entry indent 4 spaces (under `NN. `) rather than 3.
43

1	# vc-x1
2	
3	- [Overview](#vc-x1)
4	- [Architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md)
5	- [Usage](#usage)
6	  - [Revision shortcuts](#revision-shortcuts)
7	  - [Shell completion](#shell-completion)
8	  - [validate-desc](#validate-desc)
9	  - [fix-desc](#fix-desc)
10	  - [clone](#clone)
11	  - [init](#init)
12	  - [symlink](#symlink)
13	  - [finalize](#finalize)
14	  - [Testing push + finalize](#testing-push--finalize)
15	- [Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers](#cross-repo-linking-with-git-trailers)
16	- [jj Tips for Git Users](#jj-tips-for-git-users)
17	- [Thoughts for the future](#thoughts-for-the-future)
18	- [Contributing](#contributing)
19	- [License](#license)
20	
21	This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
22	We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
23	in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
24	initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
25	made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
26	and one with the conversation with the bot.
27	
28	I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
29	each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
30	of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
31	changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
32	between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
33	evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
34	most importantly **why** the code evolved.
35	
36	The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
37	(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
38	existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
39	own IDE for vc.
40	
41	See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
42	for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
43	doing so and I then created this README.md file.
44	
45	## Usage
46	
47	```
48	vc-x1 list [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # List commits in a jj repo
49	vc-x1 desc [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show full description of a commit
50	vc-x1 chid [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Print changeID(s) for a revision
51	vc-x1 show [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show commit details and diff summary
52	vc-x1 validate-desc [OPTS]                 # Validate commit descriptions
53	vc-x1 fix-desc [OPTS]                     # Fix commit descriptions (dry-run default)
54	vc-x1 clone <REPO> [NAME] [OPTS]          # Clone a dual-repo project
55	vc-x1 init <NAME> [OPTS]                  # Create a new dual-repo project
56	vc-x1 symlink [TARGET] [OPTS]             # Create Claude Code project symlink
57	vc-x1 sync [OPTS]                          # Fetch + sync both repos to their remotes
58	vc-x1 finalize --bookmark <B> [OPTS]       # Squash working copy into target
59	vc-x1 --version                            # Print version
60	vc-x1 --help                           # Print help
61	```
62	
```

```
242	### fix-desc
243	
244	Fix commit descriptions against the other repo. Default is dry-run —
245	use `--no-dry-run` to write changes. Reads other repo from
246	`.vc-config.toml` by default.
247	
248	```
249	# Dry-run: show what would be fixed
250	vc-x1 fix-desc @..
251	
252	# Actually fix
253	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --no-dry-run
254	
255	# Add missing ochid trailers by matching title
256	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --add-missing
257	
258	# Limit fixes
259	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --no-dry-run -m 3
260	
261	# Use a fallback for IDs not found in other repo
262	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --fallback /.claude/lost
263	```
264	
265	| Flag | Description |
266	|------|-------------|
267	| `--other-repo <PATH>` | Override other repo from .vc-config.toml |
268	| `--no-dry-run` | Write fixes [default: dry-run] |
269	| `--add-missing` | Infer and add ochid for commits without one |
270	| `-m, --max-fixes <N>` | Stop fixing after N commits changed [default: all] |
271	| `--fallback <VALUE>` | Replacement for IDs not found in other repo |
272	| `--id-len <N>` | Expected changeID length [default: 12] |
273	| `--title <TEXT>` | Replace commit title at the same time |
274	
275	Use `--help` for the full status label legend.
276	
277	### clone
278	
279	Clone an existing dual-repo project. Runs `git clone --recursive` to
280	get both repos, initializes `jj` in each, and creates the Claude Code
281	symlink.
282	
283	```
```

165	## args → Context + Params
166	
167	"The Context+Params port" — each subcommand's
168	`pub fn x(args: &XxxArgs)` is structured as
169	`pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams)`, with an
170	`XxxParams` flat struct + a `From` (or `TryFrom`, if the
171	conversion is fallible) at the binary edge. `init` (0.44.0)
172	is the worked example; complete across all 12 current
173	subcommands as of 0.49.0, and the shape new subcommands
174	follow. Out of scope until a real consumer surfaces: typed
175	errors, returned-outcomes-vs-`println!`, `ProgressSink`,
176	`Context` fields beyond `UserConfig` + `--log`. Per-subcommand
177	status: `notes/chores/chores-*.md` (the "Refactor tracking" section).
178	
179	## per-subcommand flags → `src/options_flags/`
```

  "new_string": "Docs (README / ARCHITECTURE) for both subcommands land\ntogether in the 0.55.0-2 sweep.\n\n## feat: add fix-todo subcommand (0.55.0-2)\n\nSecond step: `fix-todo`, the rewrite half of the pair, plus\nthe cycle's docs sweep. `fix-todo` renumbers `## Todo` /\n`## Bugs` to `1..N` and normalizes continuation indent —\ndry-run by default (printing each changed entry's corrected\nline), `--no-dry-run` writes.\n\n- `todo_helpers::analyze` grew to also return `fixed`, the\n  fully renumbered / re-indented file content, so `fix-todo`\n  writes one assembled result; it round-trips an\n  already-correct file byte-for-byte.\n- `validate-todo` and `fix-todo` print an identical\n  per-entry line, `<corrected line>  [line: …]`. The tag\n  builder `change_tag` moved into `todo_helpers` as a shared\n  helper; this also de-noised `validate-todo`, which had\n  spelled out `number X → Y` against a truncated original.\n- `notes/fix-todo.py` deleted — the Rust pair replaces it.\n  Docs sweep: root `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` module\n  map, `notes/README.md`'s stale lazy-numbering text, and\n  the `notes/todo.md` intro.\n\n### Column-0 lines\n\n`fix-todo` measures an entry's continuation base indent as\nthe minimum non-blank continuation indent. A line at column\n0 — neither an entry (`N. `) nor a heading, so malformed —\nwould make that minimum 0 and shift the whole block by the\nprefix width. A list continuation must be indented, so\ncolumn-0 lines are dropped from the measurement and passed\nthrough untouched. Flagging them as malformed input is a\nseparate follow-up captured in `## Todo`.\n\n# References"
}
```
